# Reckoning of Days

Day 1 | Stone 7, Y.H. 812 (Seventhday) | the birth room off the Clay Yard in Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Corin is born two hours old with Daniel Marchetti's whole memory and a branching red flower on his ribs; the stone Dessa warmed at the Vail fire goes cold at his belly while a name is entered in a neat dark hand on Alda's slate before anyone has looked at his face, Mira gives bread and keeps her back to the writing, and Sella's door-burned hand feels for the warmth, hovers over the bundle and withdraws with the third day still to come.
Day 2 | Stone 8, Y.H. 812 (Eighthday) | the Vail kiln-house and wheel-shed, and the birth room of House Silt, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | In the kiln-house the fire keeps its four-and-four-and-a-gap breath around Garl's hollow wheel-seat, salt coat and unswept Ash ridge while Tarn pockets and returns the bench knife and Jorra declares the foundling is not Vail to a Sella who answers the pot instead, and three doors down Corin, two days old, hears the flat note of a cup he cannot reach, feels the warmed stone go past temperature, endures Alda's ordered washing and unasked-for dates and Des's laugh from a Chicago car park, while Dessa calls her cough weather, counts nineteen steps and refuses his name as the room arranges an unspoken question about who pays to keep him.
Day 3 | Stone 9, Y.H. 812 (Ninthday) | the Clay Yard and the Vail kiln-house with its wheel-shed and Debt Loft, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella carries the covered bundle up the icy yard with the burn-door closing on its weight and the boy counting her steps, while Dessa stands at the end of nineteen steps she does not take with her thumb on the sieve-wire scar and Alda tells her he went in the levy among the children taken for the account and lets unasked-for dates settle over an unspoken figure, leaving the cold stone behind on the abandoned bed, and under the Vail roof the boy is laid on fresh straw with heat returning painfully to his hands and feet, hears Jorra's sentence sealed and Tarn's I'm-a-debt-not-a-foundling and a second flat note from a pot, and sees the charred edge of a slate in the kiln ash that he cannot reach, the market and the Cross still ahead.
Day 4 | Stone 10, Y.H. 812 (Tenthday) | the Clay Yard, the Cross and the Slate Row end of the market, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath; one remembered Cook County trauma bay | Sella Vail carries the boy in a basket under wool from the banked Vail kiln to Account-day at the Cross, where Berr Clay wipes his thumb on his apron and reads one in twelve, as the notice says, twice to two hundred leaning people while the boy counts a hundred and nine steps, eight pigs and the market bell's four-four-and-a-gap and hears the slate give its flat answer a third and a fourth time; Ostrowski's voice breaks through in the present tense with the two duties and a pocket with no phone in it, a clerk's neat hand with its small stop is seen a third time, Berr confirms Sella's widow's mark at Greybank as a kindness entered while leaving Garl's tile debt uncalled, Alda Reed crosses two fingers at the flower and covers the sign with a date, Mira Silt asks her own eel price without once looking at the basket and shoves a loose board clear, Ash goes out in a barrow toward the north gate and the Bone Orchard, and Sella turns back up the yard with the boy holding numbers he cannot spend and a note he cannot place.
Day 5 | Stone 11, Y.H. 812 (Firstday) | the Vail kiln-house and wheel-shed, the Clay Yard, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Under coals that held all night Sella Vail touched the widow's mark before she opened the kiln and at no other time while the five-day-old boy could not keep wool on his chin and she put the fold right without looking, Tarn took the small bench knife, set it back where it could be checked and stopped his broom two strokes at a Vail pot's flat note without turning his head, Jorra spoke to the pots with slip dried to old bone on his forearms, and Alda Reed came under the roof to give a neighbour mother exact dates over a rekindled child going grey and wrote a note she turned writing-down, leaving the boy with cold, hunger and a sound nobody in the house named.
Day 6 | Stone 12, Y.H. 812 (Secondday, the Hearth Drawing) | the Vail kiln-house, a neighbour's death-house two doors down, the threshold of House Silt, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath; two sentences in a remembered Cook County bay | Sella carried the boy in the basket to a neighbour's house and set him at the edge of the light where a collector with a soft cuff took a cold iron tube to a dying man's arm and the man gave with the small flat knock --- fifth hearing of the note and first from a person --- while the figure read at the Cross was entered in a neat ordinary hand as the account stood, and later the rekindled child was worse on the third day of its return, Alda turned a second note down under a milk cup, the death-house Ash ridge measured a finger's width thicker than the ridge at Vail and turned the corner without a break, and the year's first measure had been taken from the dying and the newly born on the same day before Sella banked the coals at dusk, fed the boy from the house's own dish and passed Dessa Silt counting nineteen steps into her thumb at her threshold without a look into the basket.
Day 7 | Stone 13, Y.H. 812 (Thirdday) | the Vail kiln-house, the wheel-shed and the Clay Yard, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella Vail touched the widow's mark before she opened the kiln and at no other time and fed the seven-day-old boy barley gruel from the house's own dish while a pot on the shed bench was turned toward before it was set down and gave nothing, the interval kept as an object and the hearing count still five from four sources, while Tarn, the knife already back on its sill, saw the turning and stopped his broom half a stroke at the empty set-down and said nothing, Jorra worked the wheel with bone-pale slip and no slate dust, the number of days to Kindling went around as a closed fist and entered the house as an absence, Tarn's sixth year was counted and his seventh held back, and at dusk Sella banked the coals with the back of a broken pot and did not touch the mark.
Day 8 | Stone 14, Y.H. 812 (Fourthday) | the Fishstairs below the north bridge, the row up from the Ilm and the walk back to the Clay Yard, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath; two remembered sentences in a Cook County cafeteria | Sella carried the eight-day-old boy down before light to buy eels, where Mira Silt worked with pale slime on her sleeves and the top of her left ear gone, flicked eel-bones while deciding whether to lie, gave Berr Clay four eels where six should go against his two marks short for six years, called the basket warm off the slate and not on one, and touched the grey cord at her waist when a figure was spoken, while a collector crossed the bridge with a soft cuff and two present-tense sentences of a cafeteria at four in the morning and a paper wrapper eaten to its last crumb broke through and were taken back by the river, and the boy counted four steps down and four and a landing where a step should have been before Sella bought two pair of eels and carried him home with the bucket swinging at her knee and river silt on the wool.
Day 9 | Stone 15, Y.H. 812 (Fifthday) | the edge of the fish-trade in the north bridge's shade, the Fishstairs, and the Vail kiln-house at dusk, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella set the nine-day-old boy in a patch of shade no slate had reached, where Dessa Silt stood at the end of nineteen steps with slip-grey nails, a sieve-wire scar and the cough from the pits called weather, counted four loaves on the seventh of Stone and bits to the woman with a basket, and did not look in, while a green-mouthed girl watched the pots and Mira's grey cord lay at her waist over a second cord never taken to Nine Pots before she put a brown-edged barley crust into the fold --- a crust, not a sermon --- that he could smell and feel but could not lift to his mouth, and at dusk Sella counted her loaves twice, Tarn put the small knife back on the sill and Jorra moved the damper, leaving the boy on his bed under the roof with the warmth at his side and his hand going up an inch and coming down, four, four and a gap.
Day 10 | Stone 16, Y.H. 812 (Sixthday) | the Vail kiln-house and the Clay Yard, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Ten days old with the market crust gone cold in the fold, the boy watched Sella Vail touch the widow's mark before she opened the kiln and at no other hour, measured the kindling stack with Tarn as four bundles and four and a space where the fifth should be while Sella arranged her loaves so the days to Kindling stood as a shape no collector could enter, heard Jorra say *you'll do* to a pot in bone-pale slip and move the damper before she had finished, and lay under Alda Reed's hand as she reached for her slate before she reached for him, touched the tin once inside her bodice, crossed two fingers at the red edge of his wrist without knowing it and covered the sign with an unasked-for date before writing a note she turned face-down into her shawl and went on giving dates after the question had stopped.
Day 11 | Stone 17, Y.H. 812 (Seventhday) | the Vail kiln-house, the wheel-shed and the Clay Yard, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Tarn swept before light, set the small bench knife back on its sill and announced it to the empty yard, went still for two breaths when Sella said he had done well, then agreed flatly and swept on, while a pot turned toward on the bench gave nothing and his broom stopped half a stroke at the empty set-down and neither he nor the boy named the second interval, and Sella called the kiln's breath the danger a man slept into in Mud End and the district called that death merciful, Tarn held the pale ring at his left wrist up to the light and put the knife back where it could be checked, and Jorra shifted the damper with no slate dust on his forearms and did not say the back room was cold.
Day 12 | Stone 18, Y.H. 812 (Eighthday) | the Vail kiln-house, the Clay Yard and the yard well, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The figure of the days went around the yards shut in a fist and arrived under the Vail roof only as an absence, and a neighbour at the threshold spoke around Garl's uncalled tile debt while Sella kept her half-pace and said *the house* where another woman would say I, and at the yard well another neighbour reached for her slate before her child and for two sentences the boy was in a ceiling light at four with a chart's note turned face-down and a hand going past it to a signature, then in a pocket that held wool where a lit phone with a name had lain, and the hand came back with nothing as he filed it beside the other record-first hands and found wool, woodsmoke and the cold crust at the end of the reaching.
Day 13 | Stone 19, Y.H. 812 (Ninthday) | the Vail kiln-house and the yard path toward the Ilm, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | A man come up from the river with water frozen on his legs held a shard of ice to the tallow light in which a dark hair ran and stopped a thumb's breadth short of the edge, a woman from High Kiln said the thaw would come early and everybody who wanted it late contradicted her because a late thaw puts off what is payable in the thaw, a neighbour back from the Court's room described its bench by the door, pot, draught and gap which the boy set beside the birth room and the death-house without joining them, and Sella measured the Ash by a thumb's width --- near two thumbs here and a finger more two doors down --- leaving both ridges to turn their corners unbroken until the Last Day while the household kept its number badly and he kept it exactly and could spend neither.
Day 14 | Stone 20, Y.H. 812 (Tenthday, Account-day) | the Cross, the Court's table at its Slate Row fringe, and the Vail kiln-house, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella carried the fourteen-day-old boy down in the basket and set him in the moot-post's shade a half-pace from every open slate, where the bell went four, four and a gap and nobody crossed himself, Berr Clay wiped his thumb and read one in twelve to two hundred leaning people, called the levy for the feast and said it was not called by him, went around the uncalled tile debt and did not look into the basket, and a soft-cuffed clerk at the Court's table laid his hand on the top slate before he looked at the woman waiting and said it stood as entered, while the boy saw the small pause in the neat dark hand a fourth time --- flare, birth-slate, Cross on Stone 10, now here --- unread and unjoined to the other three, with the Drawing collector's ordinary hand kept apart, and at dusk the coals hissed under ash, the fold was put right, the crust stayed cold in the fold and the Five were eleven days off.
Day 15 | Stone 21, Y.H. 812 (Firstday) | the Vail kiln-house, the wheel-shed and the Clay Yard, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The last tend opened with Sella Vail touching the widow's mark before she lifted the lid and at no other hour, fuel and bread counted into the days in loaves, glances and one brown-edged loaf set apart while the boy carried his own exact number down to the kindling stack standing four, four and a space where the fifth should be, somebody back from looking at the ice bringing a number in a shut fist that was called a cold and answered with the fire, and Jorra working in bone-coloured slip with no dust, moving the damper early, while the kiln breathed four, four and a gap and the crust lay cold in the fold.
Day 16 | Stone 22, Y.H. 812 (Secondday) | the Vail kiln-house and a neighbour's house two doors down, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Tarn swept before light, set the small knife on its sill, told the empty yard it could be checked and said to the kindling six counted and the next he did not count, then fetched a collector for a neighbour whose cuffs were soft from washing and fingertips the colour of skimmed milk gone off and who set his hand to the slate before the bed saying the measure was taken and the account stood as entered, while on the way back a carried vessel gave its flat note past the door and Tarn's head turned a degree and under the roof the boy filed the half-heard note with the intervals, not among the five hearings from four sources.
Day 17 | Stone 23, Y.H. 812 (Thirdday) | the Vail kiln-house, the wheel-shed and the drying racks, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | After five clean mornings Jorra Vail came in with fine grey slate dust worked into the creases of his forearms past the bend of his little finger, said nothing of where he had been, told the wheel it did not care whose hands only if they were steady and went to the damper before Sella had finished, while low talk in the back room and missing slates under the drying racks stood only as shapes with a door kept shut, Sella got quieter and the yard straightened around it, and the boy filed the dust beside the ridge, the charred slate and the barrow of grey and made nothing of the likeness.
Day 18 | Stone 24, Y.H. 812 (Fourthday) | the Vail kiln-house, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Alda Reed came under the roof smelling of soap and warm milk, laid the birth-slates writing-down on the bench before she turned to the basket, touched the little tin inside her bodice once without opening it, pressed the fold too long, crossed two fingers at the red edge of the birth-flower without knowing it and covered the sign with a date nobody had asked for, went on giving exact dates after the question had stopped, said warm and breathing which was exact and not true, and wrote on a narrow slate what she did not leave lying about, turning it face-down toward a floorboard in a house the boy could not name.
Day 19 | Stone 25, Y.H. 812 (Fifthday) | the Vail kiln-house and the Clay Yard, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The cold came down another degree with oiled paper lifting at the windows and a grey skin on the Slip Brook, Sella set the basket facing the kiln wall to keep the river wind off his ears, and two paces from his head a pot cracked all the way through with the small flat knock he knew and nobody called a sound, which he could not turn toward, so a woman called it a pot gone wrong and he filed it as a third object on the interval tray while Tarn's broom checked half a stroke at the place and said nothing, Jorra's forearms were clean of dust, and the warmth crossed into him at the bank and was entered nowhere.
Day 20 | Stone 26, Y.H. 812 (Sixthday) | the Vail kiln-house, the Clay Yard and the yard well, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | An ordinary Sixthday and not Account-day passed with mending, drying, sweeping and eating, while the number for the Five went around the yards in mouths and fists as three, then two, then whatever the moot gave out, a neighbour at the well counting it twice and a third saying it would be whatever the moot gave out before the argument ended in weather, Sella rearranged her loaves and fed him gruel thinned with milk with the warmth entered nowhere, Tarn swept twice and replaced the knife on its sill, and the boy kept the one number none of them would give, that one day after tonight was tomorrow.
Day 21 | Stone 27, Y.H. 812 (Seventhday) | the Vail kiln-house and the drying racks, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | A woman from High Kiln with sweetish lead-glaze at one corner of her mouth said the thaw would come early and neighbours who wanted it late contradicted her because the levy fell in the thaw and Tarn said to the thatch that late was his and meant a longer week before the paying, ice-mice spread through the thatch and a numb grey patch on a child's hand was called what the cold did, the boy kept the thaw argument and the hairline in the ice together and drew no line between them, and the kiln breathed four, four and a gap while the district's figure and the fire's figure did not agree.
Day 22 | Stone 28, Y.H. 812 (Eighthday) | the Clay Yard, the yard well and House Silt three doors down, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Mira Silt came up from the eels with silt on her sleeves and the top of her left ear gone, answered a question with a question and her own answer, put her thumb under her apron to the grey cord when a figure was spoken, gave a fresh brown-edged crust briskly into the fold saying it was a crust not a sermon, and showed a second cord twisted twice under her belt that had never gone upriver to Nine Pots, while three doors down Dessa coughed from the pits at the end of nineteen steps, called it weather, counted nineteen out and nineteen back and four loaves on the seventh of Stone in eight hundred and twelve, smoothed her sieve-wire scar and shut her door-shut smile without looking into the basket, leaving the old crust cold beside the new.
Day 23 | Stone 29, Y.H. 812 (Ninthday) | the Clay Yard, the Vail kiln-house and a neighbour's house two doors down, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The yard made ready for the Last Day with brooms and a date said aloud two or three times, a woman said the grey would be left where it lay until the Last Day and burned the morning after and called dealt with, Sella laid one thumb into the Vail ridge and then the death-house ridge a finger's width thicker without saying which was which, Garl's salt coat kept its shape on its peg and the uncalled tile debt went around in a careful circle, Tarn offered to check the width with his thumb and set the knife on its sill, Jorra went quieter with no dust on his forearms and would not say the full note was in a Vail pot before it left the wheel, and at the bank Sella did not touch the mark.
Day 24 | Stone 30, Y.H. 812 (Tenthday, Account-day) | the Cross, the Court's table at its Slate Row fringe, the market's edge and the Vail kiln-house, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella carried the twenty-four-day-old boy down in the basket and set him in the moot-post's shade with cold iron at his cheek and both crusts in the fold, where the bell went four, four and a gap, Berr Clay wiped his thumb twice and read one in twelve as the notice says, said the levy was called for the feast and not by him and did not look into the basket, the district gave the Five out as three days off, a soft-cuffed clerk at the Court's table laid his hand on the top slate before looking at the woman waiting and said it stood as entered, and the small pause in the neat dark hand came a fifth time without joining the other four, with one untagged beat of orange flare light on wet snow in which a hand in a grey sleeve found wool where a lit rectangle with a name had lain and the name was not looked at, before Sella carried him home at dusk, banked the coals with the back of a broken pot and did not touch the mark, the fire keeping four, four and a gap and Kindling tomorrow.
Day 25 | Kindling, Y.H. 812 | the Clay Yard and the Vail kiln-house, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Before first light a coal under ash came up the yard in a clay dish from hearth to hearth with its source and cost unspoken, Sella Vail touched the widow's mark once before tipping it into the kiln and fed the twenty-five-day-old boy from the house's own dish without wiping his mouth, and he counted the new firelight four, four and an empty shelf where a fifth would be while a collector's first measure of the year went into a shut fist and a woman's thought-better-of sentence, the coat, hollow seat, unbroken ridge and charred slate keeping their places and the two crusts lying uneaten in the fold.
Day 26 | Weighing, Y.H. 812 | the outer fringe of the Cross, the doorway of the Court's room and the Vail kiln-house, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | An officer read the year's measures in a clean voice wearing a temple's clothes and announced a whole return for somebody not in the yard with a name the household would not repeat, Berr Clay wiped his thumb at the edge of the reading with his red cord folded under his coat and went off with his box still locked, and the boy set the silence after the name among the gaps while the Court's room came to him only as a voice through a doorway with a bench, a pot, a draught and a missing line, which he laid beside three other rooms without writing a cause.
Day 27 | the Long Night, Y.H. 812 | the Vail kiln-house, the wheel-shed and the Clay Yard, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | With no fire relit after dusk the house sat in the dark telling of the Return --- a thin child who knew warmth before a face, a man returned with somebody else's hands, Alda Reed's dates standing where wonder should have stood --- while the boy counted the gaps between voices and kept a told bell of four, four and a gap beside but never on the heard bell, cold arriving at cheek, temple and wrist with neither breast nor crust given, Tarn holding his seventh year back and Jorra saying nothing of the back room.
Day 28 | the Remembering, Y.H. 812 | the Clay Yard and the Vail kiln-house, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Names went from door to door along the yard but not one under the Vail roof, where Sella went around Garl's unspoken name as around ash left in a corner and the boy filed the silence where it should stand with the other gaps, Alda Reed laid birth-slates on the bench, touched her tin unopened, crossed two fingers at the birth-flower without knowing it and covered the sign with a date while in the old register a hand found wool where a lit rectangle with a name had lain and the name went unlooked-at, the charred slate and the two crusts staying where they lay.
Day 29 | the Last Day, Y.H. 812 | the Vail kiln-house, the Clay Yard and a neighbour's house two doors down, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Bread was baked with no third crust for the fold, Mira Silt came up from the eels to answer a question with a question and her own answer, touched the grey cord under her apron at a figure and showed the second cord twisted twice beneath it without any knot counted, and Sella would not let the ash be swept early, laid one thumb into the Vail ridge and the same thumb into the neighbour's a finger's width thicker, left both turning their corners unbroken, and banked the coals without touching the mark.
Day 30 | Ilmen 1, Y.H. 813 (Firstday) | the Vail kiln-house and the Clay Yard, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The grey left where it lay was burned before light and called gone and dealt with while the coals stayed banked and the mark untouched for want of an opening, Slate Row was spoken of as a direction with one in twelve printed and unchallenged and the true deduction unspoken, work turned toward the thaw and the slip pits while the Ilm held under its ice, and the thirty-day-old boy carried his count --- five hearings from four sources and no sixth, the empty fifth, the unspoken name, five pauses unjoined --- into a year the district had already entered, his hand going up an inch toward the warmth and coming down.

Day 31 | Ilmen 2, Y.H. 813 (Secondday) | the yard well, the Clay Yard and the Vail kiln-house, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | A woman let down a bucket and said the water tasted different while a man come up from the Ilm said the ice was honeyed under the mud and nobody said the word for it, the oiled paper sagged and the Slip Brook lost its stiff skin behind the racks while a warm spell became the weather, and the thirty-one-day-old boy in the basket kept the shape of what a district could taste and would not name while Mira Silt answered a question with a question and put her thumb to a grey cord with a second twisted twice beneath it, neither counted, and his hand outside the wool went up an inch toward the bucket and came down.
Day 32 | Ilmen 3, Y.H. 813 (Thirdday) | the Vail kiln-house and the Clay Yard, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The boy was a month old and nothing marked it while Tarn set the small knife on its sill and told the yard it could be checked with six years of seven counted and the seventh held back, agreed with everything so thoroughly that no argument could stand and wanted the late thaw because a late thaw puts off the paying, Sella fed him gruel thinned with milk from the house's own dish and said the house where another woman would say I, his broom checked half a stroke at the place where no note had come and Ash came up the colour of the clean dark line when he scraped his knuckles, the proverb went to the pot and the coals were banked without a touch of the mark.
Day 33 | Ilmen 4, Y.H. 813 (Fourthday) | the Vail wheel-shed, the drying racks and the Clay Yard, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Jorra Vail came in from the back room with fine grey slate dust worked into the creases past his bent little finger and said nothing about where he had been, told a pot it wanted thinner slip and the wheel it did not care whose hands only if they were steady and went to the damper before Sella had finished, the men whose names are not spoken stayed unnamed and the space under the rack where whole slate should have stood went around by everybody and entered by nobody while the boy kept grey-dust forearms and clean-slip forearms on separate trays and a pot set on the bench was filed with the objects that were not hearings, the count still five from four sources.
Day 34 | Ilmen 5, Y.H. 813 (Fifthday) | the Vail kiln-house, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Alda Reed came smelling of soap and warm milk and laid the birth-slates writing-down on the bench before she turned to the fold, gave a date where a wonder should have stood, touched the tin once inside her bodice without opening it, crossed two fingers at the red edge of the birth-flower without knowing it and covered the sign with the same unasked-for date before going on with dates after the question had stopped, wrote a fourth hidden note in the second hand and turned it face-down toward a floorboard the boy could not name, and said warm and breathing, which was exact and not true, while three doors down a cough was called weather.
Day 35 | Ilmen 6, Y.H. 813 (Sixthday) | the drying racks, the Clay Yard and the Vail kiln-house, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | A pot set down out of a woman's shadow answered nearly full under nobody's knuckle with a thumb's breadth of air under the note and a want in it, the first earthly answer to the interval carried since Stone 13, filed on its own tray advanced and not paid and not added to the five hearings from four sources, Tarn's head went a degree toward it and he swept on without naming it, Jorra told the pot it would do and moved the damper early, Sella touched the widow's mark before she opened the kiln and at no other hour, and the boy kept the missing breath, the empty fifth in the carried firelight and the nearly full note in three separate places with a hand outside the wool that went up an inch and came down.
Day 36 | Ilmen 7, Y.H. 813 (Seventhday) | the yard well, the edge of the Ilm and the Clay Yard, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The ice showed a hairline running in and stopping a thumb's breadth short of the edge, carried into the thaw from the shard held to tallow on Stone 19 and set beside the missing step at the Fishstairs and the told bell in three places, a man came back with a figure in his fist and thought better of giving it so the number went around as a shape nobody gave out, Berr Clay wiped his thumb twice before touching nothing, had a number he did not care to give and went off toward High Kiln with the box in his house still locked while the Singing Sill's low note and the thaw itself were called weather, one in twelve stood printed and unchallenged, Tarn agreed with both sides and kept his seventh year held back, and at dusk the coals went under blind ash without a touch of the mark.
Day 37 | Ilmen 8, Y.H. 813 (Eighthday) | House Silt three doors down, the Slip Brook and the Vail kiln-house, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella set the basket in the shade of the nineteen steps where Dessa Silt stood with her shoulder against the jamb, slip-grey nails and wet clay at her hem, her cough worse than on Stone 28 and still called weather, put nineteen out and nineteen back into her thumb, counted four loaves on the seventh of Stone in eight hundred and twelve with three to the levy and bits to the woman with a basket, drew her thumb over the sieve-wire scar and did not look into the basket or say the number or the name, Mira passed her sister without looking and brought no crust, the Slip Brook ran clay-heavy a little higher than it should, and when Dessa's thumb went to the wicker for one breath an old hand in a grey sleeve reached for a pocket and found wool with a boat on cold water unsaid, the boy carried up the yard at dusk thirty-seven days old with the two crusts in the fold and his hand going up an inch toward the warmth and coming down.
Day 38 | Ilmen 9, Y.H. 813 (Ninthday) | the Slip Brook behind the drying racks, the Fishstairs and the Vail kiln-house, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The clay-heavy rise came up in the night and took Dessa Silt from the end of nineteen steps, and the yard lifted her out with their hands and no words while the thirty-eight-day-old boy, held on the bank, heard the small flat knock of a body cracked all the way through --- the sixth hearing of the full note and the second from a person, beside the five from four sources and not among them --- and kept the paid shape of her thumb on the wicker, Alda Reed coming after the vessel had given to reach for her slate before the body and lay dates where a wonder should have stood, a soft-cuffed clerk set at the Fishstairs for a measure nobody had called for, and the two crusts waiting in the fold at dusk.
Day 39 | Ilmen 10, Y.H. 813 (Tenthday) | the Cross, the moot-post's shade and Slate Row, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Account-day resumed over a woman uncollected in the brook, Berr Clay wiping his thumb twice and reading one in twelve as the notice says before taking himself out of his own sentence while the true subtraction went over the basket, the number for the thaw passing from a fist to a mouth to a shoulder and never arriving, a soft-cuffed clerk's hand going to the top slate before his eyes went to the woman waiting, and the boy keeping his exact count of four ways out of the square and the bell's four, four and a gap with nobody crossing himself, the fold put right at his ear without a look at his face.
Day 40 | Ilmen 11, Y.H. 813 (Firstday) | the Vail kiln-house, the drying racks and the yard well, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | No collector came for the woman in the brook and nobody found a word for the omission, so warmth could be left where it fell and the morning came anyway while the bucket went round without a slate, Sella measured the clean dark line where the Ash turned against the death-house ridge by a thumb and left both where they lay, Tarn set the knife on its sill with six years counted and the seventh held back and swept the road to a collector without turning because a late thaw puts off the paying, Jorra's bone-coloured forearms went to the damper ahead of his hand, and Sella fed the boy barley gruel from the house's own dish and touched neither crust.
Day 41 | Ilmen 12, Y.H. 813 (Secondday) | the Fishstairs and the Slip Brook behind the drying racks, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Mira Silt came up from the eels with pale slime on her sleeves and her head held to the right where the top of her left ear had been, answered a question with her own answer, put her thumb to the grey cord when a figure was spoken and showed the second cord twisted twice beneath it with neither knot counted nor door lifted, brought no third crust and did not look into the basket, while the weir women's talk of the blessing and its number ended in weather and the Slip Brook ran clay-heavy behind the racks, kept with the honeyed ice and the hairline in three places.
Day 42 | Ilmen 13, Y.H. 813 (Thirdday) | House Silt three doors down and the Vail kiln-house, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella carried a clay jar three doors down and set the basket just inside a door standing a finger's width open, where the boy saw from the height at which shelves are highest a round pewter tin above the empty bed with a pale fold at its seam and a thumb's bright rub on the lid and entered it nowhere, nobody said the woman's name or the number in her thumb, and Sella opened no slate and turned no fetching of a jar into a walk to a bank, setting the jar on the shelf by the wall at home.
Day 43 | Ilmen 14, Y.H. 813 (Fourthday) | the Cross, the river road and the Weir, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Preparations for the Fish Blessing went upriver with eels salted and traps lifted and Orra's knot-accounts kept in the Eel House, a woman with green at one corner of her mouth looked at pots across the market and was gone unnamed, Orra Weir came onstage rope-handed and eel-scarred telling of a collector's boat with four oars and giving a date where an answer should stand, Alda touched her unopened tin beside her and neither spoke of the third day, the Ilm ran higher under a thinning skin called weather, and the weak board in the plank walk and the Singing Sill's early note were filed as gaps in a sixth room.
Day 44 | Ilmen 15, Y.H. 813 (Fifthday) | the Weir, the Eel House and the blessing stone, and the Vail kiln-house at dusk, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The hearth-maids blessed the traps on the flat stone while the eels ran as they always ran, Orra tied a forty-first knot she could not account for over the story of a small warm covered bundle with a flower carried up the Clay Yard in the year of the flower and said the word forty low into the basket and watched, while the forty-four-day-old boy kept the shape of the knot and the word on separate trays with Mira's two cords still unread, the traps came up full, the river rose under its thinning skin and was called weather, and at dusk he was carried home with both crusts still in the fold and the fire behind him keeping four, four and a gap.
Day 45 | Ilmen 16, Y.H. 813 (Sixthday) | the Fishstairs below the north bridge and the Vail kiln-house at dusk, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | A brine-gull went off upriver with a coal from a banked fire that was not yet cold and the warmth left the carried fire's account with no slate entered and no house missing it, while the boy, forty-five days old in the bridge's shade, could now read the shape of Orra's forty-first knot --- a double turn, tail tucked, tied left over right, set with the weight of a thumb --- without its name and with the word forty unspoken a second time as she watched him watching and said nothing, Mira's grey cord and the second twisted twice beneath it lay on a board beside Orra's that did not touch it, the clerk for the uncalled measure put hand to slate before eyes to the stalls and was filed apart, both crusts still lay in the fold with the smoky one breathing barley when the wind turned, and at dusk under the Vail roof Sella went around the month in loaves and glances with no figure spoken, the carried fire keeping four, four and a gap.
Day 46 | Ilmen 17, Y.H. 813 (Seventhday) | the Vail kiln-house and the Clay Yard, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Alda Reed came short with soap-and-milk and birth-slates, touched her little tin once inside her bodice without opening it, pressed the fold too long at the boy's shoulder, crossed two fingers at the birth-flower and covered the sign with an exact date, saying warm and breathing which was exact and not true and going on with dates after the question had stopped, while Tarn would not count the seventh year and swept the collector's road without turning and Jorra's bent finger went to the damper ahead of Sella's instruction, and in the early afternoon the wind turned off the water and the smell of barley did not come because the smoky crust Mira put in the fold on Stone 28 was gone --- taken quietly by a hand nobody named, not eaten, lifted or spoken of --- leaving the spent crust stiff where it lay and the boy, forty-six days old, holding the empty place beside the coal the gull had taken with no word in Grathic for a thing taken and not spoken of.
Day 47 | Ilmen 18, Y.H. 813 (Eighthday) | the yard well, the drying racks and the Vail kiln-house at dusk, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Frost-bloom stood up in white fronds on the well-lip where the Warm had lately gone out of the stone and was called weather by noon, a number went from a fist to a mouth to a shoulder and arrived nowhere while Berr Clay wiped his thumb twice before touching nothing, said a number goes into a slate, not into a well and went off toward High Kiln with his box still locked, word came up that the clerk still sat at the Fishstairs with a slate for a measure nobody had called and the boy filed the uncalled collection for the woman the brook took nine mornings ago as an interval with the outline of a sound that did not come, a weir woman said one board in the plank walk would not bear a man when the river rose and he set it beside the missing Fishstairs step and the hairline stopping short of the edge without letting the three touch, and at dusk Sella set him on his bed under the roof, forty-seven days old, with one crust in the fold where two had lain and the fire behind him keeping four, four and a gap into a year the district had entered and had not entered him.
Day 48 | Ilmen 19, Y.H. 813 (Ninthday) | the yard well, the Vail window and the drying racks, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Frost wrote itself back onto the well-lip in white fronds where the Warm had lately gone out while the woman who used to say the ice held said nothing and the man who called it honeyed kept his counsel, the oiled paper in the Vail window went slack in its frame under Tarn's silent eye, Sella told the fire it was the thaw and went around an open slate with the basket held at a half-pace, and the forty-eight-day-old boy carried one crust and an unnamed empty place into a new day in which the barley breath did not come when the wind turned off the water, his hand outside the wool going up an inch toward the bucket and coming down.
Day 49 | Ilmen 20, Y.H. 813 (Tenthday, Account-day) | the Cross, the moot-post's shade and the Clay Yard, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | On Account-day Sella set the basket in the moot-post's shade at the Cross where Berr Clay, red cord folded under his coat and green of glaze at his mouth, wiped his thumb twice before touching nothing and read one in twelve twice over the top of the boy while the true subtraction and the thaw number that went from fist to mouth to shoulder were spoken by nobody, the bell went four, four and a gap with nobody crossing himself, and word came up that the clerk still sat at the Fishstairs, the eleventh morning, with his hand to a slate for a measure nobody had called and no sixth sighting of the neat dark hand.
Day 50 | Ilmen 21, Y.H. 813 (Firstday) | the Clay Yard, the Vail wheel-shed and the kiln-house, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Jorra Vail came out of the room off the wheel-shed with grey slate dust ground into the creases past his bent little finger and said nothing of where he had been or of the men under the racks, told a pot it would do and moved the damper before Sella finished her instruction, while Tarn's knife lay on its sill with six years counted and the seventh held and his head did not turn at a vessel's note passing the door, the two silences under one roof keeping their equality, Garl's coat and clean dark line holding his place and Sella going around the house's days in loaves with no figure spoken.
Day 51 | Ilmen 22, Y.H. 813 (Secondday) | the Vail kiln-house, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Alda Reed came short under the roof with soap-and-milk and birth-slates, laid the writing-down before she turned to the fold, pressed it too long at his shoulder, touched her tin once unopened, crossed two fingers at the birth-flower's red edge without knowing it and covered the sign with the date she had given Dessa, went on with exact dates after the question had stopped, said warm and breathing which was exact and not true, and turned a fifth hidden note face-down toward a floorboard in a house the boy could not name while Sella stood a half-pace off and fed him gruel from the house's own dish without wiping his mouth or saying who paid for the milk.
Day 52 | Ilmen 23, Y.H. 813 (Thirdday) | the drying racks, the Clay Yard and the Vail kiln-house, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | A pot set down in the yard out of a rack-woman's shadow answered nearly full under nobody's knuckle, a note with air under it and a want in it, and the boy filed it as a second earthly answer to the interval carried since Stone 13 beside the first of Ilmen 6, both advanced and not paid, while three intervals and six hearings stayed on their own trays with no seventh, Tarn's head did not turn and the loan that can hear stayed uncalled, Jorra's bent finger led at the damper, and the clean dark line where the Ash turned was still not swept.
Day 53 | Ilmen 24, Y.H. 813 (Fourthday) | the Fishstairs below the north bridge, the drying racks and the Vail kiln-house, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | At the Fishstairs Mira Silt, eel slime dried pale on her sleeves and her head held right where her left ear had been, answered a question with a question and her own answer while moving eel-bones against her thumb to decide whether to lie, put that thumb under her apron to the grey cord when a buyer said four marks the pair, and shifting a basket showed the second cord twisted twice beneath it, never carried to Nine Pots, never lifted at a door, the knots on neither counted, as the boy set her board beside Orra's without letting them touch and read the shape of the forty-first knot without its name, the thin child upriver and the unknocked door unspoken.
Day 54 | Ilmen 25, Y.H. 813 (Fifthday) | the yard well, the Ilm beyond the racks and the Vail kiln-house at dusk, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | More hairlines ran into the Ilm's ice and stopped a thumb's breadth short of the edge while a man brought a number in his fist and thought better of giving it and the Singing Sill sounded early and was called weather, the thaw number passed from fist to mouth to shoulder and arrived nowhere, Tarn agreed with early and late and kept wanting the late one, and a weir woman with salt white at her wrists said plainly for the first time that the weak board in the plank walk was still there and would not carry a man when the river came up and nobody had trodden it, so the boy set it beside the missing Fishstairs step and the stopping hairline and let none of them touch, and at dusk Sella set him on his bed, fifty-four days old, one crust in the fold and the empty place carried seven days, the fire missing one breath in every four and nothing joined, paid or explained.

Day 55 | Ilmen 26, Y.H. 813 (Sixthday) | the yard well, the Clay Yard and the Vail kiln-house at dusk, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | A man come downriver from Greybank with eighteen leagues of road-mud told the yard over a bucket how a counting room was shut in the last war, the damper closed and the men inside dead of the breath when their heat went out with no way to feed it, which the yard received as a curiosity with a date while Sella Vail, who had lost a husband to that danger, heard it and told the fire it was the thaw, Tarn agreed with early and late and went on wanting the late because it puts off the paying, and the boy, fifty-five days old in the well's shade, filed the saying on its own tray beside the told bell, the heard bell and the kiln's gap and wrote no cause across any of them.
Day 56 | Ilmen 27, Y.H. 813 (Seventhday) | House Silt three doors down and the Vail kiln-house, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella set the basket just inside a finger's-width-open door at the edge of the light and opened no slate and turned no errand into a bank, and from the low angle the boy saw the round pewter tin above the empty bed with its pale fold and thumb's bright rub keeping birth-cloth three years, while Alda Reed laid her birth-slates writing-down before turning to the fold, pressed it too long, crossed two fingers at the birth-flower's red edge unknowing and covered the sign with a date, said warm and breathing which was exact and not true, touched the tin inside her bodice without opening either, wrote no sixth note and left without the woman's name, so that two shut records in two materials stood on trays that did not touch.
Day 57 | Ilmen 28, Y.H. 813 (Eighthday) | the Clay Yard, the Vail wheel-shed and the kiln-house, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The house kept its days badly in loaves and glances with a space at the end of the loaf row nobody called a number while the boy counted three days to Silt exactly and could spend none of them, Jorra came in with slip dried to old bone, told a pot it would do, knew its full note before the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master, and moved the damper ahead of Sella's instruction without looking toward the bed, Tarn's knife lay on its sill with six years counted and the seventh held and his head did not turn at a vessel's note, and Garl was kept by his salt coat, hollow seat, clean dark line and proverb said to the fire and pot and never the basket.
Day 58 | Ilmen 29, Y.H. 813 (Ninthday) | the drying racks, the Clay Yard and the Vail kiln-house, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The yard prepared a sweeping it had already performed --- grey burned before light on Ilmen 1 and called dealt with --- and Sella laid one thumb in Vail's clean dark line and the same thumb in the death-house ridge a finger's width wider and fetched no broom, Garl's proverb about leaving the crack showing went to a pot with no crack in it, Tarn's broom went over the collector's road without turning and no collector went down it for the woman twenty mornings in the ground, and the boy set the line, the burned ridge, the unentered woman, the weak board at the Weir and the kiln's missing breath in five places that did not touch, seeing that a repair which pretends the break never happened was the district's ordinary kindness.
Day 59 | Ilmen 30, Y.H. 813 (Tenthday, Account-day) | the Cross, the Fishstairs and the Vail kiln-house at dusk, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | At the last market before Silt Berr Clay wiped his thumb twice, read one in twelve twice over the basket, called the levy and took himself out of his own sentence while the true subtraction went over the top and the number for the thaw went from fist to mouth to shoulder and arrived nowhere, the bell went four, four and a gap with nobody crossing himself, the soft-cuffed clerk at the Fishstairs put hand to slate before eyes to stalls and gave no sixth sighting of the neat dark hand, Mira answered a question with her own answer, moved eel-bones while deciding whether to lie and showed a second cord twisted twice beneath the grey with neither knot counted, bringing no crust, a barrow of grey went out unread toward the Bone Orchard, and the river began to move under the skin of the Ilm and was called weather.
Day 60 | Silt 1, Y.H. 813 (Firstday) | the Vail kiln-house, the yard well, the drying racks and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Silt came in on mud with no figure for the days gone before the name changed, a new hairline of grey gathered in the Vail corner beside the old clean dark line and Sella stood over it with a broom and would not sweep because it was the thaw, she touched the widow's mark only before the opening of the kiln, word came up that the clerk was still set at the Fishstairs for an uncalled measure and the weak board in the plank walk was still there and trodden by nobody, the Ilm ran under a skin so thin the dark current showed in veins while the Singing Sill gave its note early and all of it was called weather, Tarn's knife kept six years and a held seventh and his broom went over the collector's road without turning, and at dusk Sella set him on his bed, sixty days old and two months, one crust in the fold and the empty place carried into Silt without a name, the door a finger's width open, the river's low note and the fire's gap kept apart, six hearings still six, and his hand going up an inch toward the new grey and coming down.

Day 61 | Silt 2, Y.H. 813 (Secondday) | the drying racks, the Fishstairs below the north bridge and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The brook came up behind the racks with clay heavier than in Ilmen and the sixty-one-day-old boy, carried level as a pot filled, heard water going over water, a low note with no vessel in it and no word in Grathic, his hand outside the wool going up an inch and coming down, while Sella Vail gave every instruction to the fire, the wool and the bucket and never to the basket, told the fire it was the thaw and touched the widow's mark at no rack or basket; at the Fishstairs the soft-cuffed clerk put hand to slate before eyes to the stalls and said nothing, Mira Silt answered a question with her own answer, moved eel-bones while deciding whether to lie and put her thumb to the grey cord when a figure was spoken, showing a second cord twisted twice beneath it, never carried to Nine Pots or lifted at a door, neither knot counted, and Berr Clay wiped his thumb twice, said a number goes into a slate, not into a well, took himself out of his own sentence and went toward High Kiln with his box locked, never looking into the basket; at dusk Sella set him on his bed with one crust and the empty place beside it and he filed the river's own note on a place of its own beside the told bell, the heard bell, the early Sill note and the kiln's four-four-and-a-gap, writing no cause across any of them.
Day 62 | Silt 3, Y.H. 813 (Thirdday) | the Clay Yard, the Vail wheel-shed and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Tarn set the small bench knife on its sill and told the empty yard it could be checked, held the pale clip-ring at his wrist to the little light and counted six years while holding the seventh back, the one entry in this house the house could not check, agreed so entirely with an early and a late thaw that no argument could find a foot and still wanted the late because a late thaw puts off the paying, while his broom checked half a stroke at the place where a pot cracked on Stone 25, his head did not turn when a vessel gave its note past the door, and the boy kept the third interval as an object and the two ears under one roof as an equality with the loan that can hear still uncalled, while Jorra's bent finger went to the damper ahead of Sella's sentence and the new grey lay beside the clean dark line unswept.
Day 63 | Silt 4, Y.H. 813 (Fourthday) | the Vail wheel-shed, the room off it, the drying racks and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Jorra Vail came out of the room off the shed with fine grey slate dust worked into both forearms past the bend of his little finger and said nothing about the two men who had spoken low behind the door, the broken birth-slates stayed under the rack where the yard went around them and entered them into nothing, he told the wheel it did not care whose hands only if they were steady and told a pot it would do, knowing its full note before it left the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master, and moved the damper with the bent finger before Sella had finished telling him the fire was low without looking toward the bed or the basket, so that he's not Vail stayed sealed and the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates was neither given nor withdrawn.
Day 64 | Silt 5, Y.H. 813 (Fifthday) | House Silt three doors down and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella set the basket just inside a finger's-width-open door at the edge of the light and away from every open slate, opened no slate and turned no errand into a walk to Slate Row, and from the low angle the boy saw the pewter tin above the empty bed keep its pale fold and thumb-bright rub unopened while the small pale river stone was gone from the fold of grey wool on the board-bed and only the fold and the hollow remained, unmentioned; Alda Reed laid her birth-slates writing-down before turning to the fold, pressed it too long, touched the tin in her bodice without opening it, crossed two fingers at the flower before she knew it and covered the sign with the date she had given Dessa, said warm and breathing which was exact and not true, and then wrote the withheld sixth note in the second hand and turned the narrow slate face-down toward a floorboard in a house the boy could not name, so that the hidden record grew from five pages to six and was read by nobody and bought no recognition.
Day 65 | Silt 6, Y.H. 813 (Sixthday) | the drying racks in the Clay Yard and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | A rack-woman set a pot down out of her own shadow and, under nobody's knuckle, it answered nearly full with air under the note and a want in it, a third such answer filed beside Ilmen 6 and Ilmen 23, advanced and not paid, for nearly and full were still two sounds and the boy had no hand to tap the pot and no Grathic word for nearly, while the three intervals from Stone 13, Stone 17 and Stone 25 stayed objects on a separate tray, Tarn's head went a degree toward the note and he went back to his broom without naming it or looking toward the basket, the carried fire missed one breath in every four under blind ash and the back of a broken pot, and the boy wanted the crust two inches from his ear and could not lift a hand to it.
Day 66 | Silt 7, Y.H. 813 (Seventhday) | the edge of the Ilm beyond the racks, the Clay Yard and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The Singing Sill gave its low note earlier than it should, felt in the teeth before the ear placed it, and a woman at the tubs said water ran fast under the ice and then said weather and went home warm on it, while word came up that the weak board in the plank walk was still there and trodden by nobody and the soft-cuffed clerk still set at the Fishstairs for a measure nobody called, and the boy set the early note beside the told bell of the Long Night, the heard bell at the Cross and the kiln's breath under his own roof as four low sounds in four places kept apart for two months, kept six hearings six with no seventh, three near-full answers advanced, the word on its own tray with the shape of the double turn and the weight of a thumb apart, and called the low note coming up through the boards beneath him nothing rather than write a cause across four separate places.
Day 67 | Silt 8, Y.H. 813 (Eighthday) | the yard well, the edge of the Ilm beyond the racks and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The stone lip of the well was wet and mud had got into the rope, the women spoke of the skin on the bucket water to the bucket and never to the boy, and a man come back from looking said to the boards that the ice was honeyed all through, not only at the edge, and a woman said weather and went home warm on it, while beyond the racks the dark current showed through the ice in veins and more hairlines ran in and stopped a thumb's breadth short of the open water, so the boy set the widening hairlines, the honeyed saying, the new grey beside the old clean line, the early note, the still-set clerk and the weak board in places that did not touch and wrote no cause across any of them, wanting the bucket and the crust and able to reach neither; at dusk Sella set him on his bed with one crust and the empty place unnamed, the door a finger's width open, the river's note through the gap and the fire's four-four-and-a-gap through the floor kept apart as the count crossed toward Silt 9 unspent and nothing had been joined, paid, explained or ended.
Day 68 | Silt 9, Y.H. 813 (Ninthday) | the Fishstairs below the north bridge, the low houses below the sill, the plank walk reached by word and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The Ilm came over its stone lip before light and went up the road in a thin brown sheet that the district read as weather while the boy, carried at a half-pace from every open slate, heard under it the note of a whole district under strain --- many small failures inside one slow certain one, with a thin place nobody could name --- and filed it without a word in Grathic or a hand to point; word came up that the weak board in the plank walk was trodden or gone under and nobody had gone to see, so he set it beside the missing Fishstairs step and the stopping hairlines and made no fall of it, while the soft-cuffed clerk kept his slate for a measure nobody had called, Sella gave every instruction to the wool, the fire and the bucket and never to the basket and touched no mark because a road is not an opening, and at dusk one crust lay stiff in the fold with the empty place unnamed, the river's note through the gap and the fire's four-four-and-a-gap through the floor kept apart.
Day 69 | Silt 10, Y.H. 813 (Tenthday, Account-day) | the flooded moot-step at the Cross, the moot-post's shade and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Account-day held itself on the dry step above the standing water while Berr Clay, red cord folded under his coat and green of tasted glaze at one corner of his mouth, wiped his thumb twice, passed a finger over an unopened slate and read one in twelve as the notice says twice over the water and the basket, taking himself out of his own sentence, so that the true subtraction went over the basket unspoken and the number for the flood went from fist to mouth to shoulder and arrived nowhere, and he said *a number goes into a slate, not into a well; there'll be a slate at the watch* and went off toward High Kiln with the box locked and the true deduction inside, believing he had done a kindness; the bell went four, four and a gap and nobody crossed himself, word came up that the clerk was still set at the Fishstairs, and the boy kept the printed figure and the unspoken one on two trays that did not touch, knowing a figure read aloud was not the figure paid.
Day 70 | Silt 11, Y.H. 813 (Firstday) | the drying racks, the death-house ridge two doors down, the Vail corner and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The water had stopped coming and went around instead, around the spent slip and the barrow and around the clean dark line at the Vail corner where the Ash had turned for a year and two days, standing a hand's breadth short of it in a strip of dry wall no wider than two thumbs, while a neighbour said the house was saved and Sella did not, but told the fire it was the thaw and gave every instruction to the wool and the bucket and never to the basket; beside the old line the new hairline of grey, finer than the old, lay unswept, and the boy set the standing line, the water around it and the new grey in three places that did not touch, and into the file went a thing nobody had said --- that a thing left where it is may still be kept --- which he felt as a pull without making a rule or a cause, while Garl was kept by his salt coat, hollow seat and proverb said to a pot with no crack, and Tarn's broom went over the collector's road without turning.
Day 71 | Silt 12, Y.H. 813 (Secondday) | below the sill at the water's edge, the yard well and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The water went down all night and left a child below the sill with a foot dark to the knee that did not come back when she slapped it, and a woman wrapped it twice in wool and pressed it between her palms and called it the cold while the yard called the water weather, so the two facts went past each other on the same morning and never met; the seventy-one-day-old boy read the extremity going before the core and the afterdrop waiting on the far side of a warming nobody would perform, and had no Grathic word, no hand and no standing to put on the shin, so the reading worked perfectly and was worth nothing, while Sella did not go toward the child but turned the basket with her knee and gave the instruction to the wool and the fire, her not-going going on the tray with three other not-lookings as a shape and not a cause, and at the well the thaw argument gave no figure and ended in weather and the crust lay two inches from his ear.
Day 72 | Silt 13, Y.H. 813 (Thirdday) | the Fishstairs below the north bridge, House Silt three doors down and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The falling water gave back the lower steps one at a time with a slow drag over stones that the boy set beside the rising sheet as two shapes and no cause across either, while Mira Silt worked the eels with pale slime dried on her sleeves and silt in the hollow of her neck, answered a question with her own answer while moving eel-bones and putting one away when the deciding was done, and put her thumb under her apron to the grey cord when a buyer named a figure, shifting a creel to show the second twisted twice beneath it, never to Nine Pots or a door, neither knot counted; Orra Weir stood at the flood's edge with her board and knot-string, turned the board on its side and pulled one knot down without a word and got no answer, and three doors down the pewter tin above the empty bed kept its pale fold and thumb-bright rub while the grey fold below held only the hollow and Alda's sixth page stayed turned toward a floorboard, so that three private accounts in two materials stood unread while the water went down.
Day 73 | Silt 14, Y.H. 813 (Fourthday) | the Clay Yard, the Vail wheel-shed and the room off it (not entered), and the rack's edge, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The sweeping that had been done twice before went around the ridge a third time and the ridge stood through all three while the space under the lowest rack where whole slate should have stood was gone around as wide as a pail of hot water set where a child might knock it and entered by nobody, so Sella gave the instruction to the broom before anyone, *Round it. You'll pay for it in the thaw if you take it up*; Jorra Vail came through the finger's-width-open door off the wheel-shed with fine grey slate dust worked past the bend of his little finger, named neither the two men in the back room nor the room, spoke to the wheel before anyone and put his bent finger to the damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence without looking toward the bed or the basket, keeping *he's not Vail* sealed and the promise of the foundling's name neither given nor withdrawn; Tarn's knife lay on its sill with six years counted and the seventh nearly gone and still held, his broom went over the collector's road without turning and his head did not turn at a vessel's note, and the boy held the dusty forearms and the clean ones on one tray and the two silences under one roof on another, neither becoming a cause.
Day 74 | Silt 15, Y.H. 813 (Fifthday) | the mud of the Clay Yard, the yard well, the Vail wheel-shed and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The flood went out of the yard with one thing left behind that nobody would name and left mud that took a boot and gave it back slowly, so the yard counted its losses in loaves, glances and a space at the row's end nobody called a number while the boy, seventy-four days old, kept the count exactly against the house's bad arithmetic and let the printed figure, the unspoken one and the figure the water would have written lie on three places; the weak board was gone or trodden flat under the silt and no accident was made of it, and the honeyed ice, the stopping hairlines and the early Sill note were named past tense as the thaw that was, each kept on its own place; Jorra came back to the wheel with clean bone-coloured slip and no grey past his little finger, and the boy held that morning and the dusty one on one tray, while Sella stood over the two lines with a broom and did not sweep either; six hearings stayed six and no seventh came though a vessel gave its note past the door, and at dusk, with one crust stiff in the fold and the empty place unnamed, the river's muttered note came through the finger's width of open door and the fire's four, four and a gap came through the floor in separate places, his hand going up an inch toward the water and down as the count crossed into Silt 16 unspent.
Day 75 | Silt 16, Y.H. 813 (Sixthday) | the mud of the Clay Yard, the drying racks, the yard well and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The flood water went out and left mud that took a boot to the ankle and gave it back slowly, so Sella Vail told the broom to go around the clean dark line, the new finer grey and the space under the lowest rack before she told anybody and gave every instruction to the wool, the fire and the bucket and never to the basket, while the yard counted its losses in loaves, glances and a space at the row's end nobody called a number and the figure went from a fist to a mouth to a shoulder and arrived nowhere, the boy keeping it exactly; at the well a small foundling in another's cast-offs finished a kiln-hand's sentence in an affectionate voice, turned his head toward a pot a half-beat after the boy's and rubbed the green sliver at his neck, and a man from Slate Row whose name was on no roll gave him a warm loaf that went into a pocket holding no slate, while Sella went wide around an open slate and touched no widow's mark and Tarn's knife lay on its sill with six years counted and the seventh held, his broom going over the collector's road without turning and his head not turning at a vessel's note, as at dusk the count ended at seventy-five with one crust stiff in the fold and the empty place unnamed.
Day 76 | Silt 17, Y.H. 813 (Seventhday) | the Fishstairs below the north bridge, House Silt three doors down and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The steps came back four, four and a landing a hand's breadth out of line that nobody made a fall of, and the soft-cuffed clerk sat for a measure nobody called with his hand to his slate before his eyes to the stalls, while Mira Silt worked the eels with pale slime on her sleeves and silt in the hollow of her neck, answered a question with her own answer, moved eel-bones while deciding and put her thumb under her apron to the grey cord when a buyer named a figure, shifting a creel to show the second twisted twice beneath it, never to Nine Pots or a door, neither knot counted, her board beside Orra's without touching; three doors down the pewter tin above the empty bed kept its pale fold and thumb-bright rub and the grey fold below held only the hollow of the small pale stone, while Alda Reed laid her birth-slates down before turning to the fold, pressed it too long, crossed two fingers at the flower unknowing, covered the sign with the date she had given the woman thirty-eight mornings dead, said warm and breathing which was exact and not true, touched the tin in her bodice without opening it and turned her sixth narrow slate face-down toward a floorboard, so that the hidden record stood at six pages unread; at dusk the count ended at seventy-six with one crust in the fold and the empty place unnamed.
Day 77 | Silt 18, Y.H. 813 (Eighthday) | the Clay Yard, the wheel-shed and the room off it (not entered), the drying racks, the yard well and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The mud set into a brown skin over the ruts and the broom went around the two lines, the space under the rack and the broken birth-slates entered by nobody, while Jorra Vail came through the finger's-width-open wheel-shed door with fine grey slate dust worked into both forearms past the bend of his little finger and named neither the two men who came in since first light nor the back room, spoke to the wheel before anyone, put his bent finger to the damper ahead of Sella's sentence and did not look toward the bed or the basket, keeping he's not Vail sealed and the promise of the foundling's name neither given nor withdrawn; Tarn's knife held six years counted and a seventh nearly gone and still held, his broom went over the collector's road without turning and his head did not turn at a whole vessel's note, and at the well the argument about the skin on the water ended in weather as the boy kept seventy-five, seventy-six and seventy-seven as three slates set edge to edge against the house's bad arithmetic, six hearings six, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, the word unspoken and the half-beat and warm loaf on a place of their own; at dusk Sella set him on his bed under the roof with one crust stiff in the fold and the empty place unnamed, the thread at his mouth frozen and unwiped, the river's note through the finger's-width-open door and the fire's four, four and a gap through the floor kept apart, his hand going up an inch toward the water and down as the count crossed into Silt 19 unspent.
Day 78 | Silt 19, Y.H. 813 (Ninthday) | the mud of the Clay Yard, the drying racks, the yard well and the bed under the Vail kiln-house roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The mud had set into a brown skin over the ruts, holding a boot's print and letting it go slowly, so Sella Vail told the broom to go around the clean dark line, the new finer grey called thaw and the space under the lowest rack where the broken birth-slates lay entered by nobody, and a man brought a figure in his fist toward the well where it arrived nowhere while the seventy-eight-day-old boy kept it exactly against the house's loaves, glances and unnamed space; near the well a small foundling in cast-offs turned his head toward a pot a half-beat after the boy's, rubbed the green sliver at his neck and could not say why, and four objects --- the delay, the sliver, a warm loaf into a pocket holding no slate, an unfinished sentence --- went into the file on separate places with no secret passing; the carried fire kept four, four and a gap with its payer unnamed, Tarn's knife sat on its sill with six counted and the seventh held, the child's cold foot below the sill was still the cold to one woman and weather to the yard, and at dusk Sella set him on the bed with one crust stiff in the fold and the empty place unnamed, the thread at his mouth frozen and unwiped.
Day 79 | Silt 20, Y.H. 813 (Tenthday, Account-day) | the Cross, the moot-post's shade, the Court's table, the Fishstairs below the north bridge and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | On a thinner Account-day crowd Berr Clay, red cord folded under his coat and green glaze at one mouth-corner, wiped his thumb twice before touching nothing, read one in twelve over fewer bodies, put himself out of his own sentence with *The levy is called. It is not called by me* and went off toward High Kiln with the box locked and the true deduction inside believing he had done a kindness, while the printed figure and the true subtraction lay on two trays that did not touch and a figure read aloud was not the figure paid; the bell went four, four and a gap and nobody crossed himself, the Court-table clerk put hand to slate before eyes to the woman, at the Fishstairs the steps came back four, four and a landing a hand's breadth out of line that the market walked over without a fall with the soft-cuffed clerk still set for an uncalled measure, and Mira Silt answered a question with her own answer, moved eel-bones while deciding and put her thumb to the grey cord when a figure was named, showing the second twisted twice beneath it with neither knot counted and her board beside Orra's without touching; no sixth sighting of the neat dark hand came.
Day 80 | Silt 21, Y.H. 813 (Firstday) | House Silt three doors down and the bed under the Vail kiln-house roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella set the basket just inside a finger's-width-open door at the edge of the light and away from every place a slate might lie, opened no slate and turned no errand into a walk to Slate Row, and from the low angle the round pewter tin above the abandoned board-bed kept its pale fold and thumb-bright rub unopened while the grey wool below held only the hollow where the small pale river stone had lain and nobody said either was gone; Alda Reed laid her birth-slates writing-down on the bench before turning to the fold, pressed it too long, crossed two fingers at the birth-flower's red edge unknowing and covered the sign with the date given a woman forty mornings dead, said *Warm and breathing, that's all I've set down* which was exact and not true, went on with dates after the question had stopped and touched the little tin in her bodice without opening it, while her sixth narrow slate stayed turned face-down toward a floorboard in a house the boy could not name and the hidden record stood at six pages unread with no seventh written; two private records in two materials stood unread in one room and a third board downriver stood beside Orra's knot-string without touching.
Day 81 | Silt 22, Y.H. 813 (Secondday) | the Clay Yard, the Vail wheel-shed, the back room (not entered), the drying racks and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Men came in since first light and spoke low in the back room and the house gave them no name, while Jorra Vail came through with fine grey slate dust worked into both forearms past the bend of his little finger, not bone-coloured slip, and the boy kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and asked neither which slate was broken nor whether it was a birth-slate; Jorra spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), told a pot *You'll do*, knew its full note before it left the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master from High Kiln, and put his bent finger to the damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the household straightened around his silence, while he did not say the back room was cold, did not look toward the bed or basket, kept *he's not Vail* sealed and neither gave nor withdrew the promise of the foundling's name to the men who burn slates; Tarn's knife was on its sill and his broom went over the collector's road without once turning, a vessel gave its note past the door and his head did not turn and he did not name the sound, and the broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack entered by nobody.
Day 82 | Silt 23, Y.H. 813 (Thirdday) | the Clay Yard, the Vail wheel-shed and the bed under the Vail kiln-house roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Before the light Tarn set the small bench knife on its sill and told the empty yard it could be checked, six years standing in a mark worn almost flat by his thumb and the seventh nearly gone and still held, the one entry in this house the house cannot check, and told the kindling *Six counted. The next I don't count* while going on holding the breath he had decided not to spend though his chest was already tight; he agreed so entirely with the woman who wanted the thaw early and the man who wanted it late that no argument could find a foot and kept wanting the late because a late thaw puts off the paying, though the paying had stood in the ruts all week, and Ash was worked into the creases of his knuckles in the colour of the clean dark line and no scrubbing lifted it while the pale clip-ring at his left wrist held what light there was and gave it back; his broom went over the collector's road without turning and no collector came down it, a vessel gave its note past the door and his head did not turn and the boy did not call it a seventh hearing, six hearings stayed six, three near-full answers stayed advanced and not paid, and two ears and two silences kept their equality under one roof with the loan that can hear still uncalled.
Day 83 | Silt 24, Y.H. 813 (Fourthday) | the plank walk at the Weir, the Fishstairs below the north bridge, the Clay Yard and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Word came up the river road that the weak board in the plank walk was gone or trodden flat by the flood and nobody had been down to see which, and no accident was made of it, so the boy set the gone board beside the missing Fishstairs step and the hairlines that stopped a thumb's breadth short of the edge in three places that did not touch; by noon the honeyed ice, the stopping hairlines and the early Singing Sill note were spoken of only as *the thaw that was*, a thing said after the fact treated as a thing said before it, and the argument that had held the yard a month went home warm on the word, while the steps came back four, four and a landing a hand's breadth out of line that a whole market walked over carefully without making a fall of it and the soft-cuffed clerk still sat for an uncalled measure with hand to slate before eyes to stalls; Mira Silt worked the eels, answered the weir woman's question with her own answer, moved eel-bones while deciding and showed the second cord twisted twice beneath the grey with neither knot counted and her board beside Orra's not touching, the bell came back four, four and a gap with nobody crossing himself, and the new finer grey lay unswept beside the old line under the Vail roof as the count crossed toward the last day of the week unspent.
