Day 84 | Silt 25, Y.H. 813 (Fifthday) | the yard well, the Clay Yard and the bed under the Vail kiln-house roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The bucket-round began before the light and a second man come downriver from Greybank, road-mud dried grey to the calf and the salt-and-hot-clay smell of the east road on him, told the yard of a counting room shut in the last war with the damper closed and the men inside dead of the breath when their heat went out with no way to feed it, a year two wars back and eighteen leagues east, and gave no name, so the yard took it as an old curiosity with a date, spoke of the war and of a room shut up being a room nobody entered on a slate, argued the thaw early against late and went home warm on the word without saying kiln or Vail; Sella told the fire it was the thaw and made nothing of it and did not touch the widow's mark, while the boy heard the breath and the damper where the yard heard a date, knew the anatomy of a room sealed against its own heat and filed the second telling on the same tray as the first without letting the two touch or joining either to any kiln; six hearings stayed six, three near-full answers stayed advanced and not paid, the word was not spoken, no collector came, the two lines stayed unswept, the payer of the coal stayed unnamed, the cold foot stayed the cold to one woman and weather to the yard, Dessa stayed forty mornings dead and uncollected, and at dusk Sella set him on the bed with one crust in the fold and the empty place unnamed, left the thread at his mouth frozen and unwiped, and the river's note came through the open door while the fire's missing breath came through the floor, the two kept apart as the count crossed into Silt 26 unspent.

Day 85 | Silt 26, Y.H. 813 (Sixthday) | the mud of the Clay Yard, the drying racks, the yard well and the bed under the Vail kiln-house roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The mud had set into a brown skin over the ruts holding a boot's print and giving it back slowly, so Sella Vail told the broom to go around the clean dark line, the new finer grey called thaw and the space under the lowest rack where the broken birth-slates lay entered by nobody, and the yard counted its losses in loaves, glances and a space at the row's end nobody called a number while the boy, eighty-five days old, kept it exactly; over the bucket-round a woman said the shut-room story had a date in it and there was no third telling, so the boy kept the two tellings on their own tray joined to no kiln, one crust lay stiff in the fold with the empty place unnamed, Tarn's knife sat on its sill with six counted and his broom went over the collector's road without turning, the child's cold foot below the sill was still the cold to one woman and weather to the yard, and at dusk Sella set him on his bed with the fold put right and the thread at his mouth frozen as the count crossed into Silt 27 unspent.
Day 86 | Silt 27, Y.H. 813 (Seventhday) | the Clay Yard, the Vail wheel-shed, the back room (not entered) and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Before the light Tarn set the small bench knife on its sill and told the empty yard where it was, six years worn almost flat by his thumb and the seventh nearly gone and still held, the one entry in this house the house could not check, and said *Six counted. The next I don't count* to the kindling while going on holding the breath he had decided not to spend; he agreed so entirely with the early and the late thaw that no argument could find a foot and went on wanting the late because it puts off the paying, his broom went over the collector's road without turning and no collector came, Jorra Vail came through the wheel-shed door with grey slate dust worked past his bent little finger and named neither the men who had spoken low in the back room nor the room itself, spoke to the wheel before anyone, told a pot it would do, moved the damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence without looking toward the bed or the basket and kept *he's not Vail* sealed while a vessel gave its note past the door and neither man turned and the loan that can hear stayed uncalled, something having to give at the end of the seventh year that did not give here.
Day 87 | Silt 28, Y.H. 813 (Eighthday) | House Silt three doors down and the bed under the Vail kiln-house roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella set the basket just inside the finger's-width-open door at the edge of the light and away from every place a slate might lie and opened no slate, and from the low angle the pewter tin above the empty bed kept its pale fold and thumb-bright rub while the grey wool below held only the hollow where the small pale river stone had lain and nobody said either was gone; Alda Reed came short with soap-and-milk gone cold under river silt, laid her birth-slates writing-down on the bench before she turned to the fold, pressed it a breath too long, crossed two fingers at the birth-flower before she knew it and covered the sign with the date of a woman more mornings dead than any living person keeps count of, said *Warm and breathing, that's all I've set down* which was exact and not true, touched the tin in her bodice without opening it and left her sixth narrow slate face-down with six pages unread, so that two private records in two materials stood apart in one room and were carried to no door as the count crossed into Silt 29 unspent.
Day 88 | Silt 29, Y.H. 813 (Ninthday) | the Fishstairs below the north bridge and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | At the Fishstairs the steps came back four, four and a landing a hand's breadth out of line that a whole market walked over carefully without making a fall of it while the soft-cuffed clerk sat with his slate to his forearm for a measure nobody had called and his hand went to the slate before his eyes went to the stalls; Mira Silt worked the eels with pale slime dried on her sleeves and silt in the hollow of her neck, answered a question with her own answer and flicked eel-bones against her thumb while deciding whether to lie, put her thumb under her apron to the grey cord when a buyer named a figure and showed the second cord twisted twice beneath it, never carried to Nine Pots or lifted at a door with neither knot counted and the knot for the thin child upriver untouched, her board lying beside Orra's knot-string without touching it while Orra watched and got no answer, and at dusk Sella set the boy on his bed with one crust and the empty place unnamed and the fire's gap and the river's note in two places as the count crossed into Silt 30 unspent.
Day 89 | Silt 30, Y.H. 813 (Tenthday, Account-day) | the Cross, the moot-post's shade, the Fishstairs and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | On Account-day and the last market of Silt Sella set the basket in the moot-post's finger's width of shade at the Cross with the cold iron at his cheek, and Berr Clay came up with the red wool cord folded under his coat and green glaze at one corner of his mouth, wiped his thumb twice before touching nothing, read one in twelve twice over the basket, passed a finger across the figure, said *The levy is called. It is not called by me* and *A number goes into a slate, not into a well* and went off toward High Kiln with the box locked and the true deduction inside believing he had done a kindness, while the number went from a fist to a mouth to a shoulder and arrived nowhere and the bell went four, four and a gap with nobody crossing himself; a woman with green at one corner of her mouth stood across the market and was filed in silence, the market walked carefully over the out-of-line landing at the Fishstairs with the clerk still set, and a small foundling in cast-offs turned his head toward a pot a half-beat after the boy's, rubbed the green sliver at his throat and could not say why, no loaf passing and no secret sold, as the last market closed and the count crossed into Seed unspent.
Day 90 | Seed 1, Y.H. 813 (Firstday) | the Clay Yard, the drying racks, the yard well and the bed under the Vail kiln-house roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Seed opened on mud and the first work of the planting month was sweeping, not planting, as Sella gave the broom its instruction before anybody and went around the clean dark line, the new finer grey called thaw and the space under the lowest rack without sweeping any of the three; at the well she moved her lips along the loaf shelf, glanced at the clay jar and left the space at the row's end with no figure, telling the woman to ask the pot what a wet thing costs, while a man said the strips east of town would want hands by the morrow and a woman said the weather would have the last word and nobody put a figure to hands or strips, the boy ninety days old and three months setting the strips on a place of their own as a direction and not a number; Tarn's knife sat on its sill with six counted and the seventh held and his broom went over the collector's road without turning, Jorra was heard at the wheel and not seen, the carried fire kept four, four and a gap under blind ash with its payer unnamed for ninety days, one brown-edged crust lay stiff in the fold with the empty place beside it carried into Seed without a name and no third came, and at dusk Sella set him on his bed with the thread at his mouth frozen, the river's note through the finger's-width-open door and the fire's missing breath through the floor in two places, his hand going up an inch toward the water and down as the count went on unspent.
Day 91 | Seed 2, Y.H. 813 (Secondday) | the barley fields east of Low Kiln, the Clay Yard, the yard well and the bed under the Vail kiln-house roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The direction carried for a month became worked ground as brooms went around the strips east of Low Kiln and seed went in from a fist in handfuls nobody counted aloud while a man said the strips would want hands and a woman said the weather would have the last word and neither put a figure to either, Berr Clay came down with his office cord folded under his coat, wiped his thumb twice before touching nothing, said a number goes into a slate, not a strip and that the levy was called but not by him, and went off toward High Kiln with his box locked and the true subtraction unspoken, at the well Sella Vail moved her lips along the loaf shelf, glanced at the clay jar and left the empty place at the row's end with no figure, telling the woman to ask the pot what a wet thing costs set down there and that the house had said it, while the boy, ninety-one days old, kept the count exactly against the house's loaves, glances and unnamed space, Tarn's knife sat on its sill with six counted and the seventh held, a vessel gave its note past the door and neither man turned, Jorra was heard at the wheel and not seen, the carried fire kept four, four and a gap under blind ash with its payer unnamed for ninety-one days, and at dusk Sella set him on his bed with one crust stiff in the fold, the empty place beside it still unnamed and no third, the river's note through the finger's-width-open door and the fire's gap through the floor in two places, his hand going up an inch toward the water and down as the count crossed into Seed 3 unspent.
Day 92 | Seed 3, Y.H. 813 (Thirdday) | the Vail wheel-shed and the back room (not entered), the Clay Yard and the bed under the Vail kiln-house roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Men came in since first grey and spoke low in the back room and Jorra Vail gave them no name and did not say the room was cold, coming through the finger's-width-open wheel-shed door with fine grey slate dust worked into both forearms past the bend of his little finger, not bone-coloured slip, while the broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack entered by nobody, he spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), told a pot *You'll do*, knew its full note before it left the wheel and would not say so before a kiln-master from High Kiln, and put his bent finger into the damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the household straightened around his silence, reading the iron as the bite that holds a vessel whole and can take a hand off a man with no Grathic word for both mercies, while Tarn set the small bench knife on its sill with six years counted and the seventh nearly gone and still held, said *Six counted. The next I don't count* to the kindling, went on holding a breath he had decided not to spend, said *Yes, house* to an early thaw and a late and kept wanting the late because it puts off the paying, swept his broom over the collector's road without turning, and did not turn or name a vessel's note past the door in the afternoon, no collector coming and the loan that can hear staying uncalled, as at dusk Sella set the boy, ninety-two days old, on his bed with one crust and the empty place unnamed, the two sounds kept apart as for ninety-two days and the count standing at ninety-two unspent, crossed into Seed 4 with the damper where a bent finger had left it.
Day 93 | Seed 4, Y.H. 813 (Fourthday) | the Fishstairs below the north bridge, House Silt three doors down and the bed under the Vail kiln-house roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | At the dawn market Sella set the basket at the edge of the fish-trade shade where no slate lay and gave the instruction to the wool and the water and never to the basket, Mira Silt worked the eels with pale slime dried on her sleeves and silt in the hollow of her neck, answered whether the run would be thin with *Thin? Who's to say thin? It comes when it comes. Ask the river*, moved eel-bones against her thumb while deciding whether to lie and put her thumb under her apron to the grey cord when a buyer named a figure, shifting a creel to show the second cord twisted twice beneath it, never carried to Nine Pots or lifted at a door, neither knot counted and the knot for the thin child upriver untouched, her board lying beside Orra's knot-string without touching it while Orra watched and got no answer, the steps coming back four, four and a landing a hand's breadth out of line that a whole market walked over carefully without a fall, word coming that the weak board at the Weir was gone or trodden flat and nobody had gone down to see which, the soft-cuffed clerk still sitting at the landing with his slate to his forearm for a measure nobody had called and his hand going to the slate before his eyes went to the stalls, filed apart from Marra, the Drawing collector and the Court-table clerk with no sixth sighting of the neat dark hand, while three doors down in House Silt Alda Reed laid her birth-slates writing-down on the bench before turning to the fold, pressed it a breath too long, crossed two fingers at the birth-flower before she knew it and covered the sign with the date she had given a woman more mornings dead than any living person keeps count of, said *Warm and breathing, that's all I've set down*, exact and not true, went on with dates after the question stopped, touched the little tin in her bodice without opening it and left her sixth narrow slate face-down toward a floorboard with six pages unread and no seventh written, so that two private records in two materials stood apart in one room, read by nobody and carried to no door, and at dusk Sella set the boy, ninety-three days old, on his bed under the roof with one brown-edged crust stiff in the fold, the empty place beside it unnamed and no third, six hearings staying six, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, the word forty unspoken, the two tellings joined to no kiln, the loan uncalled and the cold foot still the cold, the river's note through the finger's-width-open door and the fire's four, four and a gap through the floor in two places, his hand going up an inch toward the water and down as the first planting week went into the ground with the arithmetic unchanged and the count at ninety-three unspent.
Day 94 | Seed 5, Y.H. 813 (Fifthday) | the barley fields east of Low Kiln, the Clay Yard, the yard well, the drying racks and the bed under the Vail kiln-house roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The planting became worked ground as brooms went around the stone the flood had set in the strips and a woman's fist let seed out and came back with chaff in its creases while nobody put a figure to hands, strips or seed, Sella Vail gave the broom its instruction before anybody and never to the basket and told the woman at the well to ask the pot what a wet thing costs set down there because *the house has said it*, Tarn swept the collector's road over and over without once turning along it with six years counted and the seventh nearly gone and still held and said *Six counted. The next I don't count* to the kindling, and the boy, ninety-four days old, read the emptied fist as once he had read a chart closed with a neat line and a signature --- a clean reading worth nothing --- while his drool froze, his head went where it wanted with the small wet sound, cold arrived cheek, temple, wrist and his hand went up an inch toward the note of water and came down, at dusk the carried fire keeping four, four and a gap under blind ash and the back of a broken pot with its payer unnamed for ninety-four days and one brown-edged crust lying stiff in the fold with the empty place beside it still unnamed and no third.
Day 95 | Seed 6, Y.H. 813 (Sixthday) | the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf and clay jar, the Clay Yard and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | A loaf's worth went off the shelf between dusk and light with no scrape, crumb, print or word so the row stood with two empty places where one used to stand --- the old space at the end nobody calls a number and a new palm's breadth of bare board with the next loaf settled a finger's breadth toward it --- and a neighbour's hand passed over the gap, opened for the half-beat it takes to find what is not there, closed on the loaf beyond and went on with her pot without a second look, while Sella moved her lips along the row, glanced at the clay jar, gave the instruction to the pot and the fire and never to the basket and said *the house* where another woman would say *I*, and the boy set the shelf's new gap and the empty place in the fold on two places that did not touch and wrote no cause across the space between them, having learned what three likenesses in a row will do to a man who wants an answer, wanting the number of his days said by somebody who would know what it counted and getting none, while Tarn's knife lay on its sill with six counted in a mark his thumb had worn almost flat and the seventh nearly gone and still held, a loaf carried out under a woman's arm went unentered and unnamed, and at dusk the fire missed its fifth breath with its payer unnamed for ninety-five days, the new grey lay unswept and was called the thaw and the fold kept one crust and one unnamed empty place in a room that did not touch the shelf's.
Day 96 | Seed 7, Y.H. 813 (Seventhday) | the back room off the Vail wheel-shed (not entered), the wheel-shed, the Clay Yard and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Men came in before first grey and spoke low in the finger's-width-open back room and were given no name, and Jorra Vail came through with fine grey slate dust worked into both forearms past the bend of his little finger, not bone-coloured slip, spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), told a pot *You'll do* and knew its full note before it left the wheel without saying so before a kiln-master from High Kiln who would call it early and make something of it, then put his bent finger into the damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the household straightened around his silence the way cloth straightens when a weight is lifted, while the broken birth-slates stayed under the lowest rack gone around as wide as a pail of hot water set where a child might knock it and entered by nobody and the boy read the iron as the bite that holds a vessel whole and can take a hand off a man with no Grathic word for both mercies and filed it as an object read perfectly and worth nothing, while Tarn's broom went over the collector's road without turning and he kept the unforged sentence from the frozen ford with the seventh year, a vessel gave its note past the door and his head did not turn and the boy did not call it a seventh hearing, *he's not Vail* stayed sealed and the promise of the foundling's name was neither given nor withdrawn, and at dusk one crust lay stiff in the fold with no third, the damper was cold under Jorra's bent finger and the room off the shed stood a finger's width open with no name given to the men.
Day 97 | Seed 8, Y.H. 813 (Eighthday) | the Fishstairs below the north bridge, House Silt three doors down and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella set the basket at the edge of the fish-trade shade where no slate lay and gave the instruction to the wool and the water and never to the basket, Mira Silt worked the eels with pale slime dried on her sleeves and silt in the hollow of her neck, answered whether the run would be thin with *Thin? Who's to say thin? It comes when it comes. Ask the river*, moved eel-bones against her thumb while deciding whether to lie and put one away when the deciding was done, and put her thumb under her apron to the grey cord when a buyer named a figure, shifting a creel to show a second cord twisted twice beneath it, never carried upriver to Nine Pots or lifted at a door with neither knot counted and the knot for the thin child upriver untouched, her board lying beside Orra Weir's knot-string without touching it while Orra watched and got no answer, the steps coming back four, four and a landing a hand's breadth out of line that a whole market walked over carefully without a fall and the soft-cuffed clerk still sitting at the landing with his slate to his forearm for a measure nobody called and his hand going to the slate before his eyes to the stalls, filed apart from Marra, the Drawing collector and the Court-table clerk with no sixth sighting of the neat dark hand, while three doors down in House Silt Alda Reed laid her birth-slates writing-down on the bench before turning to the fold, pressed it a breath too long, crossed two fingers at the birth-flower's red edge before she knew it, covered the sign with a date she had given a woman more mornings dead than any living person keeps count of, said *Warm and breathing, that's all I've set down* which was exact and not true, went on with dates after the question had stopped, touched the little tin in her bodice without opening it and turned her sixth narrow slate face-down toward a floorboard in a house the boy could not name so that the hidden record stood at six pages unread with no seventh written, while the pewter tin above the empty bed kept its pale fold and the grey wool below held only the hollow where the small pale stone had lain and the boy kept the two private records apart with the only instrument he had, which was attention, and bought nothing.
Day 98 | Seed 9, Y.H. 813 (Ninthday) | below the sill by the low houses, the edge of the Fishstairs market and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The water went out of the low houses and left a child with a foot that stayed dark inside three turns of wool while a woman wound it round and called it the cold and a neighbour at the tubs called the weather so the two facts went past each other and never met, and the boy read the extremity going before the core and the afterdrop waiting on the far side of a warming nobody would perform and had no word and no hand two paces long so the reading worked perfectly and was worth nothing, Sella shifting the basket out of the draught, saying *The house doesn't go down to a foot* and not going down though it was told of her in the yard that she once sat three nights with a child of her own who cooled while everybody was kind, while at the market's edge a small foundling in cast-off clothes worn like a uniform with silt dried pale in his wrists, a gap where a front tooth had been and a green sliver of lead-glaze on a string at his throat turned his head half a beat after the boy toward a pot set down, after the knuckle and after the note, then stopped, rubbed the sliver and could not say why, opened his mouth on the tail of a kiln-hand's unfinished sentence and let nothing out that a slate could take down, no new loaf passing and no slate being opened within reach of the basket while the warm loaf from Slate Row on Silt 16 went on gathering interest in an account the child could not read, and the boy filed the delay on a place of its own after the knock and not before it, not a hearing and not a seventh, and kept his own turning beside it without letting the two touch, the soft-cuffed clerk still set at the landing for a measure nobody called, and at dusk the cold foot lay on its own place and the half-beat on another with the damper's double bite filed as an object, six hearings six, one crust and its unnamed empty place and no third, and the fire's payer unnamed for ninety-eight days.
Day 99 | Seed 10, Y.H. 813 (Tenthday, Account-day) | the Cross (reading block and moot-post shade), a short turn at the Fishstairs landing and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Account-day turned out for the figure as for weather while eight pigs went over the wet reading block and a Sill assessor wrote them down before anybody could ask which eight he meant, his hand going to the slate before his eyes to the rail and then his mouth, an order the boy had seen in a midwife, at a well, behind a Court table, in a man who priced a basket and in a house that instructed a pot before a person, each instance on its own place and no cause joining them, and Berr Clay came up with the red wool cord of his office folded under his coat and green glaze at one corner of his mouth, wiped his thumb twice before touching nothing, read *one in twelve, as the notice says* twice over the basket, passed a finger over the figure, called the levy and said *It is not called by me* and *A number goes into a slate, not into a strip; if the house wants it again, there'll be a slate at the watch*, and went off toward High Kiln with the box locked and the true subtraction inside believing he had done a kindness, so that the number for the planting went from a fist to a mouth to a shoulder and arrived nowhere and a figure read aloud was not the figure paid, the bell going four, four and a place where the fifth should have been with nobody crossing himself and the boy keeping the heard bell and the told bell on two places, while at the Slate Row fringe a man whose name was on no roll with soft hands and a patient face handed a figure to a slate at the Court's table, holding it where nobody else could see and resting his thumb on the corner afterward without passing it over the number as Berr had, and the boy filed the soft hands apart from Marra, the Drawing collector, the Court-table clerk and the Fishstairs clerk, apart from the child and the loaf, and did not call it a sale, the soft-cuffed clerk still at the landing with hand to slate before eyes to stalls and no sixth sighting of the neat dark hand, and the boy lay ninety-nine days old inside an account that had gone over him twice without once addressing him or knowing his number, as at dusk the fire's payer went unnamed for ninety-nine days and one crust kept its unnamed empty place with no third.
Day 100 | Seed 11, Y.H. 813 (Firstday) | the Clay Yard, the barley fields east of Low Kiln and the bed under the Vail kiln-house roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The boy was one hundred days old, three months and ten days, and nobody marked it, said it or gave him a figure for it, the seed standing in the strips where the fists had let it out and the yard going around the boot-print, the clean dark line, the new finer grey called thaw and the space under the lowest rack with a broom and a glance and no figure spoken, Sella giving the broom its instruction before anybody and never to the basket, Tarn's knife lying on its sill with six years standing in a mark his thumb had worn almost flat and the seventh nearly gone and still held while he said *Six counted. The next I don't count*, said *Yes, house* to an early and a late thaw and kept wanting the late because it puts off the paying, something having to give at the end of the seventh year that did not give on the hundredth morning, Jorra heard at the wheel and not seen with the back room unmentioned and *he's not Vail* sealed, and under the roof the private file checked unchanged with six hearings staying six beside and never among and no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, three intervals on another tray, the word forty unspoken with the readable double turn beside it and the weight of a thumb apart, four low things in four places with the river's note a fifth and not a hearing, the two tellings of the shut room joined to no kiln and no third telling, the damper's double bite filed as an object and the loan that can hear still uncalled, while the loaf shelf kept its two unnamed gaps and the fold its one stiff crust and one unnamed empty place in two rooms that did not touch and no third crust came, every warmth having a payer and none entered, and at dusk Sella set him on his bed under the roof and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face and left the thread frozen, the river's note coming through the finger's-width-open door and the fire's four, four and a gap through the floor in two separate places as for a hundred days, his hand going up an inch toward the note of water going over water and coming down with the count standing at one hundred, unspent.
Day 101 | Seed 12, Y.H. 813 (Secondday) | the Clay Yard, the loaf shelf in the Vail kiln-house and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The boy was one hundred and one days old and nobody said so, while a neighbour at the loaf shelf reached past the bare board and named the old space at the row's end as a thing that came in with the planting and the empty place at his ear as a child's appetite in one breath without once seeing they were one absence, a witness planted for a far-off account (T097), and Sella Vail said the house where another would say I, instructed the pot, fire and broom but never the basket, and left both gaps unfigured, while Tarn swept the collector's road over and over without once turning along it, his knife holding six counted years and a seventh nearly gone and still held as he told the kindling Six counted. The next I don't count, and a woman moving a pot lifted it a hand's breadth too high so something came up to the rim of a note and went back down without crossing it, giving nothing worth calling and adding no seventh, and at dusk Sella put him on his bed under the roof with the fold right and the thread frozen, the carried fire answering four, four and a gap under blind ash with its payer unnamed for a hundred and one days and the river's note and the fire's gap coming in two separate places as his hand went up an inch toward the water and came down, the count standing at one hundred and one unspent.
Day 102 | Seed 13, Y.H. 813 (Thirdday) | the Vail kiln-house bench, the Clay Yard, the yard well, the Vail wheel-shed and back room (not entered), and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Tarn agreed the thaw early against late so wholly that no argument found a foot yet wanted the late because it puts off the paying, and swept the collector's road without turning and did not turn at a vessel's note, while Berr Clay came up the clay road with his office cord folded under his coat and glaze at one mouth-corner, wiped his thumb twice, read one in twelve, called the levy and took himself out of his sentence with There'll be a slate at the watch and went toward High Kiln with his box locked and the true subtraction inside believing he had done a kindness, and a neighbour at the bucket-round only mentioned a counting room shut in the war with the damper closed and its men dead of the breath, a mention and not a telling with no third coming, while Jorra Vail came through with grey slate dust past his bent finger, named neither the men who spoke low in the back room nor the room, spoke to the wheel before anyone, put his bent finger into the damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the household straightened around his silence, and read the iron as the bite that holds a vessel whole and takes a hand off a man with no Grathic word for both, and at dusk the private file was unchanged and the fire kept four, four and a gap with its payer unnamed for a hundred and two days, the count standing at one hundred and two unspent.
Day 103 | Seed 14, Y.H. 813 (Fourthday) | the Fishstairs below the north bridge, House Silt three doors down, the Clay Yard, the loaf shelf and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella set the basket at the end of the fish-trade shade where no slate lay and gave the instruction to the wool and the water and never to the basket, while Mira Silt worked the eels with silt dried pale in her neck's hollow, answered whether the run would be thin with her own answer, moved eel-bones while deciding whether to lie and, when a buyer named a figure, put her thumb to the grey cord showing a second cord twisted twice beneath it that was never carried to Nine Pots or lifted at a door, neither knot counted, her board lying beside Orra Weir's without touching, the steps coming back four, four and a landing out of line with the soft-cuffed clerk still set for an uncalled measure and no sixth sighting of the neat dark hand, and three doors down Alda Reed laid her birth-slates before turning to the fold, pressed the wool a breath too long, crossed two fingers at the birth-flower unknowing and covered the sign with a dead woman's date, said Warm and breathing which was exact and not true, touched the tin in her bodice without opening it and left her sixth page face-down toward a floorboard with six unread and no seventh, the pewter tin above the empty bed kept shut, while below the sill a woman wound wool round a child's dark foot and called it the cold while the yard called it weather, and at dusk the boy, one hundred and three days old and told by nobody, checked his file unchanged with six hearings and no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and not paid and the word forty unspoken, the shelf keeping two unnamed gaps and the fold one crust beside its empty place and no third in rooms that did not touch, and Sella set him on his bed under the roof with the fold right and the thread frozen, telling the fire it was the thaw as it answered four low pulls and a gap with its payer unnamed for a hundred and three days, the river's note and the fire's gap in two places as his hand went up an inch toward the water and came down, the count standing at one hundred and three unspent.
Day 104 | Seed 15, Y.H. 813 (Fifthday) | the worked strips east of Low Kiln in the barley fields, the Clay Yard, the loaf shelf in the Vail kiln-house and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The hands the strips were said to want went into the worked brown ground at last, counted in glances and handfuls and never in a figure, as a woman let seed out of a fist and would not say what it held and a man with a barrow shaped the number of hands and thought better of it so the figure went from the strip to his teeth and back, while Berr Clay came down off the north road with his office cord folded, wiped his thumb twice, read one in twelve as the notice says, said a number goes into a slate not into a strip, took himself out of his own sentence and went off toward High Kiln with his box locked and the true subtraction inside, and Sella gave the bucket and the broom their instructions before anybody and never the basket, moved her lips along the loaf row and gave nobody a figure, went around every open slate and the space under the rack at a half-pace and said the house where another woman would have said I, and at the loaf shelf a neighbour reached past the bare board before looking at the boy and said the old place at the row's end had come in with the planting and went with her pot without ever seeing the empty place at his ear, T097 advanced and not paid, and at dusk the boy, one hundred and four days old and told by nobody, set the direction carried for a month beside the number the yard would not give and kept them on two places, his hand going up an inch toward the worked strip and coming down, the count standing at one hundred and four unspent.
Day 105 | Seed 16, Y.H. 813 (Sixthday) | the Vail wheel-shed and its finger's-width-open back room (not entered), the damper, the yard well, the collector's road, the Clay Yard and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Men came in before first grey and spoke low in the back room and Jorra Vail gave them no name and did not say the room was cold, coming through with fine grey slate dust worked into both forearms past the bent little finger, not bone-coloured slip, keeping dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray, speaking to the wheel before anyone and telling a pot it would do, knowing its full note before it left the wheel and would not say so before a High Kiln kiln-master, and putting his bent finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the household straightened around his silence the way cloth straightens when a weight is lifted, while the boy read the iron as the bite that holds a vessel whole and can take the hand off a man with no Grathic word for both mercies and filed it as an object joined to no kiln, and at the well Tarn took the broom only after Sella had given it its instruction, said Yes house to both an early and a late thaw while wanting the late because it puts off the paying, his chest tight since the dark with the breath he had decided not to spend, his knife on its sill with six years worn almost flat and the seventh nearly gone and still held, to the kindling Six counted. The next I don't count, ash worked into his knuckle creases and the pale clip-ring at his left wrist holding the little light, his broom going over the collector's road over and over without once turning along it and no collector coming, the held breath pressed to its edge without giving, as at dusk the file stood unchanged with six hearings and no seventh, one crust stiff in the fold, the new grey unswept and the two sounds in two places, the count at one hundred and five unspent.

Day 107 | Seed 18, Y.H. 813 (Eighthday) | the wheel-shed kindling sill, the yard well, the collector's road, the worked strips east of Low Kiln, House Silt three doors down, the loaf shelf under the Vail roof and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Tarn's small bench knife lay on its sill with six years worn almost flat by his thumb and the seventh nearly gone and still held, the one entry in this house the house cannot check, and to the kindling he said *Six counted. The next I don't count* while his chest stayed tight with a breath he had decided not to spend, his broom going over the road a house sends a man down when it wants a collector over and over without once turning along it and no collector coming, while the planting went on in the strips in glances and handfuls and never a figure; three doors down Alda Reed laid her birth-slates on the bench before turning to the fold, pressed the wool a breath too long, crossed two fingers at the birth-flower unknowing and covered the sign with a dead woman's date, said *Warm and breathing* which was exact and not true, left her sixth slate face-down with six pages unread and the tin in her bodice shut; at the loaf shelf a second neighbour named the old gap as a thing the planting brought in and went with her pot without seeing the empty place at his ear, two tellings of one absence kept on two places with no third and no finger laid across, T097 advanced and not paid; under the roof the boy, one hundred and seven days old and told by nobody, read the held breath as a breath held against a clamp, exact and worth nothing, and added one hundred and seven to a count nobody could check.
