Day 110 | Seed 21, Y.H. 813 (Firstday) | the edge of the worked strips east of Low Kiln, the loaf shelf in the Vail kiln-house, the Clay Yard and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The planting entered its last tend with the hands in the strips counted in glances and handfuls and never a figure, a man with a barrow shaping a number and thinking better of it, while Sella Vail carried the boy at the half-pace from every open slate, gave the bucket, broom, pot and fire their instructions before anybody and never the basket, and at the loaf shelf a neighbour reached past a bare board before her eyes found the child, paused over the palm's breadth that came in on Seed 6, closed on the loaf beyond and went off saying in one breath that the old space at the row's end came in with the planting and this was the price of a wet thing set down wrong, never seeing the two were one absence or the empty place at his ear, T097 advanced and not paid, Sella moving her lips along the row without a figure and leaving the widow's mark untouched, and at the going of the light he turned out his file and found six hearings with no seventh, three near-full answers advanced, the word forty unspoken with its readable turn, four low things with the river's note a fifth, the two shut-room tellings joined to no kiln, the damper's bite filed as an object and the loan uncalled, one brown-edged crust stiff in the fold, the shelf's two gaps in a room that did not touch it, the knife at the edge of its sill with the seventh still held, his hand going up an inch toward the water and coming down as the count stood at one hundred and ten, exact, unspent and spoken by nobody.
Day 111 | Seed 22, Y.H. 813 (Secondday) | the Vail wheel-shed and its finger's-width-open back room (not entered), the damper, the yard well, the clay road toward High Kiln and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Men came up before first grey and spoke low in the back room and Jorra Vail gave them no name, coming through with fine grey slate dust ground into both forearms past the bent finger, not bone-coloured slip, speaking to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), telling a pot *You'll do* and putting that finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the household straightened around his silence, the iron taking the cold out of the fingertip and giving it back a little warmer, while Tarn took the broom only after it was instructed and his stroke checked half a beat behind Jorra's standing finger before going on without a word or a turn of the head, the two silences standing in one room with no hand joining them and the loan uncalled, at the well Sella sending the woman with wet sleeves to ask the pot what a wet thing costs because *the house has said it*, Berr Clay wiping his thumb twice on his apron, reading one in twelve and saying a number goes into a slate, not into a strip, then going off up the clay road with the box locked and the true subtraction inside believing he had done a kindness, a man putting his fist back in his sleeve so the figure arrived nowhere, Tarn saying *Yes, house* to an early thaw and a late while wanting the late because it puts off the paying, his chest tight and his breath unspent, and at going of light the count standing at one hundred and eleven, exact and unspent.
Day 112 | Seed 23, Y.H. 813 (Thirdday) | the Fishstairs below the north bridge and the outer boards of the fish-trade shade, House Silt three doors down, the loaf shelf in the Vail kiln-house and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella set the basket at the end of the shade where no slate lay, gave the instruction to the wool and the running water and never to the basket, and stood a half-pace from every open slate, while Mira Silt answered whether the run would be thin with her own answer (*Thin? Who's to say thin? It comes when it comes. Ask the river*), moved eel-bones against her thumb while deciding and put it under her apron to the grey cord when a buyer named a figure, shifting a creel to show the second cord twisted twice beneath it, neither knot counted, her board beside Orra Weir's knot-string and not touching it, the soft-cuffed clerk still set at the out-of-line landing with his slate for a measure nobody called and no sixth sighting, no foundling turning late and no loaf passing, and at midday three doors down Alda Reed laid her birth-slates on the bench before turning to the fold, pressed the wool a breath too long, crossed two fingers at the birth-flower before she knew it and covered the sign with a dead woman's date, said *Warm and breathing* which was exact and not true, went on with dates after the question stopped, touched the tin in her bodice without opening it and left her sixth slate face-down with six pages unread and no seventh while the pewter tin above the board-bed kept its pale fold and the grey wool below only the hollow of the pale stone, nobody saying either was gone, and under the roof at going of light the boy added what the day gave without entering any of it, the file unchanged, one crust stiff in the fold, the shelf's two gaps in the room that did not touch it, the cold foot still the cold, the widow's mark untouched, the carried fire missing its fifth breath with its payer unnamed for a hundred and twelve days, the river's note through the gap and the kiln's breath through the floor in two places that did not meet, his hand going up an inch toward the water and coming down as the count stood at one hundred and twelve, exact, unspent and spoken by nobody.
Day 113 | Seed 24, Y.H. 813 (Fourthday) | the worked strips east of Low Kiln, the loaf shelf and yard well in the Vail kiln-house, and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The last tend held with the seed up and the hands counted in glances and handfuls and never a figure, a man brought a number up out of a fist toward the well, looked at it and put it back in his sleeve so it arrived nowhere, Sella gave the bucket, broom, pot and fire their instructions before anybody and never the basket and sent a woman with wet sleeves to ask the pot what a wet thing costs because *the house has said it*, another neighbour at the loaf shelf reached past a bare board and in one breath laid the old gap at the row's end and the empty place at the boy's ear under one cause without seeing what she had done, the boy set her saying beside the shelf's two gaps and the fold's crust and its empty place on three places that did not touch, and at going of light the file was unchanged and one hundred and thirteen stood unspent, spoken by nobody.
Day 114 | Seed 25, Y.H. 813 (Fifthday) | the Vail wheel-shed and its finger's-width-open back room (not entered), the damper, the flagstones, House Silt three doors down and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Men came up before first grey and spoke low in the back room and Jorra gave them no name, came through with fine grey slate dust worked into both forearms past the bent finger, spoke to the wheel before anyone and told a pot it would do, and put that finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the household straightened around his silence, and this time Tarn's stroke went on over the flagstones without the half-check while the stones kept the place where it had stopped, neither man naming the other's silence and the loan uncalled, while three doors down Alda laid her birth-slates before the fold, crossed two fingers at the birth-flower before she knew it, covered the sign with a dead woman's date, said warm and breathing which was exact and not true, and left her sixth page unwritten with the pewter tin and the little tin shut, and at going of light one hundred and fourteen stood unspent.
Day 115 | Seed 26, Y.H. 813 (Sixthday) | the fish-trade shade below the north bridge, the out-of-line landing at the Fishstairs, the Cross and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella set the basket where no slate lay and gave the instruction to the wool and the running water and never to the basket, Mira answered whether the run would be thin with her own answer and put her thumb under her apron to the grey cord when a buyer named a figure, a shift of a creel showing the second cord twisted twice beneath it with neither knot counted and her board beside Orra's not touching, the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set at the landing for a measure nobody called, at the Cross a soft-handed man from Slate Row handed a figure to a slate held at the edge where nobody else could see it and rested his thumb on the corner with no sixth sighting and no sale, and at going of light the boy turned out his unchanged file, added the day's six objects without entering any of them, and held one hundred and fifteen exact and unspent as his hand went up an inch toward the water and came down.
Day 116 | Seed 27, Y.H. 813 (Seventhday) | the worked strips east of Low Kiln, the loaf shelf in the Vail kiln-house and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The last hands came out of the worked strips with the seed up, counted in glances and handfuls and never a figure --- two fingers lifted with chaff in their creases, a barrow-man shaping a number with his mouth and thinking better of it --- and one hand that had gone in on Seed 15 did not come out with the rest, so the yard went around the empty place in the line with a look and no number, T098 planted with no name, no cause, no body and no payment, while Sella Vail carried the boy at the edge of the work at her half-pace from every open slate, gave the instruction to the wool and the river wind and never to the basket and said the house where another woman would have said I, the boy reading the going-around as a chart closed with a neat line over a note nobody had opened and setting the field's empty place on a place of its own, and at the loaf shelf a neighbour reached past the bare board and called the old gap a planting and the new palm's breadth the price of a wet thing set down wrong without seeing she had joined two rooms, while Sella glanced once at the clay jar and did not touch the widow's mark, and at the going of the light the boy found his file unchanged, added the day's two objects on places of their own and held one hundred and sixteen unspent.
Day 117 | Seed 28, Y.H. 813 (Eighthday) | the Vail wheel-shed and the finger's-width-open back room (not entered), the damper, the flagstones, the stalls beyond the Cross and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Before first grey men came up the Clay Yard with their collars high and spoke low in the back room and Jorra Vail gave them no name and did not say the room was cold, coming through with fine grey slate dust ground into both forearms past the bent finger, not bone-coloured slip, keeping dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and asking neither which slate was broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, speaking to the wheel before anyone and telling a pot it would do and withholding its full note from a High Kiln kiln-master, and putting that finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the household straightened around his silence while the boy read one piece of iron as the bite that holds a vessel whole and the bite that takes a hand off a man with no Grathic word for both, and Tarn took the broom only after it had been instructed, his seventh year pressed to the sill and still held, the breath from Seed 16 unspent with a small interval at the top of each reach, his stroke going on over the flagstones without the half-check while the stones kept the shallow bright patch where it had stopped and neither man named the other's silence and the loan stayed uncalled, while across the market a silent woman with lead-glaze green at one corner of her mouth stood looking at pots and the boy filed the colour beside two other greens without a name, a number going from a fist to a mouth to a shoulder and arriving nowhere, and at the going of the light one hundred and seventeen stood unspent with he's not Vail sealed and the promise of the foundling's name neither given nor withdrawn.
Day 118 | Seed 29, Y.H. 813 (Ninthday) | the yard well, the loaf shelf in the Vail kiln-house, the Fishstairs below the north bridge, House Silt three doors down and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | A neighbour come up from the strips asked what the planting cost now the hands were out of it and Sella glanced at the clay jar and sent her to the pot with Ask the pot what a wet thing costs set down there, the house has said it, so the answer went off unanswered, and at the loaf shelf a second neighbour reached for the bare board before her eyes found the boy and said in one breath that the old gap came in with the planting and the empty place at his ear was a child's appetite, laying two rooms under one cause without seeing it, which the boy filed beside the Seed 24 saying and the earlier tellings on two places with no joining made his own, T097 advanced and not paid with no third gap and no fourth likeness, while at dawn Mira Silt answered whether the run would be thin with her own answer and put her thumb under her apron to the grey cord when a buyer named a figure, showing the second cord twisted twice beneath it with neither knot counted and her board beside Orra Weir's and not touching, and three doors down Alda Reed laid her birth-slates before the fold, crossed two fingers at the birth-flower before she knew it, covered the sign with a dead woman's date, said Warm and breathing which was exact and not true, and left her sixth narrow slate turned down with no seventh, the pewter tin and the little tin shut, while below the sill a child's foot stayed dark in three turns of wool and at the going of the light one hundred and eighteen stood unspent.
Day 119 | Seed 30, Y.H. 813 (Tenthday, Account-day and the last market of Seed) | the Cross reading block and moot-post's shade, the worked strips east of Low Kiln, the Fishstairs out-of-line landing and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The planting month ended and Reed came on with one place in the returning line still gone around with a look and no figure, T098 carried and unpaid, while Berr Clay came up to the block short with the red cord of his office folded under his coat and green at one mouth-corner, wiped his thumb twice before touching nothing, held a narrow slate unopened to his forearm, read one in twelve as the notice says, called the levy and said it was not called by him, and told the man who brought a number up out of a fist that a number goes into a slate not into a strip and your house never I, taking himself out of his own sentence so that the figure read was not the figure paid and did not know the hand that had not come back, while the bell went four four and a gap and nobody crossed himself, the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set at the out-of-line landing for a measure nobody called and the market walked over the missing step carefully without making a fall of it, and under the roof at the going of the light the boy found his file unchanged with six hearings and no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, the word forty unspoken, one crust and two shelf gaps in rooms that did not touch, the held breath unspent and the loan uncalled, added the day's objects on places of their own, and held one hundred and nineteen exact and unspent as his hand went up an inch toward the water and came down with nothing joined, named, paid or ended.
Day 120 | Reed 1, Y.H. 813 (Firstday) | the yard at Low Kiln, the edge of the worked strips east of it, the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Reed came on without a boundary drawn as the wind off the Ilm brought wet mud and cut grass downriver and the yard turned toward the reeds, the work spoken of as a direction and a going-downriver and never as a figure with a bundle judged by the arm that carried it, while a woman come up from the strips asked what the reeds would cost now the hands were out of them and Sella Vail glanced once at the clay jar and sent her to the pot with Ask the pot what a wet thing costs set down there, the house has said it, and the yard went around the clean dark line, the new finer grey called the thaw, the space beneath the lowest rack where the broken birth-slates lay entered by nobody and the place in the worked strip where a hand had gone in with the seed and had not come out with the rest, with a look and a wide step and no number (T098 carried, not paid), and under the roof Sella instructed the bucket, broom, pot and fire before anybody and never the basket, moved her lips along the loaf row without a figure, set the boy, one hundred and twenty days old, on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread frozen and unwiped, told the fire it was the thaw, turned the coals with the back of a broken pot and said The house will keep it, while he read the field's going-around as a chart closed over an unopened note, exact, complete and worth nothing, and kept six hearings with no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, the told bell beside the heard bell and the word forty unspoken as the river's note came through the finger's-width gap and the fire's four, four and a gap came through the floor in two places that did not meet.
Day 121 | Reed 2, Y.H. 813 (Secondday) | the Clay Yard, the Vail wheel-shed and its finger's-width-open back room (not entered), the damper, the flagstones, the stalls beyond the Cross, the out-of-line landing below the north bridge and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Before first grey men came up the Clay Yard with their collars high and spoke low in the back room and Jorra Vail gave them no name and did not say the room was cold, coming through with grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent little finger, speaking to the wheel before anyone and telling a pot You'll do while withholding its full note from a High Kiln kiln-master, then putting that finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the household straightened around his silence, while Tarn took the broom only after it had been instructed, his seventh year pressed past the planting's end and still held with six years in a worn thumb-mark and the seventh nearly gone, the breath from Seed 16 unspent at the top of each reach and his stroke going on over the flagstones past the shallow bright patch without the half-check as neither man named the other's silence and the loan stayed uncalled, and at the stalls beyond the Cross the soft-cuffed clerk sat at the out-of-line landing with his cuff grey from washing and his slate to his forearm for a measure nobody called, hand to slate before eyes to stalls, while at the market's edge a foundling in cast-offs with silt pale in his wrists and a green sliver on a string at his throat turned his head half a beat after the boy's, after the knuckle and after the note, stopped in the middle of the turn, rubbed the sliver twice, opened his mouth on the tail of a sentence and let nothing out that any clerk could take down, no loaf passing and no slate seen, and at going of light one hundred and twenty-one stood unspent with nothing joined, named, paid or ended.
Day 122 | Reed 3, Y.H. 813 (Thirdday) | the yard and worked strips at Low Kiln, the road downriver and the plank walk at the Weir, the outer boards of the fish-trade shade, the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The first bundles came in before the light had settled, green at the broken ends and grey at the frost-bitten tips, laid in the ruts and judged by the arm that carried them while a reed-cutter told a woman who asked about the hands that cut them that a bundle is counted in the arm that carries it and went past with her question unanswered, and Sella Vail sent a man from the sluice who asked what the reed work would want before the week was out to Ask it, it'll tell you when it's had enough, the house has said it, before carrying the boy downriver to the Weir where the traps stood with their mouths upriver, a man walked the boards at dusk with his collar up and nobody spoke his name, and a reed-cutter turned a length of cord once on his thumb so the knots stood in the small light, tied one low with teeth and thumb and put it back inside his coat, saying You can ask the cord to a weir woman who could not read it and No more can the Court, so that T099 was planted, a third private record carried and not counted and held on a place of its own beside Mira Silt's grey cord and second twist, Orra Weir's knot-string, Alda Reed's six hidden pages and Dessa Silt's pewter tin, touching none of them and joined to no figure, while Mira worked the eels with slime grey on her sleeves and answered a thin run with Ask the river, her thumb resting longer than the figure required on the cord at her waist, the second twist beneath it unlifted, her board lying beside Orra's knot-string and not touching it and neither knot counted, and at going of light Sella set the boy on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread frozen and unwiped, told the fire it was the thaw and said The house will keep it, and he found the new cord held beside the older objects, all unread and unpaid, with one hundred and twenty-two exact and unspent as the river went over water beyond the gap and the fire missed its fifth breath under the floor.
Day 123 | Reed 4, Y.H. 813 (Fourthday) | the yard at Low Kiln, the edge of the worked strips, the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The reeds went into the beds as the seed had gone into the strips, green at the broken ends and grey at the frost-bitten tips, judged by the arm that carried them and never by a figure, while a woman who asked of the hands that cut them went unanswered and a man who asked what the beds would want was answered with the weather and the river; the yard went around the clean dark line, the finer grey named the thaw, the space beneath the lowest rack and the place where a hand gone in on Seed 15 had not come out on Seed 27 with a look and a wide step and no number (T098 carried, not paid), and Sella Vail sent the woman who asked what the reeds cost to ask the pot what a wet thing cost set down there, the house has said it, before carrying the boy, one hundred and twenty-three days old, at the half-pace from every open slate, giving the instruction to the wool and the wind and never the basket; he read the going-around as a chart closed over a note nobody had opened, exact, complete, worth nothing, while under the roof she instructed bucket, broom, pot and fire before anybody, kept the shelf's two gaps and the fold's one crust and its empty place in rooms that did not touch, told the fire it was the thaw and said The house will keep it, and his file stood with six hearings and no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, the told bell beside the heard bell and the word forty unspoken as river, fire and floorboards kept three counts in three places that did not become one.
Day 124 | Reed 5, Y.H. 813 (Fifthday) | the Clay Yard, the Vail wheel-shed and its finger's-width-open back room (not entered), the damper, the flagstones, the hearth and kindling under the Vail roof and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Before first grey men came up the Clay Yard and spoke low in the back room and Jorra Vail gave them no name and did not say the room was cold, coming through with grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent finger, speaking to the wheel, telling a pot You'll do and withholding its full note from a High Kiln kiln-master, and putting that finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the household straightened around his silence, while the boy read the iron as the bite that holds a vessel whole and takes a hand off a man with no Grathic word for both and filed it as an object joined to no kiln, and Tarn's seventh year was pressed past the third tend of the planting and still held with six years in a worn thumb-mark and the seventh where the house could not check it, his stroke going on over the flagstones without the half-check while the stones kept the shallow bright patch and neither man named the other's silence; that night at the hearth he brought the sentence about an ear worth more than the debt to the threshold of the word that would call the loan and stopped there with no figure and no name, the loan uncalled, drew the breath he had held since Seed 16 and did not spend it, and the boy filed the stopped sentence on the interval tray beside the half-beat, the unchecking stroke and his own held breath as an interval and not a payment while the kindling kept four, four and an empty fifth and something must give at the end of the seventh year and had not given.
Day 125 | Reed 6, Y.H. 813 (Sixthday) | the road downriver to the Weir, the reed beds, the plank walk and flat blessing stone, the stalls beyond the Cross, the out-of-line landing below the north bridge, the clay road, the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella Vail carried the boy downriver to the Weir and set him at the edge of the work where no slate lay, and a reed-cutter's tally went past again in a short cord gone grey with handling, straightened on the thumb with one knot tied low by teeth and thumb and put back inside the coat, saying You can ask the cord and No more can the Court with its knots unread and counted by nobody, held beside Mira Silt's grey cord and second twist, Orra Weir's knot-string, Alda Reed's six pages and Dessa Silt's pewter tin (T099 carried, not paid), while Orra gave a date where a levy figure was asked and got no answer from the basket with the word forty unspoken, Mira answered a thin run with Ask the river and put her thumb to the grey cord longer than the figure required without looking into the basket, the soft-cuffed clerk sat at the out-of-line landing with hand to slate before eyes to stalls while the market sold over him, and Berr Clay came up short with the green of tasted glaze at his mouth, wiped his thumb twice, read one in twelve as the notice says, said a number goes into a slate not into a bundle and took himself out of his sentence with your house, never I, going off with the box locked and the true subtraction inside; at the going of the light Sella carried him back up the clay road, set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread frozen, told the fire it was the thaw and said The house will keep it, while the river's note came through the finger's-width gap and the carried fire missed its fifth breath under the floor with its payer unnamed for a hundred and twenty-five days and the boards kept four nails and a knot, three counts in three places that did not become one, and his file held six hearings with no seventh, three answers unpaid and the near-saying on the interval tray as he, one hundred and twenty-five days old and told by nobody, reached an inch toward the water and came down.
Day 126 | Reed 7, Y.H. 813 (Seventhday) | the Clay Yard and the edge of the worked strips, the yard well, the stalls beyond the Cross and the moot-post's shade, the out-of-line landing below the north bridge, the loaf shelf and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The reeds stood in rows at last in the ruts where the barley had stood a month before, green at the broken ends and pale at the frost-bitten tips, judged by the arm that carried them and never by a figure, a cutter telling a girl with a creel *Ask the arm that carried it. It'll tell you*, while Sella Vail carried the boy, one hundred and twenty-six days old and told by nobody, at the half-pace from every open slate and sent a woman who asked what the reeds cost to the pot with *the house has said it* and a man who asked what the beds would want to the weather and the river, the woman shutting two fingers out of a fist as she went; the yard went around the clean dark line, the finer grey called the thaw, the space beneath the lowest rack where the broken birth-slates lay entered by nobody and the place in the strip where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out on Seed 27 with a look and a wide step and no number (T098 carried, not paid), while Tarn took the broom only after it was instructed, said *Yes, house* to an early and a late thaw and kept wanting the late because it puts off the paying with the seventh year still held and the near-saying left on the tray; at the Cross a silent woman turned a cup a quarter and set it back where it stood with lead-glaze green at one corner of her mouth, a pale cord-line on the back of her hand and clay under her nails, and went off with the light --- the third green at a mouth and the fourth shape with Berr's, held on a place of its own with no name and no cause (T050/T051 advanced) --- while the bell went four, four and a gap with nobody crossing himself, the soft-cuffed clerk held his slate to his forearm at the landing with the market selling over him, a seller wiped his thumb once after touching fish and a girl with a creel took the out-of-line landing sideways without anybody making a fall of it, and at dusk Sella set the boy on his bed with one stiff crust in the fold, told the fire it was the thaw and said *The house will keep it* as the river's note, the fire's missing fifth breath and the boards' thumb-width knot kept three counts in three places that did not become one and one hundred and twenty-six stood unspent.
Day 127 | Reed 8, Y.H. 813 (Eighthday) | the Clay Yard, the Vail wheel-shed and its finger's-width-open back room (not entered), the damper, the flagstones, the clay road and market edge, the hearth and kindling and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Three or four unnamed men came up before first grey and spoke low in the back room and Jorra Vail gave them no name and did not say the room was cold, coming through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent finger, speaking to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), telling a pot *You'll do* and withholding its full note from a High Kiln kiln-master who would call it early and make something of it, then putting that finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the household straightened around his silence while the boy read one piece of iron as the bite that holds a vessel whole and the bite that takes a hand off a man with no Grathic word for both and no third telling of the shut room came; Tarn took the broom only after it had been instructed and went on over the flagstones without the half-check while the stones kept the shallow bright patch and neither man named the other's silence; Berr Clay came up at the market's edge with his office cord folded under his coat and green at his mouth, wiped his thumb twice before touching nothing, held a narrow slate unopened to his forearm, read *one in twelve, as the notice says*, called the levy and said *It is not called by me* and *your house*, never *I*, going off with the box locked and the true subtraction inside while the figure he read did not know the hand that had not come back; that evening the sentence about an ear worth more than the debt came a second time to the threshold of the word that would call the loan --- with the slate at the watch, the name and number and the collector coming down the road --- and stopped there with no figure and no name, Tarn drawing the breath he did not spend and going on with the broom, and the boy filed it on the interval tray as a second interval and not a second payment while the loan stayed uncalled and the seventh year had not given, and at the going of the light one hundred and twenty-seven stood unspent with the fire's payer unnamed for a hundred and twenty-seven days.
Day 128 | Reed 9, Y.H. 813 (Ninthday) | the road downriver, the Weir plank walk, the flat blessing stone and lower boards, the fish-trade shade and the out-of-line landing at the Fishstairs, House Silt three doors down, the loaf shelf and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella carried the boy downriver and set him at the edge of the work where no slate lay while a reed-cutter turned a short grey cord once on his thumb at the blessing stone, tied one low with teeth and a pull of the thumb and put it back inside his coat, saying *You can ask the cord* and *No more can the Court* with its knots unread and no figure given (T099 carried, not paid); Orra Weir pulled one knot down with her thumb and gave a date --- *The ninth of Ilmen*, the collector's boat with six oars and one man at the stern who never touched them --- where a figure was asked, saying the river takes what the Court does not, her knot-string lying beside Mira's board and not touching it with the forty-first double turn legible and the word forty unspoken, while Mira answered a thin run with *Ask the river*, moved eel-bones while deciding whether to lie and kept her thumb under her apron to the grey cord longer than the figure required, a shifting creel showing the second twist twisted twice that was never carried to Nine Pots or lifted at a door, neither knot counted and the thin child's knot untouched, and did not look into the basket; the soft-cuffed clerk was still set at the landing for a measure nobody called, and three doors down Alda Reed laid her birth-slates before the fold, crossed two fingers at the birth-flower unknowing, covered the sign with the date she gave Dessa and said *Warm and breathing, that's all I've set down* --- exact and not true over a woman more mornings dead than any living person keeps count of --- touching the little tin in her bodice without opening it and leaving six pages turned toward a floorboard with no seventh and both tins shut; at dusk Sella set him on his bed under the roof, told the fire it was the thaw and went to her own bed, and he, one hundred and twenty-eight days old and told by nobody, found every object in the file in its place with the second stopping on the interval tray, six hearings and no seventh, three answers unpaid, the word forty unspoken and the cold foot still the cold, as the river's note came through the finger's-width gap, the fire missed its fifth breath with its payer unnamed for a hundred and twenty-eight days and the boards kept a knot the width of a thumb, three counts in three places that did not become one, his hand going up an inch toward the water and coming down with the count unspent.
Day 129 | Reed 10, Y.H. 813 (Tenthday, the first Account-day of Reed) | the Cross reading block and the moot-post's shade, the stalls beyond, the out-of-line landing below the north bridge, the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf and hearth, the clay road and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | On the first Account-day of Reed Sella Vail carried the boy at the half-pace into the moot-post's shade where no slate lay and stood a half-pace off while two hundred leaned over the block and eight pigs went over it with an assessor's hand on the slate before his eyes went to the rail; Berr Clay came up short with his office cord folded under his coat and green glaze at one mouth-corner, wiped his thumb twice before touching nothing, passed a finger over his narrow slate, read *one in twelve, as the notice says*, called the levy and said it was not called by him, and to the man who brought a number up out of a fist said there would be a slate at the watch --- *your house*, never *I* --- so the figure went into the air while the true subtraction stayed in the locked box up the clay road; the bell went four, four and a gap with nobody crossing himself and the heard bell was kept beside the told bell, the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set below the bridge for a measure nobody called with hand to slate before eyes to stalls and the market sold over him, and a man from the strips went around the place in the returning line where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out on Seed 27 with a look and no number (T098 carried, not paid); back under the Vail roof at midday Sella set the basket low near the bench leg at the angle where the underside of the loaf shelf becomes a roof, and from that angle the boy first saw the short charcoal marks in clusters on the board's underside, some pressed hard, some faded nearly into the grain, a keeping put where nobody thought to look, which he did not read, count, copy or lay beside the two gaps above, the crust in the fold or any other private record but set on a place of its own, advanced and unpaid (T100 planted), while a neighbour who asked what the row would want before the tend was out was sent to the pot with *the house has said it* and carried the unanswered thing away level; at dusk Sella carried him up the clay road, went around the rack space, set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread frozen, told the fire it was the thaw and said *The house will keep it*, and the river's note, the fire's silent fifth and the boards' knot were three counts in three places that did not become one as one hundred and twenty-nine stood exact, unspent and spoken by nobody.
Day 130 | Reed 11, Y.H. 813 (Firstday) | the Clay Yard, the Vail wheel-shed and its finger's-width-open back room (not entered), the damper, the flagstones, the fish-trade shade and out-of-line landing at the Fishstairs, below the sill, the hearth and kindling under the Vail roof and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Before first grey three or four unnamed men came up the Clay Yard with their collars high and spoke low in the back room, which kept its finger's width of open, and Jorra Vail gave them no name and did not say the room was cold, coming through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent little finger, not bone-coloured slip, keeping dusty and clean forearms on one tray and asking neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate; he spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), told a pot *You'll do*, knew its full note before it left the wheel and withheld it from a High Kiln kiln-master who would call it early, and put his bent finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the household straightened around his silence, while the boy read one piece of iron as the bite that holds a vessel whole and the bite that takes a hand off a man with no Grathic word for both and filed it as an object joined to no kiln, and no third telling of the shut counting room came; Tarn took the broom only after it had been instructed, said *Yes, house* to an early thaw and a late while wanting the late because it puts off the paying, his seventh year pressed past another tend with six years in a worn thumb-mark and the seventh nearly gone, and his stroke went on over the flagstones without the half-check while the stones kept the shallow bright patch and neither man named the other's silence and the loan stayed uncalled; in the afternoon at the Fishstairs the soft-cuffed clerk was still set for a measure nobody called and entered a buyer's figure before his eyes found the man who had spoken it, while below the sill a woman wound a child's dark foot in three turns of wool and called it the cold and a neighbour at the tubs called the water weather, the boy reading the extremity going before the core and the afterdrop waiting on a warming nobody would perform and buying nothing with it; at the hearth, coals banked and the small knife set back where it could be checked, Tarn told the kindling *Six counted. The next I don't count* and the sentence about an ear worth more than the debt came a third time to the word that would call the loan and stopped with no figure and no name, so he drew the breath and did not spend it, said *Yes, house* to the fire and went on with the broom, Sella within hearing asking nothing and Jorra not asking about the ear while Tarn did not ask about the room, and the boy laid the third stopping on the interval tray beside the first two as an object and not a third payment or a doctrine, with the loan uncalled, the seventh year not given and one hundred and thirty exact and unspent as the carried fire missed its fifth breath with its payer unnamed for a hundred and thirty days.
Day 131 | Reed 12, Y.H. 813 (Secondday) | the river road downriver, the Weir plank walk, the flat blessing stone and lower boards, House Silt three doors down, the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf and hearth, the clay road and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella carried the boy downriver at the half-pace to the Weir, where salt stood white on the posts and the traps had their mouths upriver, and the reed work went on in armfuls judged by the carrying arm and by nothing set on a slate, a cutter telling a girl with a creel *Lift it. Your arm'll tell you. It'll tell you what went into it, and that's the whole of what it knows*; the reed-cutter came along the boards with a bundle and the grey cord inside his coat gone grey with the oil of a working thumb, set the bundle on the blessing stone, straightened the cord, turned it so the knots stood, tied one low with his teeth and a pull of the thumb, bit the end off and put it back inside his coat, saying *You can ask the cord* and *No more can the Court* with no figure, knot, name or joining (T099 carried, not paid), while Orra Weir at the lower boards pulled a knot down and gave a date --- the ninth of Ilmen, the collector's boat with six oars and the man at the stern who never touched them --- saying *It's a date. The river takes what the Court doesn't* with the forty-first double turn legible and the word forty unspoken, and Mira Silt worked the eels with slime dried grey on her sleeves and silt pale in her neck's hollow, answered a thin run with her own answer, moved eel-bones while deciding whether to lie and put her thumb under her apron to the grey cord when a buyer named a figure, the second twist twisted twice beneath it never carried to Nine Pots or lifted at a door, neither knot counted, the thin child's knot untouched and her board lying beside Orra's without touching; at midday Sella set the basket just inside the finger's-width-open door of House Silt and stood a half-pace off, and Alda Reed laid her birth-slates before turning to the fold, pressed the wool a breath too long, crossed two fingers at the red edge of the birth-flower unknowing, covered the sign with the date she gave Dessa, said *Warm and breathing, that's all I've set down* (exact and not true over a woman more mornings dead than any living person keeps count of), touched the tin in her bodice without opening it and left six pages turned toward a floorboard with no seventh while the pewter tin above the abandoned bed kept its pale fold and thumb-bright rub shut; in the afternoon back under the Vail roof Sella reached a finger beneath the loaf shelf where the charcoal tally lay, without looking at it and without a word, and brought it away, and the boy saw the hand go under and come back and made no cause of it (T100 carried, not paid), and at dusk she carried him up, went around the rack space, set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right, left the thread frozen, told the fire it was the thaw and counted under her breath toward no opening, the river's note, the fire's silent fifth with its payer unnamed for a hundred and thirty-one days and the boards' four nails, four, and a knot the width of a thumb standing as three counts in three places that did not become one, his hand going up an inch toward the water and coming down with one hundred and thirty-one exact, unspent and spoken by nobody.
Day 132 | Reed 13, Y.H. 813 (Thirdday) | the Clay Yard, the drying racks and the edge of the worked strips, the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf and hearth, the Weir plank walk, the flat blessing stone and lower boards, the fish-trade shade and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The reed month stood in its middle tend as Sella Vail carried the boy, one hundred and thirty-two days old and four months and twelve days, at the half-pace from every open slate, giving the instruction to the wool and the river wind and never to the basket, going around the clean dark line, the new finer grey called the thaw, the space under the lowest rack where the broken birth-slates lay and the place in the returning line where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out, with a look and a wide step and no number (T098 carried, not paid), while reeds came in armfuls judged by the arm that carried them, a cutter telling a girl with a creel that her arm knew what went into the bundle and that was the whole of it, and a man who asked what the work would want before the tend was out being given the weather and the river; under the loaf shelf Sella reached a finger beneath the board where the charcoal tally lies without looking and without a word and brought it away (T100 carried, not paid), a neighbour with a wet pot was sent to ask the pot what a wet thing costs because the house has said it, and at the Weir a reed-cutter tied one knot with his teeth, bit the end off and put his grey cord back inside his coat, saying *You can ask the cord* and *No more can the Court* (T099 carried, not paid), while Orra Weir gave a date where a figure was asked and Mira Silt answered a thin run with her own answer, moving eel-bones while deciding whether to lie and keeping her thumb under her apron to a grey cord with a second twist twisted twice beneath it, neither knot counted; at dusk Sella set the boy on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread frozen, told the fire it was the thaw and said *The house will keep it*, and the river's note, the carried fire's missing fifth breath with its payer unnamed for a hundred and thirty-two days and the boards' four nails, four, and a knot kept three counts in three places that did not become one as the count stood at one hundred and thirty-two, exact and unspent.
Day 133 | Reed 14, Y.H. 813 (Fourthday) | the Clay Yard, the Vail wheel-shed and its finger's-width-open back room (not entered), the damper, the flagstones, the out-of-line landing below the north bridge, the hearth and kindling under the Vail roof and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Before first grey three or four unnamed men came up through the mud with their collars high and went low-voiced into the room off the shed, whose door kept its finger's width of open, and Sella Vail carried the boy past at the half-pace, gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold, while Jorra Vail came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent finger, kept dusty and clean forearms on one tray, spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), told a pot *You'll do*, withheld its full note from a High Kiln kiln-master who said it was singing before its time and put that finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the household straightened around his silence, the boy reading one piece of iron as the bite that holds a vessel whole and the bite that takes a hand off a man with no Grathic word for both and filing it joined to no kiln with no third telling of the shut room; Tarn took the broom only after it had been instructed, said *Yes, house* to an early thaw and a late and went on wanting the late because it puts off the paying, his seventh year pressed past another tend and still held, the knife at the very edge of its sill with six counted where the house can check and the seventh where it cannot, his stroke going on over the flagstones without the half-check while the stones kept the shallow bright patch and neither man named the other's silence; the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set at the out-of-line landing for a measure nobody called, cuff grey from washing and grey again by midday, hand to slate before eyes to stalls, the market selling over him and a buyer's figure being entered before the buyer was looked at; and at the hearth, with coals banked and the knife set back where it could be checked, Tarn told the kindling *Six counted. The next I don't count*, brought the sentence about an ear under this roof being worth more than the debt that holds it a FOURTH time all the way to the word that would call the loan and stopped there with no figure and no name, drew the breath he did not spend, said *Yes, house* to the fire and went on with the broom, Sella giving the end of her instruction to the pot and asking nothing, Jorra spoke to the wheel and not to the man and not asking about the ear, and the boy filing the fourth stopping on the interval tray beside three others as an interval and not a payment, refusing to let four likenesses stand up and call themselves a doctrine, the loan uncalled, the seventh year not given and the fire's payer unnamed for a hundred and thirty-three days.
Day 134 | Reed 15, Y.H. 813 (Fifthday) | the Cross, the pot board and market stalls, the Vail wheel-shed and back room (not entered), the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf and hearth, the fish-trade shade below the sill, the out-of-line landing below the north bridge and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Frost lay on the slate-posts at the Cross as Sella Vail carried the boy, one hundred and thirty-four days old and four months and fourteen days, up from the river road at the half-pace and gave the instruction to the wool and the river wind and never to the basket, while across the square a silent woman with the sweetish green of lead glaze at one mouth-corner, clay under her nails and a pale line across the back of one hand where something had once been worn tight and long enough to leave its mark, turned a cup a quarter, set it back where it stood and went off with the light --- the FOURTH green at a mouth and the FIFTH shape of the colour counting Berr Clay's glaze at his own mouth, a colour and not a name, never Fenna in the narration (T050/T051 advanced) --- and Berr came up short with his office cord folded under his coat, wiped his thumb twice before touching nothing, paid two marks short for eels, said *A number goes into a slate, not a bundle* and, with *your house*, never *I*, let the low voice about the levy fall on the boards and went toward High Kiln with the box locked and the true subtraction inside; below the sill Mira Silt answered a thin run with her own answer, moved eel-bones while deciding whether to lie and showed the second cord twisted twice beneath her apron when a buyer named a figure, her board beside Orra Weir's knot-string and not touching, while Orra gave a date where a figure was asked and kept the forty-first double turn legible and the word forty unspoken, and a child's foot stayed dark inside three turns of wool, the cold to the woman who wrapped it and weather to the yard, the boy's reading of the extremity going before the core working perfectly and buying nothing (T093/T094/T096 carried); the soft-cuffed clerk was still set at the landing for an uncalled measure, and back under the roof Sella's finger went beneath the board where the charcoal tally lies again without a word, and at dusk she set the boy on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread frozen, told the fire it was the thaw and said *The house will keep it*, so that in the small hours his file held six hearings and no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, four stoppings as objects and not a rule, the charcoal unread, Alda's six pages and both tins and Dessa's pewter tin shut, the word forty unspoken, the cold foot uncured, the clerk still set, the loan uncalled and the seventh year not given, his hand going up an inch toward the river's note and coming down with one hundred and thirty-four exact, unspent and spoken by nobody.
Day 135 | Reed 16, Y.H. 813 (Sixthday) | the Clay Yard, the drying racks and the edge of the worked strips, the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf and hearth, the reed beds and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The reed month went into its last third as Sella Vail carried the boy, one hundred and thirty-five days old and four months and fifteen days, at the half-pace from every open slate, giving the instruction to the wool and the river wind and never to the basket, while reeds came in armfuls judged by the arm that carried them, a cutter telling a girl with a creel *Lift it. Your arm'll tell you what went into it, and that's the whole of what it knows* and a woman at the racks who asked what the hands would be paid being answered with the weather and the river, never a figure, and shutting two fingers out of a fist as she went; the yard went around the clean dark line, the finer grey called the thaw, the space beneath the lowest rack where the broken birth-slates lay and the place in the returning line where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out with a look and a wide step and no number (T098 carried, not paid), and the boy read the carrying arm as a record in muscle that tells what was carried and not what it cost --- exact, complete and worth nothing --- keeping it on a place of its own beside the other absences as Account-day stood four days off at Reed 20; under the roof Sella set the basket low near the bench leg at the angle where the board's underside becomes a roof, moved her lips along the row with its two gaps, glanced once at the clay jar cold under its glaze and reached a finger BENEATH the board where the charcoal tally lies without looking and without a word, held it a breath out and back, brought it away and did not go back to the shelf (T100 carried, not paid), while a neighbour with a wet pot who asked what the row would want was sent to the pot with *Ask the pot what a wet thing costs set down there. The house has said it* and carried the unanswered question away level, the widow's mark untouched because a shelf and an afternoon are not openings; at dusk she set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread frozen and unwiped, told the fire it was the thaw and said *The house will keep it*, and in the small hours his hand went up an inch toward the note of water and came down with one hundred and thirty-five exact and unspent.
Day 136 | Reed 17, Y.H. 813 (Seventhday) | the Clay Yard, the Vail wheel-shed and its finger's-width-open back room (not entered), the damper, the flagstones, the out-of-line landing below the north bridge, the hearth and kindling under the Vail roof and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Before first grey three or four men with their collars high came up the Clay Yard and went low-voiced into the room off the shed, which kept its finger's width of open, and Sella Vail carried the boy past at the half-pace, gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold (T035/T031); Jorra Vail came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent finger, kept dusty and clean forearms on one tray, spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), told a pot *You'll do*, knew its full note three turns before it left the wheel and withheld it from a High Kiln kiln-master who said it was singing before its time, then put that bent finger into the cold iron of the damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the household straightened around his silence, the boy reading one piece of iron as the bite that holds a vessel whole and the bite that can take a hand off a man with no Grathic word for both and no third telling of the shut counting room (T062, T065/T095); Tarn took the broom only after it had been instructed, said *Yes, house* to an early thaw and a late while wanting the late because it puts off the paying, his seventh year pressed nearer its end and still held, the knife at the very edge of its sill with six counted where the house could check and the seventh where it could not, the Seed 16 breath unspent at the top of each reach and his stroke going on over the flagstones past the bright patch without the half-check, neither man naming the other's silence; at the Fishstairs the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set at the out-of-line landing for a measure nobody called, cuff grey from washing and grey again by midday, hand to slate before eyes to stalls, the market selling its reeds over him and a girl with a creel taking the landing sideways without a fall (T090/T080/T083); and at the hearth with coals banked Tarn told the kindling *Six counted. The next I don't count* and brought the sentence about an ear under this roof being worth more than the debt that holds it a **fifth** time all the way to the word that would call the loan, stopped there with no figure and no name, drew the breath he did not spend, said *Yes, house* to the fire and went on with the broom, Sella giving the end of her instruction to the pot and asking nothing, Jorra not asking about the ear and Tarn not asking about the room, while the boy filed the fifth stopping on the interval tray beside Reed 5, 8, 11 and 14 as an interval and not a payment, refusing to let five likenesses stand up and call themselves a doctrine (T036/T076, T061/T082), the carried fire missing its fifth breath with its payer unnamed for one hundred and thirty-six days and the count standing one hundred and thirty-six exact and unspent.
Day 137 | Reed 18, Y.H. 813 (Eighthday) | the river road downriver, the Weir plank walk, the flat blessing stone and lower boards, the Cross, the pot board and market stalls, the fish-trade shade below the sill, the out-of-line landing below the north bridge, the clay road and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The reed month ran into its last days as Sella Vail carried the boy down to the Weir at the half-pace, the work going on in armfuls judged by the arm that carried them and a woman who asked what the cutters' hands would be paid being answered with the weather and the river, never a figure, while a reed-cutter set a bundle on the flat blessing stone, straightened his grey thumb-oiled cord, turned it once so the knots stood, tied one low with his teeth, bit the end off, put it back inside his coat and told the weir woman who asked what it held *You can ask the cord* and *No more can the Court*, going up the boards with its knots unread (T099 carried, not paid); Orra Weir pulled one knot down at the lower boards and gave the ninth of Ilmen, the collector's boat round the bend on the flood with six oars and one man at the stern who never touched them, saying *It's a date. The river takes what the Court doesn't. You can shout at either one. I've tried* and keeping the forty-first double turn legible with its name unspoken, while Mira Silt answered a thin run with her own answer, flicked eel-bones while deciding whether to lie and, when a buyer named a figure, put her thumb under her apron to the grey cord with a second twist twisted twice beneath it, never carried upriver to Nine Pots or lifted at a door, neither knot counted, her board beside Orra's and not touching (T024/T059/T025); at the Cross Berr Clay came up short with his office cord folded under his coat and green glaze at one mouth-corner, wiped his thumb twice before touching nothing, held a narrow slate unopened to his forearm, passed a finger over the figure, took two pair of eels and paid two marks short, said *A number goes into a slate, not a bundle* and *If your house wants it again, there'll be a slate at the watch* with *your house*, never *I*, and went off toward High Kiln with the box locked and the true subtraction inside, no woman putting a fifth green to her mouth this day (T015/T041/T047, T050/T051 held); below the sill a child's foot stayed dark inside three turns of wool, the cold to the woman who wrapped it and weather to the yard, the boy's reading of the extremity going before the core and the afterdrop waiting on a warming nobody would perform working perfectly and buying nothing, with no silt-leach, cause or cure named (T093/T094/T096), while the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set at the landing for an uncalled measure; and at the going of the light Sella carried him up the clay road around a pan set out to dry and a trough with a skin of ice, set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread frozen, told the fire it was the thaw and said *The house will keep it*, and in the small hours he turned out his file and found every object in its place and nothing laid across anything else --- six hearings and no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, five stoppings as objects and not a rule, the charcoal under the shelf unread, the word forty unspoken, the loan uncalled and the seventh year not given --- as the river's note came through the door, the carried fire gave four low pulls and the silence where the fifth should have been with its payer unnamed for one hundred and thirty-seven days, the boards kept four nails, four, and a knot, three counts in three places that did not become one, and his hand went up an inch toward the water and came down with one hundred and thirty-seven exact and unspent.
Day 138 | Reed 19, Y.H. 813 (Ninthday) | the Clay Yard, the drying racks and reed beds below Low Kiln, the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf and hearth and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The reed month stood in its last tend with nobody drawing a boundary around it as Sella Vail carried the boy, one hundred and thirty-eight days old and four months and eighteen days, at the half-pace from every open slate, giving the instruction to the wool and the river wind and never to the basket, while the last bundles came downriver in armfuls judged by the arm that carried them, a cutter told a girl with a creel to lift her bundle and let her arm say what went into it, that being the whole of what it knew, and a woman at the racks who asked what the hands would be paid was answered with the weather and the river and shut two fingers out of a fist as she went;
