under the roof Sella set the basket low near the bench leg at the angle where the underside of the board becomes a roof, moved her lips along the row with its two unnamed gaps, glanced once at the clay jar cold under its glaze and reached a finger BENEATH the front edge where the charcoal tally lay without looking and without a word, held it a breath out and back and brought it away without going back to the shelf (T100 carried, not paid), while a neighbour with a wet pot who asked what the row would want was sent to the pot with the house has said it, and the boy read the carrying arm as a record in muscle that tells what was carried and not what it cost, exact, complete and worth nothing, and held one hundred and thirty-eight unspent as the reeds stood grey in the ruts uncounted.
Day 139 | Reed 20, Y.H. 813 (Tenthday, the second Account-day of Reed) | the Cross reading block and moot-post shade, the pot board and market stalls, the fish-trade shade below the sill, the out-of-line landing below the north bridge, the hearth and kindling under the Vail roof and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | On the second Account-day Berr Clay came up short under the moot-post with the red cord of his office folded under his coat and the green of tasted glaze at one corner of his mouth, wiped his thumb twice before touching nothing, passed a finger over a narrow slate and read one in twelve, as the notice says, called the levy and took himself out of his sentence with your house, never I, so the figure went into the air while the true subtraction stayed in the locked box up the clay road; across the square a silent woman with clay under her nails and a pale line across the back of one hand turned a cup a quarter and set it back where it stood, the fifth green at a mouth and the sixth shape of the colour counting Berr's glaze, a colour and not a name, joined to no ear and never Fenna in the narration (T050/T051 advanced, not paid), and the bell went four, four and a gap with nobody crossing himself, the heard bell kept beside the told; below the bridge the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set for a measure nobody called with his hand to his slate before his eyes to the stalls, and at the stalls Berr paid two marks short for two pair of eels with the stall-keeper putting the marks in her palm uncounted and went up the road believing he had done a kindness, while below the sill a child's foot stayed dark in three turns of wool, the cold to the woman who wrapped it and weather to the yard, the boy's reading of the extremity going before the core and the afterdrop waiting on a warming nobody would perform exact and worth nothing; under the roof that night Tarn brought the sentence about an ear worth more than the debt that holds it a sixth time, on Reed 20 after Reed 5, 8, 11, 14 and 17, all the way to the word that would call the loan and stopped there with no figure and no name, drawing the breath he did not spend, and the boy filed the sixth stopping on the interval tray beside the first five as a sixth interval and not a sixth payment, not a hearing and not a seventh, while the carried fire kept four low pulls and the silence where the fifth should have been with its payer unnamed for one hundred and thirty-nine days and one hundred and thirty-nine stood exact and unspent.
Day 140 | Reed 21, Y.H. 813 (Firstday) | the Vail wheel-shed and its finger's-width-open back room (not entered), the damper, the flagstones and the Clay Yard, the Weir plank walk, flat blessing stone and lower boards, the fish-trade shade below the sill, the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf, bed and hearth, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Before first grey three or four men with their collars high went low-voiced into the room off the shed whose door kept its finger's width of open, and Sella Vail carried the boy past at the half-pace and gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold, while Jorra Vail came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent little finger, keeping dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray, speaking to the wheel before anyone, telling a pot You'll do and withholding its full note from a High Kiln kiln-master who said it was singing before its time, and putting that bent finger into the cold iron of the damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the household straightened around his silence, the boy filing one piece of iron with two bites as an object joined to no kiln with no third telling of the shut room (T062, T065/T095); Tarn's seventh year pressed nearer its end and still held, his stroke going on over the flagstones past the bright patch without the half-check, the unforged collector sentence and the Seed 16 breath unspent beside the knife at the edge of its sill; downriver at the Weir a reed-cutter straightened his grey thumb-oiled cord on the flat blessing stone, turned it once so the knots stood, tied one low with his teeth and bit the end off and put it back inside his coat, telling a woman who could not read it You can ask the cord and No more can the Court (T099 carried, not paid), while Orra Weir pulled one knot down and gave the ninth of Ilmen, the collector's boat with six oars and one man at the stern who never touched them, with the forty-first double turn legible and the word forty unspoken, and Mira Silt answered a thin run with her own answer, moved eel-bones while deciding whether to lie and kept her thumb under her apron to a grey cord with a second twist twisted twice beneath it never carried upriver to Nine Pots or lifted at a door, neither knot counted (T024/T059/T025), and below the sill the child's foot stayed dark in three turns of wool; in the small hours Sella set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft with the fold right and the thread frozen unwiped, told the fire it was the thaw and said The house will keep it, and he turned out his file and found every object in its place and nothing laid across anything else --- six hearings of the full note beside and never among with no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, six stoppings of the near-saying as objects and not a rule, the damper's double bite, the two shut-room tellings with no third, the heard bell beside the told, the fifth green a colour and not a name, the word forty unspoken, the cold foot worth nothing, one crust in the fold and two shelf gaps in rooms that did not touch, every private keeping unread --- and one hundred and forty days old, four months and twenty days, wanted the crust two inches from his ear and the number spoken by somebody who would know what it counted, and got neither, his hand going up an inch toward the note of water over water and coming down against the fold, exact, complete and unspent.
Day 141 | Reed 22, Y.H. 813 (Secondday) | the Clay Yard, the drying racks and reed beds below Low Kiln, the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf and hearth, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The reed month went into its last week without a boundary drawn as Sella Vail carried the boy, one hundred and forty-one days old and four months and twenty-one days, at the half-pace from every open slate, instruction to the wool and the river wind and never the basket, while the last bundles came up judged by the arm that carried them, a cutter telling a girl with a creel that her arm knew what went into a bundle and that was the whole of it, and a woman who asked what the hands would be paid being answered with the weather and the river and shutting two fingers out of a fist; the yard went around the clean dark line, the finer grey called the thaw, the broken birth-slates under the lowest rack and the place where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out, with a look and a wide step and no number (T098 carried), and a reed-cutter's grey cord went past along the boards straightened and turned where no slate lay with its knots unread (T099 carried); under the roof a neighbour reached past the bare board with her hand before her eyes and named the old shelf-gap and the empty place at the boy's ear under one appetite in one breath, a further shaping of T097 filed on a place of its own, advanced and not paid, no third gap and no cause made, while Sella moved her lips along the row, glanced once at the clay jar cold under its glaze, sent the woman to the pot with the house has said it and reached a finger beneath the board where the charcoal tally lay without looking or a word, and brought it away (T100 carried, unread), the widow's mark untouched because a shelf and an afternoon are not openings, and the boy read the carrying arm as a record in muscle that tells what was carried and not what it cost, exact, complete and worth nothing, one hundred and forty-one going on with nobody's thumb upon it.
Day 142 | Reed 23, Y.H. 813 (Thirdday) | the Vail wheel-shed and its finger's-width-open back room (not entered), the damper, the flagstones, the Cross and market stalls, the out-of-line landing below the north bridge, the hearth and kindling under the Vail roof and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Before first grey three or four men with collars high went low-voiced into the room off the shed and Sella Vail carried the boy past at the half-pace, giving them no name and not saying the room was cold, while Jorra Vail came through with grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent finger, spoke to the wheel before anyone, told a pot You'll do, knew its full note and withheld it from a High Kiln kiln-master who said it was singing before its time, and put his bent finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the household straightened around his silence, the boy filing one iron with two bites as an object joined to no kiln with no third telling of the shut room (T062, T065/T095); Tarn's seventh year pressed nearer its end than ever and still held, his stroke going on over the flagstones past the shallow bright patch without the half-check; at the Cross Berr Clay read one in twelve, called the levy and denied it with your house, never I, paid two marks short for two pair of eels which the stall-keeper put in her palm without counting where anyone could hear, and went up the road with the true subtraction locked in the box believing he had done a kindness, while below the bridge the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set for a measure nobody called; under the roof at the going of the light Tarn told the kindling Six counted. The next I don't count and brought the ear-sentence a SEVENTH time --- on Reed 23 after Reed 5, 8, 11, 14, 17 and 20 --- all the way to the word that would call the loan, stopping there with no figure and no name and drawing the breath he did not spend, and the boy filed the seventh stopping on the interval tray beside the six as an interval and not a seventh payment, refusing to let seven likenesses stand up and call themselves a doctrine because no payer had been named to any, the loan uncalled, the seventh year one tend from its end and the fire's payer unnamed for a hundred and forty-two days, one hundred and forty-two standing exact and unspent.
Day 143 | Reed 24, Y.H. 813 (Fourthday) | the road downriver, the Weir plank walk, the flat blessing stone and lower boards, the fish-trade shade below the sill, the out-of-line landing below the north bridge, the Clay Yard and reed beds, the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf, bed and hearth, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella Vail carried the boy, one hundred and forty-three days old and four months and twenty-three days, downriver to the Weir with instruction to the wool and the running water and never the basket, and a reed-cutter set his bundle on the flat blessing stone, straightened his grey thumb-oiled cord, turned it so the knots stood, tied one low with his teeth, bit the end off and put it back inside his coat, telling the woman who could not read it You can ask the cord and No more can the Court, with no knot counted (T099 carried); Orra Weir pulled one knot down and gave the ninth of Ilmen, the collector's boat with six oars and the man at the stern who never touched them, keeping the forty-first double turn legible and the word forty unspoken, while Mira Silt answered a thin run with Ask the river, flicked eel-bones while deciding whether to lie, and when a buyer named a figure put her thumb under her apron to the grey cord with a second twist twisted twice beneath it, never carried upriver to Nine Pots or lifted at a door, neither knot counted, her board beside Orra's and not touching (T024/T059/T025), so that three private records stood within a dozen paces of the basket, visible and none enterable, and below the sill a child's foot stayed dark in three turns of wool, the cold to the woman who wrapped it and weather to the yard, the boy's reading of the extremity going before the core and the afterdrop waiting past a warming nobody would perform exact and worth nothing with no silt-leach, cause or cure named (T093/T094/T096); the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set at the landing for the uncalled measure, and in the afternoon the last bundles stood grey in the ruts with nobody counting them, the third Account-day six days off, while the yard went around the clean dark line, the thaw, the broken birth-slates and the place where the hand had gone in on Seed 15 and not come out, with a look and a wide step and no number (T098 carried); under the roof a neighbour with a wet pot was sent to the pot with the house has said it and Sella, moving her lips along the row with its two unnamed gaps, reached a finger beneath the board where the charcoal lay without looking or a word (T100 carried, unread), the two rooms not touching, and at going of light she carried him up around the pan and iced trough, set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread frozen, told the fire it was the thaw and said The house will keep it, and in the small hours he turned out his file and found it exact, complete and worth nothing with seven stoppings on the tray and no payer named to any, one hundred and forty-three unspent and the loan uncalled with the seventh year one tend from its end.
Day 144 | Reed 25, Y.H. 813 (Fifthday) | the Clay Yard, the drying racks and reed beds below Low Kiln, the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf and hearth, the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The reed month went out without a boundary drawn as Sella Vail carried the boy, one hundred and forty-four days old and four months and twenty-four days, at the half-pace from every open slate, instruction to the wool and the river wind and never the basket, while the last bundles came up in armfuls judged by the arm that carried them, a cutter telling a girl with a creel that her arm knew what went into a bundle and that was the whole of what it knew, and a man who asked what the hands would be paid being answered with the weather and the river, never a figure (T071/T072, T077); the yard went around the clean dark line, the finer grey called the thaw, the space beneath the lowest rack where the broken birth-slates lay and the empty place in the returning line where a hand went in on Seed 15 and had not come out, with a look and a wide step and no number (T098 carried), and a reed-cutter's grey cord went past along the boards straightened and turned where no slate lay with its knots unread (T099 carried); under the roof Sella set the basket low near the bench leg at the angle where the board's underside becomes a roof, moved her lips along the row with its two unnamed gaps, glanced once at the clay jar cold under its glaze and reached a finger BENEATH the board where the charcoal tally lay without looking and without a word, brought it away and did not go back to the shelf (T100 carried, unread), told the fire it was the thaw, turned the coals with the back of a broken pot, said The house will keep it, counted under her breath toward no opening, pushed coal-black hair back with the burn-wrist and left the widow's mark untouched, while the boy read the carrying arm as a record in muscle that tells what was carried and not what it cost, exact, complete and worth nothing, held seven stoppings on the interval tray with no payer named to any and refused to bring the eighth early into the yard to make a row come out even, and one hundred and forty-four went on exact and unspent with nobody's thumb upon it.
Day 145 | Reed 26, Y.H. 813 (Sixthday) | the Vail wheel-shed and its finger's-width-open back room (not entered), the damper, the flagstones, the Clay Yard, the plank walk at the Weir, the flat blessing stone and lower boards, the fish-trade shade below the sill, the out-of-line landing below the north bridge, the hearth and kindling under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Before first grey three or four men with collars high went low-voiced into the room off the shed and Sella Vail carried the boy past at the half-pace, giving them no name and not saying the room was cold, while Jorra Vail came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent little finger, kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray, spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady*), told a pot You'll do, knew its full note before it left the wheel and withheld the knowing from a High Kiln kiln-master who said it was singing before its time, and put that bent finger into the cold iron of the damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the household straightened around his silence, the boy filing one iron with two bites as an object joined to no kiln and Jorra's withheld knowing beside the three near-full answers and not among the six hearings with no seventh (T062, T061/T082), while no third telling came of the counting room shut in the war (T065/T095); Tarn took the broom only after it had been instructed, his seventh year one tend from its end and still held, the knife at the very edge of its sill with six counted where the house could check and the seventh where it could not, the unforged collector sentence beside it, the Seed 16 breath unspent with its small interval at the top of each reach, and his stroke going on over the flagstones past the shallow bright patch without even the half-check while he did not turn his head along the collector road; downriver the reed-cutter turned his grey cord on the blessing stone, tied one knot with his teeth, bit the end off and put it back inside his coat, saying You can ask the cord and No more can the Court (T099 carried), Orra Weir pulled one knot down and gave the ninth of Ilmen, the collector's boat with six oars and the man at the stern who never touched them, keeping the forty-first double turn legible and the word forty unspoken (T024), Mira Silt answered a thin run with Ask the river, flicked eel-bones while deciding whether to lie and kept her thumb under her apron to the grey cord longer than the figure required with the second twist twice beneath it never carried to Nine Pots or lifted at a door (T059/T025), a child's foot below the sill stayed dark in three turns of wool with the boy's reading of the extremity going before the core and the afterdrop waiting past a warming nobody would perform exact and worth nothing (T093/T094/T096), and the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set at the landing for a measure nobody called (T090/T080); that night Tarn told the kindling Six counted. The next I don't count and brought the ear-sentence an EIGHTH time --- after Reed 5, 8, 11, 14, 17, 20 and 23 --- all the way to the word that would call the loan, with a slate at the watch, a name, a number under the name and a collector coming down the road, and stopped there with no figure and no name, drew the breath he did not spend, said Yes, house and went on with the broom, and the boy filed the eighth stopping on the interval tray beside the seven as an interval and not a payment, not a hearing and not an eighth hearing, holding eight likenesses apart so they could not stand up and call themselves a doctrine with no payer named to any (T036/T076, T061/T082), while Jorra did not ask about the ear and Tarn did not ask about the room, the carried fire kept four low pulls and the silence where the fifth should have been (T088), and one hundred and forty-five stood exact and unspent.
Day 146 | Reed 27, Y.H. 813 (Seventhday) | House Silt three doors down, the finger's-width-open door, the plank walk and lower boards at the Weir, the fish-trade shade, the clay road, the pan set out to dry and the iced trough by the well, the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf, hearth and bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella Vail set the basket just inside the finger's-width-open door of House Silt at the edge of the light and stood a half-pace off, asking nothing about the writing, the tin, the sign or the date, while Alda Reed came short with soap-and-milk gone cold under river silt and lye sour under the soap, laid her birth-slates writing-down on the bench before turning to the fold, pressed the wool flat at his shoulder a breath longer than a fold requires, touched the little tin in her bodice without opening it, crossed two fingers at the red edge of the birth-flower before she knew she had done it and covered the sign with the date she had given Dessa, Stone seven. The account went up on the eighth, before the paper went out, standing over a woman more mornings dead than any living person keeps count of and uncollected; to He lies quiet she said Warm and breathing, that's all I've set down, exact and not true, and went on with the cooper's seventh in Mud End, a potter's third child in Stonemouth and the last high water after the question had stopped, leaving six pages in a second hand turned face-down toward a floorboard in a house the boy could not name, no seventh written, the round pewter tin above the abandoned board-bed shut with its pale fold and thumb-bright rub, the grey wool below holding only the hollow where the small pale river stone had lain, nobody saying either was gone (T007/T008, T023/T089), while the boy, who could not read Grathic, kept sign, turned slate and covered flower on places of their own and made no cause (T061/T082); along the Weir boards in the afternoon the reed-cutter turned his grey cord, tied one knot low with his teeth and put it back inside his coat with its knots unread (T099 carried) and Orra Weir set one knot among the others with a small flat knock, giving no figure and keeping the forty-first double turn legible and unnamed (T024), two private records side by side and not across; at the going of the light Sella carried him up the clay road at the half-pace, went around a pan set out to dry, the trough with its skin of ice and the space beneath the lowest rack where the broken birth-slates lay, set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread of drool frozen and unwiped, rested her hand a half-beat longer than the setting needed, told the fire it was the thaw, said The house will keep it, counted under her breath toward no opening, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand and left the widow's mark untouched because a dusk is not an opening, then went to her own bed; in the small hours he turned out his file as a man lays instruments on a towel --- six hearings of the full note with no seventh, the four near-full answers advanced and not paid, the older intervals with the unchecking stroke beside the half-beat, EIGHT stoppings on the widened interval tray (Reed 5, 8, 11, 14, 17, 20, 23 and 26) as objects and not a rule with no payer named to any, the damper's two bites joined to no kiln, the two shut-room tellings with no third, the told bell beside the heard bell --- while the charcoal under the shelf waited for a hand that was not his, the fifth green stayed a colour joined to no ear, the word forty stayed unspoken, the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set for the uncalled measure and the cold foot stayed the cold below the sill, and three counts in three places --- the river's note unnumbered, the fire's missing fifth pull and the fifth that was a knot and not a nail --- did not become one, so that one hundred and forty-six stood exact and unspent and spoken by nobody as his hand went up an inch toward the note of water over water and came down against the fold, the loan uncalled and the seventh year one tend from its end.
Day 147 | Reed 28, Y.H. 813 (Eighthday) | the Clay Yard, the drying racks and reed beds below Low Kiln, the edge of the worked strips, the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf and hearth, the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella Vail carried the boy, one hundred and forty-seven days old and four months and twenty-seven days, down at the half-pace from every open slate with instruction to the wool and the river wind and never the basket while the last bundles came out of the beds in armfuls judged by the arm that carried them, a cutter telling a girl with a creel that her arm knew what went into a bundle and that was the whole of what it knew and a man asking what the hands would be paid being answered with the weather and the river, never a figure (T071/T072, T077); the yard went around the clean dark line, the finer grey called the thaw, the space beneath the lowest rack where the broken birth-slates lay and the empty place in the returning line where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out, with a look and a wide step and no number (T009/T013/T092, T098 carried), and a reed-cutter's grey cord went past along the boards, straightened and turned where no slate lay, tied low with teeth and thumb, the end bitten off and the cord put back inside his coat with the flat of a hand, its knots unread (T099 carried), while the boy read the carrying arm as a record in muscle that tells what was carried and not what it cost, exact, complete and worth nothing, and noted the order of reaching, the hand to the record before the hand to the person, without standing to enter either (T061/T082); under the roof the shelf kept its two unnamed gaps in a room the fold did not touch, a neighbour come up from the strips asked what the row would want before the last market and was sent to the pot with the house has said it, carrying the question away level so nothing slopped, and Sella, standing with her back to the basket and moving her lips along the row, glanced once at the clay jar cold under its glaze and reached a finger BENEATH the front edge where the charcoal tally lay without looking or a word, brought it away and did not go back to the shelf (T100 carried, unread), told the fire it was the thaw, turned the coals with the back of a broken pot, said The house will keep it, counted under her breath toward no opening, pushed coal-black hair back with the burn-wrist and left the widow's mark untouched (T058/T079), and at going of light the file held six hearings with no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and not paid and eight stoppings with no payer named to any while one hundred and forty-seven stood exact and unspent with the last market two days off and the month going out without a boundary drawn.
Day 148 | Reed 29, Y.H. 813 (Ninthday) | the Vail wheel-shed and its finger's-width-open back room (not entered), the damper, the flagstones, the Clay Yard, the plank walk and flat blessing stone at the Weir, the fish-trade shade below the sill, the out-of-line landing below the north bridge, the hearth and kindling under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Before first grey three or four unnamed men with collars high came low-voiced into the room off the shed and Sella Vail carried the boy past at the half-pace, giving them no name, turning no head and not saying the room was cold (T035/T031), while Jorra Vail came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent little finger and under the nails, not bone-coloured slip, kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray, spoke to the wheel before anyone (*It doesn't care whose hands. Tarn took the broom only after it had been instructed, his seventh year one tend from its end and still held, the knife at the very edge of its sill with six counted where the house could check and the seventh where it could not, the unforged collector sentence beside it, the Seed 16 breath unspent with its small interval at the top of each reach, and his stroke going on over the flagstones past the shallow bright patch without even the half-check while he did not turn his head along the collector road and no collector came (T036/T076); downriver the reed-cutter turned his grey cord on the blessing stone, tied one knot low with his teeth, bit the end off and put it back inside his coat, telling the woman who could not read it You can ask the cord and No more can the Court with no knot counted (T099 carried), Orra Weir pulled one knot down and gave the ninth of Ilmen, the collector's boat with six oars and the man at the stern who never touched them, keeping the forty-first double turn legible and the word forty unspoken (T024), Mira Silt answered a thin run with Ask the river, flicked eel-bones while deciding whether to lie and put one away, kept her thumb under her apron to the grey cord longer than the figure required and showed the second twist twice beneath it, never carried to Nine Pots or lifted at a door, her board beside Orra's and not touching (T059/T025), a child's foot below the sill stayed dark in three turns of wool with the boy's reading of the extremity going before the core and the afterdrop waiting past a warming nobody would perform exact and worth nothing (T093/T094/T096), and the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set at the landing with his slate inward and hand to slate before eyes to stalls (T090/T080); that night Tarn set the small knife back where it could be checked, told the kindling Six counted. The next I don't count and brought the ear-sentence a NINTH time --- after Reed 5, 8, 11, 14, 17, 20, 23 and 26 --- all the way to the word that would call the loan, with a slate at the watch, a name, a number under the name and a collector coming down the road, and stopped there with no figure and no name, drew the breath he did not spend, said Yes, house and went on with the broom without the half-check, while the boy filed the ninth stopping on the widened interval tray beside the eight as an interval and not a payment, not a hearing and not a ninth hearing, and held nine likenesses apart so they could not stand up and call themselves a doctrine with no payer named to any (T036/T076, T061/T082), Jorra not asking about the ear and Tarn not asking about the room, the two silences unjoined, and one hundred and forty-eight standing exact and unspent with the seventh year one tend from its end and the loan uncalled.
Day 149 | Reed 30, Y.H. 813 (Tenthday, the third Account-day and the last market of Reed) | the Cross, the reading block under the moot-post, the market stalls and pot board, the out-of-line landing below the north bridge, the market's edge, the clay road, the Vail kiln-house hearth and the bed under the Vail roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The reed month went out in the square without a boundary drawn as Sella Vail set the basket in the moot-post's finger's width of shade where no slate lay, instruction to the wool and the river wind and never the basket, and the boy, one hundred and forty-nine days old and four months and twenty-nine days, lay with cold iron at his cheek while everything the district kept from its own slates stood together and none was read (T058/T079, T071/T072); Berr Clay came up short off the clay road with his office cord folded square under his coat and green of tasted lead glaze at one mouth-corner, wiped his thumb twice before touching nothing, held a narrow slate unopened to his forearm, read one in twelve, as the notice says, low into the goers-by over the last bundles, called the levy and denied it with your house, never I, told the man with the half-shut fist that a number goes into a slate, not a bundle, and that there would be a slate at the watch, paid two marks short for two pair of eels into a palm that did not count them aloud, and went off up the clay road with the box locked and the true subtraction inside, believing he had done a kindness, so that the figure read was not the figure paid and the figure in the slate did not know the hand that had gone in on Seed 15 and had not come back (T015/T041/T047/T077, T098 carried), while the bell went four, four and a gap with nobody crossing himself and the heard bell kept beside the told bell (T078), the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set below the bridge for a measure nobody called with thumb to slate before face came up and a girl taking the out-of-line step sideways without a fall (T090/T080), a silent woman at the pot board with clay under her nails and a pale line across the back of one hand turned a cup a quarter without lifting it, set it back exactly where it had stood and went off with the light, the SIXTH green at a mouth, a colour joined to no ear and never Fenna in narration (T050/T051 advanced, not paid), and at the market's edge the foundling stood in cast-offs worn like a uniform with silt pale in his wrist creases, a gap where a front tooth had been and a green sliver on a string at his throat, and when a kiln-hand set a pot down his head went toward it half a beat after the boy's, after the knuckle and after the note, then stopped in the middle of the turn, and he rubbed the sliver twice and opened his mouth on the tail of a sentence and let nothing out that any clerk could take down, the SIXTH silent glimpse, unnamed, silent and given no dialogue with no loaf passed, no slate seen near the basket and no sale (T033/T034 advanced, not paid), the boy keeping green and half-turn on places of their own, neither a face nor a sale, no fourth likeness made a cause and no third gap coming (T061/T082, T060/T086, T097); under the roof that night Sella set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread of drool frozen and unwiped, rested her hand a half-beat longer than the setting, told the fire it was the thaw, said The house will keep it, counted under her breath toward no opening, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand and left the widow's mark untouched, and in the small hours his file held six hearings with no seventh, four near-full answers advanced and not paid and NINE stoppings --- Reed 5, 8, 11, 14, 17, 20, 23, 26 and 29 --- as objects and not a rule with no payer named to any, the sixth colour and the sixth shape kept on places of their own, the charcoal waiting under the shelf, the word forty unspoken, the clerk still set for the uncalled measure and the cold foot still the cold below the sill, so that one hundred and forty-nine stood exact and unspent and spoken by nobody as his hand went up an inch toward the note of water over water and came down against the fold, the reed month going out without a boundary drawn and the count crossing into Smoke with the loan uncalled and the seventh year one tend from its end.
Day 150 | Tallow 1, Y.H. 813 (Firstday) | the Clay Yard and the kiln mouth, the drying racks and the edge of the worked strips, the yard well, the Vail wheel-shed and its finger's-width-open back room (not entered), the damper and the flagstones, the Vail kiln-house hearth and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The firing month opened at last as Sella Vail carried the boy, one hundred and fifty days old and five months exactly and told by nobody, at the half-pace from every open slate with instruction to the wool, the fire, the pot and the broom and never to the basket, and at the kiln mouth she put her hand to the widow's mark before the opening, held it one breath, let go and did not touch it again, tipping the coal under the back of a broken pot while the fire answered four low pulls and the silence where the fifth should have been with its payer unnamed for a hundred and fifty days (T058/T079, T088, T071/T072); the kilns had been brought up for the year's last working season and the danger changed address to the kiln's breath in fours with a gap, hot clay, grey slate dust ground small as meal under the nails and the sweetish edge of lead glaze on a wrong wind off High Kiln, a yard woman speaking sideways of a man in Mud End taken in his sleep and nobody making a lesson of it, while the trough by the well had lost its skin of ice and was called weather, the clay jar stood cold under its glaze and the pan set out to dry through Reed was gone from its place with no scrape, crumb, print or word and nobody saying so (T062, T097); the yard went around the clean dark line where the Ash ridge had turned, the finer grey called the thaw, the broken birth-slates under the lowest rack and the place in the returning line where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out, with a look and a wide step and no number (T009/T013/T092, T098 carried), and before first grey three or four unnamed men had gone low-voiced into the finger's-width-open back room with no name given and no third telling of the counting room (T035/T031, T065/T095); Jorra Vail came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent little finger, spoke to the wheel before anyone, told a pot You'll do, knew its full note and withheld it from a High Kiln kiln-master who said it was singing before its time, and put that bent finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence so the household straightened around his silence, the boy filing one iron with two bites joined to no kiln and the knowing among the near-full answers and not among the hearings (T062, T061/T082); Tarn took the broom only after it had been instructed, his seventh year one tend from its end and still held, the knife at the very edge of its sill with six counted where the house could check and the seventh where it could not, the unforged collector sentence beside it, the Seed 16 breath unspent with its small interval at the top of each reach, his chest tight since the dark and Ash worked into his knuckle creases, and his stroke went on over the flagstones past the shallow bright patch without even the half-check while he did not turn along the collector road and no collector came, neither man naming the other's silence (T036/T076); at dusk she went wide around the pan's empty place, the ice-less trough and the rack space, set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread frozen, rested her hand a half-beat longer, told the fire it was the thaw, said The house will keep it, counted under her breath toward no opening, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand and left the widow's mark untouched because a dusk is not an opening, and in the small hours his file held six hearings of the full note beside and never among with no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, the older intervals and NINE stoppings --- Reed 5, 8, 11, 14, 17, 20, 23, 26 and 29 --- as objects and not a rule with no payer named to any, the damper's double bite, the two shut-room tellings with no third and the told bell beside the heard bell, while three counts in three places --- the river's note unnumbered, the fire's missing fifth and the fifth that was a knot and not a nail --- did not become one, so that one hundred and fifty stood exact, complete and unspent as his hand went up an inch toward the note of water and came down against the fold.
Day 151 | Tallow 2, Y.H. 813 (Secondday) | the river road downriver, the Weir plank walk on posts, the flat blessing stone and lower boards, the fish-trade shade below the sill, the out-of-line landing below the north bridge, the clay road, the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf and hearth, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The last of the reed work went downriver as a direction rather than a bundle, counted in the arm that carried it, and Sella Vail set the basket at the edge of the work where no slate lay with instruction to the wool and the running water and never to the basket, while a woman who asked what the hands would be paid for the going down and the coming back was answered with the going down and the coming back and went off with the question still in her mouth and no entry anywhere (T071/T072, T077, T058/T079); the boy, one hundred and fifty-one days old and five months and a day, watched the reed-cutter set his bundle on the flat blessing stone, straighten his grey thumb-oiled cord, turn it once so the knots stood, tie one low with his teeth, bite the end off and put it back inside his coat with the flat of his hand, saying You can ask the cord and No more can the Court and going up the boards to lift a trap with no figure and no knot counted (T099 carried), while below the stone Orra Weir, her hands like old rope, the pale eel scars uncovered, salt white at her wrists, the grey streak first through the black hair and the Fen half-song flat in her voice, pulled one knot down and gave the ninth of Ilmen, the collector's boat round the bend on the flood with six oars and one man at the stern who never touched them, saying It's a date. The river takes what the Court doesn't. You can shout at either one. I've tried, and keeping the forty-first double turn legible with the word forty unspoken (T024/T059/T025); below the sill Mira Silt, pale eel slime dried in grey strokes on her sleeves and silt pale in the hollow of her neck, her head held a little right where the top of her left ear had been and her small smile kept like a door on the latch, answered a thin run with Thin? Who's to say thin? It comes when it comes. Ask the river, flicked eel-bones while deciding whether to lie, and when a buyer named a figure said Two marks the pair and two short. You've said it twice and I've counted it twice. There's nothing in the sentence that wants an answer, her thumb going under her apron to the grey cord and staying longer than the figure required with the second twist twisted twice beneath a shifting creel, never carried upriver to Nine Pots or lifted at a door, her board beside Orra's and not touching (T059/T025/T024); a child's foot below the shade stayed dark inside three turns of wool, the cold to the woman who wound it and weather to the neighbour with a pail, the boy's reading of the extremity going before the core, the almost-smile that is not peace and the afterdrop waiting past a warming nobody would perform exact and worth nothing with no silt-leach, cause or cure named (T093/T094/T096 carried), while the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set at the out-of-line landing for an uncalled measure with his cuff grey from washing and grey again by midday, his slate inward and his hand to it before his eyes went to the stalls, and the market sold over him as water goes over a stone that lies where a stone lies (T090/T080 carried), five private records standing within a dozen paces of the basket and none enterable with no foundling, no loaf, no slate and no sale (T033/T034 held); under the roof in the going of the light the pan's gone place on the wall kept the exact width of a thing taken away with no dust disturbed and the boy held it on a tray of its own, apart from the shelf's two unnamed gaps, the stiff brown-edged crust two inches from his ear in the other room, the field's empty place and the sixth colour and sixth shape, with no third gap and no fourth likeness made a cause (T060/T086, T097 advanced, T098, T050/T051, T033/T034); the clay jar stood cold under its glaze and Sella glanced at it once and did not touch it, then stood with her back to the basket, moved her lips along the row with its two gaps and, without looking or a word, reached a finger BENEATH the front edge where the charcoal tally lies, held it a breath out and back, brought it away and did not go back to the shelf, such a hand either knowing the board by heart or told not to look with the reason standing between the two (T100 carried, unread); a neighbour with a wet pot who asked what a firing cost set down there was sent to the pot with Ask the pot what a wet thing costs set down there. The house has said it and carried the unanswered question away level so nothing slopped (T077), and at the hearth Sella told the fire it was the thaw, turned the coals with the back of a broken pot, said The house will keep it, counted under her breath toward no opening and left the widow's mark hanging where it hung because an afternoon is not an opening, while the fire gave four low pulls and the silence where the fifth should have been with its payer unnamed for a hundred and fifty-one days (T088, T058/T079), so that one hundred and fifty-one stood exact and unspent with the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand and the charcoal waiting under the shelf for a hand that was not his.
Day 152 | Tallow 3, Y.H. 813 (Thirdday) | the Clay Yard, the Vail wheel-shed, the cold damper, the flagstones, the hearth and kindling, the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The third day of Tallow came up with no woman marking it and no slate the wiser as Sella Vail gave the instruction to the wool, the fire, the pot and the broom before anybody and never to the basket, and Tarn took the broom only after it had been instructed, saying Yes, house to an early thaw and a late while going on wanting the late because it puts off the paying, his seventh year standing one tend from its end and still held with six counted in the worn thumb-mark where the house can check and the seventh where it cannot, the small knife at the very edge of its sill and the unforged collector sentence lying beside it, the Seed 16 breath unspent with its small interval at the top of each reach, his chest tight since the dark, Ash worked into the creases of his knuckles in the colour of the clean dark line that no scrubbing lifts and the pale clip-ring at his left wrist holding what light there is and giving it back (T036/T076); his stroke went on over the flagstones past the shallow bright patch without even the half-check, he did not turn his head along the road a house sends a man down when it wants a collector and no collector came, while two ears had kept their equality under the roof for a hundred and fifty-two days --- his, which heard a pot before a knuckle touched it, and the boy's, which turned toward the note before anybody made it --- and a loan that could hear had never been called (T036/T076); Jorra Vail came through with the day's grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent little finger and under the nails, not bone-coloured slip, keeping dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and asking neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, the broken birth-slates under the lowest rack entered by nobody, and spoke to the wheel before anyone, told a pot You'll do, knew its full note and withheld it from a High Kiln kiln-master who said it was singing before its time and would sing itself out early, the knowing going on the near-full tray and not among the six hearings with no seventh and none invented (T035/T031, T062, T061/T082, T009/T013/T092); before Sella had finished telling him the fire was low his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and left it standing, the household straightening around his silence the way cloth straightens when a weight is lifted, the boy reading one piece of iron with two bites, the holding and the taking, filed joined to no kiln with no Grathic word for both mercies, while the two tellings of the shut counting room stood side by side with no third and men with their collars high had gone low into the finger's-width-open back room before first grey with no name given (T062, T065/T095); at the going of the light the coals were banked under the back of a broken pot, Tarn told the kindling Six counted. The next I don't count, and the sentence about an ear under this roof being worth more than the debt that holds it came up as it had nine times before and on this day was not brought to the word that would call the loan, stopping short of the threshold with no figure and no name, no tenth stopping standing up to join the nine, the breath that should have gone in at the top of the reach drawn and not spent, the Seed 16 breath unspent, the seven years one tend from its end and not given, Sella within hearing giving the end of her instruction to the pot and asking nothing, Jorra not asking about the ear and Tarn not asking about the room so the two silences stayed unjoined (T036/T076, T061/T082); at dusk she carried him up the clay road at the half-pace, went around the pan's empty place, the trough without its skin of ice and wide around the rack space, set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread frozen, let her hand rest a half-beat longer, told the fire it was the thaw, said The house will keep it, counted under her breath toward no opening, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand and left the widow's mark untouched because a dusk is not an opening, and in the small hours his file held six hearings beside and never among with no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, the older intervals at Stone 13, 17 and 25, NINE stoppings of the near-saying as objects and not a rule with no payer named to any, the damper's double bite, the two shut-room tellings with no third and the told bell beside the heard bell, while the shelf kept its two gaps and unread charcoal, the crust kept its empty place unnamed, the pan's gone place kept its round ring on a tray of its own, the sixth green stayed a colour joined to no ear and the foundling a shape with no sale, and three counts in three places --- the river's note over water through the finger's-width door, the fire's four low pulls and silence under the floor with its payer unnamed for a hundred and fifty-two days, the boards' four nails, four, and a fifth that was a knot the width of a thumb --- did not become one, so that one hundred and fifty-two stood exact, complete, unspent and spoken by nobody as his hand went up an inch toward the note of water and came down against the fold, the charcoal waiting under the shelf for a hand that was not his and a day that had not come.
Day 153 | Tallow 4, Y.H. 813 (Fourthday) | the Clay Yard and kiln mouth, the drying racks and the edge of the worked strips, the yard well and the bare trough, the Vail wheel-shed edge, the damper and the flagstones, the Vail kiln-house hearth and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The firing month's second working morning opened with the boy one hundred and fifty-three days old and five months and three days and nobody under any roof saying so, the first smell hot clay under tallow and the first sound the kiln's breath in fours with a gap, not a bell, the carried fire under the floor answering four low pulls with the silence where the fifth should have been and its payer unnamed for a hundred and fifty-three days while the river kept its note of water over water as a fifth low thing never counted among the hearings (T088, T071/T072); Sella Vail gave the instruction to the wool, the fire, the pot and the broom and never to the basket, kept her half-pace from every open slate, sent a wet-pot neighbour's question about a firing's cost to the pot with The house has said it, and left the widow's mark hanging where it had hung since the first firing because a morning was not an opening (T058/T079); the yard went around the clean dark line where the Ash ridge had turned, the new finer grey called the thaw, the broken birth-slates under the lowest rack and the empty place in the returning line where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out, with a look and a wide step and no number, and at the well the trough stood bare and dark with its skin of ice gone these three days and was called weather while the clay jar stood cold under its glaze, glanced at once and not touched (T009/T013/T092, T098 carried, T058/T079); Tarn took the broom only after it had been instructed, with six counted in the worn thumb-mark where the house could check and the seventh at the lip of the sill where it could not, the knife at the very edge, Garl Vail's unforged collector sentence beside it, the Seed 16 breath unspent, his chest tight since the dark and Ash worked into his knuckle creases, and his stroke went on over the flagstones past the shallow bright patch without even the half-check, no collector coming and no tenth stopping standing up before the seventh year had been earned (T036/T076, T061/T082); the boy read the kiln mouth as a theatre he could not enter, heat kept and counted behind a screen and a gate with two bites, the cost paid by a man whose name was not spoken under any roof, and in the small hours one hundred and fifty-three stood exact, complete and unspent as his hand went up an inch toward the river's note and came down against the fold (T062).
Day 154 | Tallow 5, Y.H. 813 (Fifthday) | the Vail wheel-shed and its finger's-width-open back room (not entered), the damper, the flagstones, the Clay Yard and drying racks, the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Before first grey three or four unnamed men went low-voiced with their collars high into the room off the wheel-shed that kept its finger's width of open, going down as men go into a room they have entered before, and Sella Vail carried the boy past at the half-pace, turned no head, gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold, while no third telling came of the counting room shut in the war and the two tellings lay side by side and did not touch (T035/T031, T065/T095); the boy was one hundred and fifty-four days old and five months and four days and nobody said so, and he set fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past Jorra Vail's bent little finger and under the nails on one tray and bone-coloured slip on another, a thing ground being a thing broken and a thing mixed a thing put together on purpose, while Jorra asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate and the broken birth-slates lay under the lowest rack entered by nobody (T009/T013/T092); Jorra spoke to the wheel before anyone (It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady), told a pot You'll do, knew the full note it would give under a knuckle and withheld the knowing from a High Kiln kiln-master who came up on the bad wind with Ash the colour of the ridge on his cuffs and said the pot was singing before its time and would sing itself out early, the knowing going to the tray of near-full answers advanced and not paid and not among the six hearings with no seventh (T061/T082); before Sella had finished telling him the fire was low his bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and left it standing, and the household straightened around his silence the way cloth straightens when a weight is lifted, the boy filing one iron with two bites, the holding and the taking, joined to no kiln with no Grathic word for both mercies (T062); Tarn took the broom only after instruction, his stroke going on over the flagstones past the shallow bright patch without even the half-check and not turning along the road a house sends a man down when it wants a collector, no collector coming, while he did not ask about the room and Jorra did not ask about the ear, the two silences standing unjoined under one roof; at midday she set the boy on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, going around the round bare ring where the pan had stood out to dry through Reed, and stood with her back to the basket moving her lips along the row with its two unnamed gaps while her finger did not go beneath the front edge today and the charcoal waited unread, so that the pan's gone place, the shelf's two gaps, the crust's empty place and the field's empty place stayed each on its own tray with no third gap and no fourth likeness made a cause, and one hundred and fifty-four stood exact, complete and unspent (T097, T100, T060/T086).
Day 155 | Tallow 6, Y.H. 813 (Sixthday) | the river road downriver, the Weir plank walk on posts, the flat blessing stone and lower boards, the fish-trade shade below the sill, the out-of-line landing below the north bridge, the Vail kiln-house bed and hearth, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The last of the reed work came downriver as a direction and not a bundle, the going down and the coming back counted in the arm that carried them, and a woman who asked what the hands would be paid now the reeds were out of them was answered with the going down and the coming back and with the river and the weather, leaving no figure and no entry, for an arm that carries a load all morning knows the weight of what it carried and has no nerve that can enter the cost (T071/T072, T077); Sella Vail set the basket at the edge of the work where no slate lay, going around a propped slate, and gave the instruction to the wool and the running water and never to the basket; on the flat blessing stone the reed-cutter straightened his grey thumb-oiled cord, turned it once so the knots stood, tied one low with his teeth and a pull of the thumb, bit the end off and put it back inside his coat with the flat of the hand, telling the woman who asked what it held You can ask the cord and No more can the Court, his hand going to the cord before the trap and before the woman and no knot or figure counted (T099 carried); below the stone Orra Weir, her hands like old rope, the pale eel scars uncovered, salt white at her wrists, the grey streak first through the black hair and the nasal Fen half-song flat in her voice where she was tired, pulled one knot down with a small flat knock and gave the woman who asked what the levy would take now the hands were out of the reeds the date The ninth of Ilmen. The collector's boat came round the bend on the flood and had six oars. One man stood at the stern and never touched them, and to That isn't the levy, It's a date. The river takes what the Court doesn't. You can shout at either one. I've tried, keeping the forty-first double turn legible, tail tucked, left over right with the weight of a thumb, and the word forty unspoken, while her thumb went to the cord again at the end and the boy made no cause of whether it was a lie or the habit of counting (T024/T059/T025); below the sill Mira Silt, slime dried in long grey strokes on her sleeves and silt pale in the hollow of her neck with her head held a little right where the top of her left ear had been and her small smile kept like a door on the latch, answered a thin run with Thin? Who's to say thin? It comes when it comes. Ask the river, flicked eel-bones while deciding whether to lie, and told a buyer who named a figure Two marks the pair and two short. You've said it twice and I've counted it twice. There's nothing in the sentence that wants an answer, her thumb going under her apron to the grey cord and the second twist shown twice beneath a shifting creel, never carried upriver to Nine Pots or lifted at a door, her board beside Orra's and not touching (T059/T025); a woman wound a child's foot in three turns of grey wool below the shade, the foot dark past the ankle, the cold to her and weather to the neighbour with a pail, and the boy's exact reading of the extremity going before the core, the grimace that is a smile and not peace and the afterdrop waiting past a warming nobody would perform, was worth nothing at two paces with no instrument but wool, while the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set at the out-of-line landing for a measure nobody called with his cuff grey from washing and grey again by midday, his slate inward and his hand to it before his eyes to the stalls, and a girl with a creel took the landing sideways without a fall (T093/T094/T096, T090/T080 carried); at the going of the light she carried him up the river road and set him on his bed, and one hundred and fifty-five stood exact, complete and unspent with the crust and its empty place unnamed, the charcoal waiting under the board and the fire missing its fifth with its payer unnamed for a hundred and fifty-five days.
Day 156 | Tallow 7, Y.H. 813 (Seventhday) | the Clay Yard and the kiln mouth, the yard well and the bare trough, the drying racks and the edge of the worked strips, the flagstones, the Vail kiln-house hearth and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | A woman raking the kiln mouth in the morning spoke sideways to nobody, as a person speaks to a wall in the presence of a child who may repeat it, of a man in Mud End found in his sleep, quiet as a lamb, and them that found him called it merciful, and nobody asked which man, said what had taken him or put a figure to the quietness, while the rake and the work went on and the danger had a name in the yard, the kiln's breath, spoken sideways once and put down with the tool that had said it (T088); the boy, one hundred and fifty-six days old and five months and six days and spoken to by nobody, read it as a theatre he could not enter, heat kept and counted behind a screen of brick in four low pulls with a silence, a gate with two bites, and a man gone head first with the skin doing what the vessels under it do, the core holding on after the extremities had given out and the almost-smile settling on the face, and the knowing was exact and worth nothing with the heat two paces off, no payer named to the silence and no lesson made of the lamb; at the well the bucket came up with a slap against the staves and the trough stood bare and dark under its little roof with the skim like fat set aside in a dish gone these four days, and one woman tasted the water from her palm and said it was different, another said Different from what it was, a third called it weather, and the argument ended not in a winner but in nobody left to carry it, the change standing as a shape with no name and no cause entered in no slate; the yard went around the clean dark line and the finer grey called the thaw, the broken birth-slates beneath the lowest rack and the empty place in the returning line where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out, a woman looking once, taking a wide step and saying nothing and another taking the step behind her without looking, no number, name, body, cause, figure or payment coming out of it and no fourth likeness made a cause (T009/T013/T092, T098 carried); Tarn took the broom only after instruction and said Yes, house to a woman who wanted the thaw early and a man who wanted it late, agreeing so thoroughly that the argument had nowhere to put a foot while going on wanting the late one in the private part of his head because a late thaw puts off the paying, his seventh year nearer its end than it had ever stood and still held with six counted where the house could check and the seventh where it could not, the knife at the lip of its sill, the unforged sentence beside it, the Seed 16 breath unspent, his chest tight and Ash in his knuckle creases, and his stroke went on past the shallow bright patch without the half-check and over the road a house sends a man down when it wants a collector without once turning along it, no collector coming and no tenth standing up (T036/T076); at the going of the light Sella carried him up the clay road at the half-pace, went around the pan's empty place, the bare trough and wide around the rack space, set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread frozen and unwiped and let her hand rest a half-beat longer than the setting needed before it went away, the only thing in that room that admitted anything, then told the fire it was the thaw, turned the coals with the back of a broken pot, said The house will keep it, counted under her breath toward no opening, pushed coal-black hair back with the burn-wrist and the grey streak pale off the coals, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand, did not touch the widow's mark because a dusk is not an opening, and went to her own bed, the two rooms not touching and one hundred and fifty-six standing exact, complete and unspent with the fire's fifth unnamed for a hundred and fifty-six days (T058/T079, T088).
Day 157 | Tallow 8, Y.H. 813 (Eighthday) | the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf seen from the basket's low angle, the hearth, the bed under the roof, the Clay Yard and the going of the light on the clay road, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The underside of the loaf shelf became a roof at the angle where the basket sat and the boy, one hundred and fifty-seven days old and five months and seven days and told by nobody, watched the loaves go in the order a row keeps and not as a number, the brown one, the pale one, the cracked corner set last so the crack would not travel, and the space at the end where a loaf had stood and was not standing, for no woman called that space a number because calling it would have made one, while the house kept its days in loaves pushed along a board, a glance at the clay jar cold under its glaze and that space at the row's end (T071/T072, T060/T086); morning came as hot clay under tallow, wet wool and the small mineral edge of the Ash ridge along the skirting board that no broom had been instructed about, and Sella moved a loaf an inch and then an inch back with her lips moving along the row as if reading a line in some other room of her head, her eye going once to the jar as a hand goes to an instrument before the case is opened, and she did not look into the basket or speak to it (T058/T079); a neighbour with a wet pot asked what a firing would cost set down there, asking the room rather than Sella, and Sella answered without turning Ask the pot what a wet thing costs set down there. The house has said it, and the woman carried the question away level as a woman carries a full pail so nothing slops, a cost asked of a pot being a cost put where no Court thumb will find it, and the boy did not join the exchange to the two shut-room tellings standing side by side behind the wall because a joining would want a third telling and no third had come (T077, T065/T095); then Sella stood with her back to the basket and, moving her lips along the row, reached a finger BENEATH the front edge where the charcoal tally lies without looking down and without a word, brought it away and did not go back to the shelf, and the boy saw the hand go under and come back and saw nothing of what the marks said, for such a hand either knows the board by heart or has been told not to look and the reason stood exactly between the two, a keeping put where nobody expects to be read telling more by the care of its hiding than the writing would, and he did not read, copy or enter the marks because a figure is a thing that must be paid by somebody and no payer had been named to the charcoal since the first Account-day of Reed (T100 carried, unread); the shelf kept its two unnamed gaps, the old space at the row's end and the palm's breadth that came in on the night of Seed 6, like two teeth gone from the same jaw, and no third came, while the stiff brown-edged crust lay two inches from his ear with its empty place unnamed and no third, and the pan's gone place kept the round shape of its absence on a tray of its own as a missing instrument keeps its hollow in a towel, not laid across the shelf's gaps, the crust, the field's empty place, the sixth green as a colour joined to no ear or the foundling as a shape with no sale, because a fourth likeness would make a cause and a cause would want a payer and no finger of his drew the line (T097, T050/T051 held, T033/T034 held); at the hearth she told the fire it was the thaw, turned the coals with the back of a broken pot, said The house will keep it, stood a moment counting under her breath toward no opening, pushed coal-black hair back with the burn-wrist and the grey streak pale off the coals, set the pot rim out with its handle two inches beyond the reaching hand, did not look toward the bed and did not touch the widow's mark because a dusk is not an opening and a hearth is not a door, and one hundred and fifty-seven stood exact, complete and unspent with the fire missing its fifth for a hundred and fifty-seven days (T058/T079, T088).
Day 158 | Tallow 9, Y.H. 813 (Ninthday), the eve of the Account-day | the Clay Yard, the trough and the drying racks, the wheel-shed edge, the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf and the bed under the roof, the clay road toward Greybank, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The firing month had got into the Clay Yard by its second working week and made itself at home there, and word went around from mouth to ear that the morrow was the first Account-day of Tallow, that there would be a market at the Cross, that the slate would be read twice, once for the moot and once for the households that came late, and that eight pigs would go over the weighing board with nobody asking which eight were meant, none of it spoken as a thing that wanted anything of her; Berr Clay was spoken of as a direction up the clay road toward High Kiln, not as a place anybody meant to go, his red wool cord of office folded square under his coat, his printed one in twelve standing unchallenged until the reading and his box locked up the road with the true subtraction inside, and a man came up from the road below the well with a number shut in his fist and asked what the firing would cost set down there, and the number went from fist to mouth to shoulder and up the road and arrived nowhere, the road taking it as water takes a thing thrown in and gives back no shape (T015/T041/T047, T077); then she came through the yard from the direction of the Cross with a narrow runner's slate, Hessa Rale of Slate Row, who had walked the road between the Cross and Greybank often enough to have stopped measuring it and came at the pace of a person who has made a bargain with distance, her coat shortened at the skirt for the road's mud with the dried pale scurf unbrushed because a runner's coat is a tool and nobody scours a tool for its own sake, a strap crossed her chest and a second shorter strap let out to take the weight when the road was long, and her hands the hands of a woman who carries and does not open, fingers closed on the slate's edge with a working looseness, thumb along the grain of the frame, nothing pressing where the writing was; she held the slate edge-on to her own forearm so the face turned toward her sleeve and the writing looked at nobody, and her hand went to it before her eyes went to the people in the yard, the small flat correction of the strap and the turn of the wrist coming first, and to a woman who asked what the levy would take now that hands were in the month she said I don't read it. I carry it. There's a difference, and the road doesn't care which, and with her hands on her hips Cross to Greybank. Reading goes up before noon. I've got a road to walk, and went on through with the question standing where it had been set down, no figure coming off the slate and nobody asking her to open it (T080 advanced, not paid); the boy filed the order of reaching as an eighth such hand-shape on a place of its own --- not Marra's pause between strokes at a flare light, not the neat dark hand that sets a name down before a face, not the clerk at the Court's table, not the soft cuff set below the bridge for a measure nobody called, not the soft hands at the fringe of Slate Row, not Berr's twice-wiped thumb, not the finger beneath the loaf shelf in the other house --- eight hands in one district performing one grammar and not one explaining it, and he did not let the number trouble him into a rule because a rule wants a payer and none had been named to any, the edge-on being the manner of carrying a slate so the writing is not rubbed and no goer-by takes a figure off it and not the hiding of a thing from herself; under the roof Tarn took the broom only after instruction, saying Yes, house to an early thaw and a late and going on wanting the late because it puts off the paying, the knife at the very edge of its sill with six counted where the house could check and the seventh where it could not, Garl Vail's unforged sentence beside it, the Seed 16 breath unspent, his seventh year nearer its end than it had ever stood and not given, no tenth stopping standing up, no collector coming and the loan that could hear not called (T036/T076); Jorra came through with the day's grey dust ground small as meal into both forearms, spoke to the wheel and a pot, put his bent finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence and did not look toward the basket, no kiln-master coming up on the wrong wind today to say that a pot was singing before its time, and Tarn did not ask about the room and Jorra did not ask about the ear, the two silences staying unjoined; the loaf shelf kept its two gaps and its unread charcoal, the crust its empty place and no third, and one hundred and fifty-eight stood exact, complete and unspent as the unknown number went up the road and arrived nowhere and the narrow slate went edge-on up the road ahead of it with the writing turned to a runner's sleeve (T071/T072).
Day 159 | Tallow 10, Y.H. 813 (Tenthday), the first Account-day and market-day of Tallow | the Cross, the moot-post and the reading block, the pot board and market stalls, the out-of-line landing below the north bridge, the clay road, the Vail kiln-house hearth and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The tenth of Tallow came up over the Cross grey and close with the river wind under the eaves and hot clay coming down on the same breath from High Kiln, the first Account-day of the firing month and the market come with it the way a cart comes with its load, the pigs already over the weighing board before the light was properly in the boards and potters setting bowls out on the pot board with dust in the hollows of the rims, and the boy, one hundred and fifty-nine days old and five months and nine days and with no reason for anybody in the square to say so, lay in the basket in the moot-post's finger's width of shade with his cheek against cold iron while the crowd leaned over the reading block above him (T071/T072, T058/T079); Sella set the basket where no slate lay and stood a half-pace from every open slate and sent a woman near the pigs who asked what the firing would cost to the pot with Ask the pot what a wet thing costs set down there. The house has said it, carrying the question away level so nothing slopped; Berr Clay came up short off the clay road with the red cord of his office folded square under his coat and the green of tasted lead glaze at one corner of his mouth, his hands large and clean, and wiped his thumb twice before touching nothing, held a narrow slate unopened to his forearm, passed a finger over the figure and let his eyes rest on it a breath longer than the writing required, and read One in twelve, as the notice says low into the goers-by, called the levy and said it was not called by him, told the man with the number in his fist that a number goes into a slate and not a bundle and that if his house wanted it again there would be a slate at the watch, and took himself out of his own sentence with your house, never I, as neatly as a hand goes out of a field before the dressing goes on, while the printed one in twelve stood unchallenged and he went off up the clay road with the box locked and the true subtraction inside, believing he had done the district a kindness so that the figure read was not the figure paid (T015/T041/T047, T077); eight pigs went over the board and a Sill assessor put the figures on a slate with his hand to the slate before his eyes went to the rail, a stall-keeper with meal in her forearm creases took a mark into her palm uncounted before the loaf, people brought numbers to a heap of turnips and left them side by side without making them agree, and the bell went four, four and a gap with nobody crossing himself, the heard bell kept on a separate place beside the told and the boy not calling the market a lesson because a lesson wants a payer (T078); Hessa Rale came through again in the middle of the morning taking the slate up the clay road toward Greybank, her hand to it before her eyes to the rails, the strap set and the wrist turned, the mud dried pale in scurf on the shortened skirt she had not brushed, and to a man who asked what reading had gone up before noon she said Road's long. Reading goes up. Carrying goes out. I've got the one that goes out, giving the direction instead of the figure, the boy filing her as the eighth such hand on a place of its own and the square's grammar being counted as nine hands with Berr's twice-wiped thumb and finger laid on a figure among them on a place of their own, no joining and no payer named to any (T080 advanced, not paid); below the north bridge the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set for a measure nobody called with his cuff grey from washing and grey again by midday, his slate inward and his hand to it before his eyes to the stalls, the market selling over him as water goes over a stone, a girl with a creel taking the landing sideways and no fall made of it, and the boy keeping the two inward records apart as a shape and not a cause (T090/T080); Sella did not shift the basket out of the shade and said nothing about what had been read over him, and at the going of the light carried him home up the clay road, going around the wall where the pan had stood out through Reed, the bare dark trough and the space beneath the lowest rack, while the kiln breathed behind the wall in fours with a gap and the fires of Low Kiln were banked under broken pots, four low pulls apiece and a silence where the fifth should have been with its payer unnamed for a hundred and fifty-nine days (T088, T058/T079); in the small hours he turned out his file as a man lays instruments on a towel --- six hearings beside and never among with no seventh and none invented, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, the older intervals with the unchecking stroke and the held breath, nine stoppings of the near-saying as objects and not a rule with no payer named to any, no tenth before the seventh year was earned and the loan uncalled, the damper's double bite joined to no kiln, the two shut-room tellings with no third, the told bell beside the heard --- and in the other room the shelf kept its two unnamed gaps and its unread charcoal, the crust its empty place and no third, the pan's gone place its round ring on a tray of its own, the sixth green a colour joined to no ear and the foundling a shape with no sale, so that three counts in three places --- the river's note, the fire's four and a gap, the boards' four nails, four and a knot --- did not become one, and one hundred and fifty-nine stood exact, complete and unspent as his hand went up an inch toward the note of water over water and came down against the fold, the fire missing its fifth under the floor and the charcoal waiting under the shelf for a hand that was not his and a day that has not come (T061/T082, T060/T086, T097, T100).
