Day 160 | Tallow 11, Y.H. 813 (Firstday) | the Clay Yard and kiln mouth, the drying racks, the yard well and the bare trough, the Vail wheel-shed edge, the damper and flagstones, the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf and hearth, the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The first working morning after the Account-day came up with the boy one hundred and sixty days old and five months and ten days and nobody saying so, hot clay under tallow and the kiln breathing in fours with a gap while the carried fire missed its fifth under the floor with its payer unnamed for a hundred and sixty days (T088, T071/T072); Sella Vail gave the instruction to wool, fire, pot and broom and never to the basket, kept her half-pace from every open slate and left the widow's mark untouched because a morning, a shelf and a well are not openings (T058/T079), and the yard went around the clean dark line, the new finer grey called the thaw, the broken birth-slates under the lowest rack and the empty place in the returning line where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out, with a look and a wide step and no number (T009/T013/T092, T098 carried); the trough stood bare and dark and was called weather while the clay jar stood cold under its glaze, glanced at once and not touched, and Tarn took the broom only after it had been instructed with six counted where the house can check and the seventh at the lip of the sill where it cannot, his stroke going over the flagstones past the shallow bright patch without even the half-check and over the collector's road without turning, no collector coming and the loan uncalled (T036/T076); the boy read the firing as a theatre he could not enter, heat kept and counted behind a screen and a gate with two bites with a cost paid by a man nobody named (T062), and in the afternoon under the roof Sella sent a wet-pot neighbour's cost to the pot with The house has said it and reached a finger BENEATH the loaf shelf's front edge where the charcoal lay, without looking, and brought it away unread (T077, T100); at the going of the light she set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread frozen, let her hand rest a half-beat longer than the setting needed, told the fire it was the thaw and The house will keep it, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand and touched no mark, and in the small hours his file held six hearings with no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and not paid and nine stoppings with no payer named to any, so that one hundred and sixty stood exact, complete and unspent as his hand went up an inch toward the note of water and came down against the fold, the charcoal waiting under the shelf for a hand that was not his and a day that has not come.
Day 161 | Tallow 12, Y.H. 813 (Secondday) | the Clay Yard, the Vail wheel-shed and its finger's-width-open back room (not entered), the cold damper, the flagstones, the drying racks, the Vail kiln-house loaf shelf, hearth and kindling, the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Before first grey three or four men came up with their collars high against the sweetish wrong wind and went low-voiced into the back room, which kept its finger's width of open, and Sella Vail carried past at the half-pace, turned no head, gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold while the two tellings of the shut counting room stood side by side with no third (T035/T031, T065/T095); Jorra Vail came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent little finger and under the nails, not bone-coloured slip, keeping dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and asking neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, spoke to the wheel before anyone (It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady), told a pot You'll do, knew its full note and withheld it from a High Kiln kiln-master who came up on the wrong wind with Ash pale on his cuffs and said the pot was singing before its time and would sing itself out early, the knowing going to the near-full tray and not among the hearings, and no pot was lost in the yard (T061/T082, T009/T013/T092); then the chapter's one meaningful change: Sella had begun The fire's low. It wants--- and stopped, and for the first time under the roof Jorra's bent finger went to the cold iron and stopped with it, standing with his weight on the balls of his feet while she finished It wants feeding, and only then moved the tongue a hair's breadth and left it standing, the household straightening around a sentence allowed to finish as it had straightened around his silences for three years and nobody saying a word of the waiting, the boy filing the fraction on a place of its own as a change held and not spoken, not a reconciliation, a hearing or a payment, with no payer named and no slate entered while he's not Vail stayed sealed and the promise to the slate-burners stayed ungiven (T062, T035/T031, T061/T082); Tarn took the broom only after instruction, said Yes, house, carried the lateness of the thaw in the private part of his head because it puts off the paying, went over the flagstones past the shallow bright patch without the half-check and over the collector's road without turning, no collector coming, and at the kindling told the wood Six counted. The next I don't count, bringing the ear-sentence only as far as his teeth and drawing the Seed 16 breath without spending it so that nine stoppings stood and no tenth before the seventh year was earned and the loan that could hear stayed uncalled (T036/T076, T061/T082); the boy read the damper as one iron with two bites joined to no kiln with no Grathic word for both mercies, held the pan's gone place, the shelf's two gaps, the crust's empty place and the field's empty place on separate trays with no third gap and no fourth likeness made a cause (T097, T060/T086), and at the going of the light she did not go back to the shelf and the charcoal waited unread, and one hundred and sixty-one stood exact, complete and unspent as his hand went up an inch toward the river's note and came down against the fold, the fire missing its fifth with its payer unnamed for a hundred and sixty-one days (T088).
Day 162 | Tallow 13, Y.H. 813 (Thirdday) | the clay road downriver, the Weir plank walk on posts, the flat blessing stone, the lower boards, the fish-trade shade below the sill, the out-of-line landing below the north bridge, the Vail kiln-house hearth and bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The last of the reeds had gone downriver before the boy did and the work had stopped being a bundle and become a direction counted in the carrying arm, which kept the weight and not the cost, and when a woman asked what the reeds would want Sella Vail answered with the river and the weather so that no entry stood anywhere (T071/T072, T077); she set the basket at the edge of the work where no slate lay, gave the instruction to the wool and the running water and never to the basket and kept her half-pace, and on the flat blessing stone the reed-cutter straightened his grey thumb-oiled cord, turned it once so the knots stood, tied one low with his teeth, bit the end off and put it back inside his coat with the flat of the hand, telling the weir woman who could not read it You can ask the cord and No more can the Court, no knot counted and no figure coming out of it (T099); Orra Weir, hands like old rope, pale eel scars uncovered and salt white at her wrists, pulled one knot down with a small flat knock and gave the ninth of Ilmen where a figure was asked, the collector's boat round the bend on the flood with six oars and one man at the stern who never touched them, saying It's a date. The river takes what the Court doesn't. You can shout at either one. I've tried, her forty-first double turn legible, tail tucked, left over right with the weight of a thumb, the word forty unspoken and her thumb's return left undecided as a lie or the habit of counting (T024/T059/T025); Mira Silt, slime dried grey on her sleeves and silt pale in her neck's hollow with her head held a little right where the top of her left ear had been and her small smile kept like a door on the latch, answered a thin run with Thin? Who's to say thin? It comes when it comes. Ask the river, flicked eel-bones while deciding whether to lie and told a buyer Two marks the pair and two short. You've said it twice and I've counted it twice. There's nothing in the sentence that wants an answer, her thumb under her apron to the grey cord and the second twist shown twice beneath a shifting creel, neither knot counted and her board beside Orra's not touching (T059/T025); below the sill a child's foot stayed dark in three turns of wool and the boy's exact reading of the extremity going before the core, the cold vessel that cannot clot, the foot not rubbed and not set to the fire and the afterdrop waiting past a warming nobody would perform, bought nothing at two paces with no silt-leach, cause or cure named (T093/T094/T096), while the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set at the out-of-line landing for a measure nobody called, cuff grey from washing and grey again by midday, hand to slate before eyes to stalls, the market selling over him and a girl with a creel taking the landing sideways without a fall (T090/T080); at the going of the light Sella carried him home up the clay road, woodsmoke before and river wind behind, going wide around the pan's gone place, the bare dark trough and the space beneath the lowest rack with no explanation to the basket, set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, left the thread frozen, let her hand rest a half-beat longer than the setting needed, told the fire it was the thaw and The house will keep it, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand and did not touch the widow's mark because a dusk is not an opening (T058/T079); in the small hours his file held six hearings with no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, nine stoppings with no payer named to any, the damper's double bite, the fraction from the morning before held and unspoken, the two shut-room tellings with no third and the told bell beside the heard, and three counts in three places --- river, fire and boards --- did not become one, so that one hundred and sixty-two stood exact, complete and unspent as his hand went up an inch toward the note of water over water and came down against the fold, the fire missing its fifth with its payer unnamed for a hundred and sixty-two days and the charcoal waiting under the shelf for a hand that was not his and a day that has not come (T061/T082, T060/T086, T097, T100, T088).
Day 163 | Tallow 14, Y.H. 813 (Fourthday) | under the Vail kiln-house roof at the hearth and the bed, the kiln mouth, the Clay Yard, the yard well, the drying racks and the flagstones, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The third working week opened with the boy one hundred and sixty-three days old and five months and thirteen days and nobody under any roof saying so, the first smell hot clay under tallow and the first sound the kiln's breath in fours with a gap rather than a bell, the carried fire under the floor answering four low pulls with the silence where the fifth should have been and its payer unnamed for a hundred and sixty-three days (T071/T072, T088), while Sella Vail gave every instruction to the wool, the fire, the pot and the broom and never to the basket, said the house where another woman would say I, kept her half-pace from every open slate and left the widow's mark hanging where it had hung, for a morning and a shelf are not openings (T058/T079), and the women went around the clean dark line where the Ash ridge had turned, the finer grey called the thaw, the space beneath the lowest rack where the broken birth-slates lay and the empty place in the returning line where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out, with a look and a wide step and no number (T009/T013/T092, T098 carried), the trough at the well standing bare and dark and the water called weather while the clay jar stood cold under its glaze, glanced at once and not touched; Tarn took the broom only after it had been instructed, six counted where the house could check and the seventh at the lip of the sill where it could not, his stroke going over the flagstones past the shallow bright patch without the half-check and over the collector road without turning, no collector coming, nine stoppings still nine and the loan uncalled (T036/T076), and the boy read the firing as a theatre he could not enter, heat kept and counted behind a screen and a gate of two bites with a cost paid by a man whose name was not spoken (T062), the river's fifth low note through the finger's-width door, the fire's gap under the floor and the boards' four nails, four, and a knot the width of a thumb kept as three counts in three places that did not become one, his hand going up an inch toward the note and coming down against the fold with one hundred and sixty-three exact, complete and unspent.
Day 164 | Tallow 15, Y.H. 813 (Fifthday) | the Vail wheel-shed, its finger's-width-open back room (not entered), the flagstones, the cold damper, the drying racks, the kiln-house loaf shelf and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Before first grey three or four men went low up the Clay Yard with their collars high against the sweetish wind and into the back room, which kept its finger's width of open, and Sella Vail carried past at the half-pace, turned no head, gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold, while the two shut-room tellings stood side by side with no third (T035/T031, T065/T095); Jorra Vail came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent little finger and under the nails, not bone-coloured slip, kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray, asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, spoke to the wheel before anyone (It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady), told a pot You'll do, knew its full note before it left the wheel and withheld the knowing from a High Kiln kiln-master who came up on the wrong wind with Ash pale on his cuffs and said a pot singing before its time generally sings itself out early, the knowing going to the near-full answers advanced and not paid and not among the six hearings, no pot lost in the yard and no lesson made of the saying (T061/T082); then, as on the morning of Day 161, when Sella's voice went low at the hearth Jorra's bent finger went to the cold iron of the damper and stood there while she finished It wants feeding, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and left it standing, the household straightening around a sentence allowed to finish and nobody saying a word of the waiting or naming a payer to the fraction (T062), the boy keeping it on the place where it had been put and not laying it across anything else; Tarn took the broom only after it had been given its instruction and went over the flagstones without the half-check and over the collector road without turning, no collector coming, and at the going of the light one hundred and sixty-four stood exact, complete and unspent while the fire missed its fifth with its payer unnamed for a hundred and sixty-four days.
Day 165 | Tallow 16, Y.H. 813 (Sixthday) | the Clay Yard, the drying racks, the yard well, the kindling, the flagstones and the hearth under the Vail kiln-house roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The firing month made itself at home in the Clay Yard with the danger present in the dust ground small as meal on a man's forearms, the sweetish lead edge on a wrong wind off High Kiln and the kiln's breath in fours with its gap, gathering as heat under banked ash toward an edge that could be paid later and not paid now; Sella carried the boy at the half-pace and gave the instruction to the wool, the fire, the pot and the broom and never to the basket, leaving the widow's mark untouched, while the women went around the clean dark line, the finer grey called the thaw, the broken birth-slates under the lowest rack and the empty place in the returning line, the trough standing bare and dark and the water called weather, the clay jar glanced at once and not touched; Tarn took the broom only after it was instructed, said Yes, house to an early thaw and a late while going on wanting the late because a late thaw puts off the paying, his stroke going over the flagstones past the shallow bright patch without even the half-check and over the road a house sends a man down when it wants a collector without once turning along it, no collector coming; at midday a kiln-hand crouched by the kindling and said sideways, as a person speaks to a wall in the presence of a child who might repeat it, that a man in Mud End had been taken quiet in his sleep and them that found him called it merciful, and nobody asked which man, said what had taken him, put a figure to the quietness or made a lesson of the lamb (T077); at the going of the light Tarn banked the coals under the back of a broken pot, set the small knife at the very edge of its sill with Garl's unforged collector sentence beside it, six counted where the house could check and the seventh where it could not, and told the wood Six counted. The next I don't count, bringing the sentence about an ear under this roof being worth more than the debt that holds it all the way to his teeth and no further, drawing the breath from Seed 16 and not spending it, no tenth stopping standing up out of the nine, the seventh year pressed nearer its end and still held and the loan that could hear uncalled (T036/T076, T061/T082); the warm stone Sella had given him in the winter Garl died lay two years gone and cold in a clay pot in the loft, kept and not reconciled, which is not forgiving (T006 held), and the boy read the firing as a theatre with a gate of two bites he could not enter, one hundred and sixty-five standing exact, complete and unspent while the fire missed its fifth with its payer unnamed for a hundred and sixty-five days.
Day 166 | Tallow 17, Y.H. 813 (Seventhday) | the loaf shelf seen from the basket's low angle, the hearth, the kindling and the bed under the Vail kiln-house roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The afternoon came into the kiln-house along the low angle where the underside of the loaf shelf became a roof over the basket, and the boy, one hundred and sixty-six days old and five months and sixteen days and told by nobody, set that count against the house's arithmetic in loaves pushed along a board, a glance at the clay jar cold under its glaze and a space at the row's end nobody called a number; Sella set a wet pot down where no loaf lay, moved her lips along the row and sent a neighbour with water on her sleeves who asked what a firing cost to the pot with Ask the pot what a wet thing costs set down there. The house has said it, carrying the unanswered question away level so nothing slopped, the boy filing the shape beside the other answers that had had their questions taken out and keeping it from standing up among them into a cause (T077); then came the chapter's one meaningful change: standing with her back to the basket and moving her lips along the row, she reached a finger BENEATH the front edge where the charcoal tally lies in clusters pressed hard or faded nearly into the grain, as on Days 151, 157 and 160, but this time **held the finger under the board for the length of one of the breaths she counted** --- the small intake at the top, the hold, the letting go --- before bringing it away, without looking, reading, copying or giving a word and without going back to the shelf when the light went, and the boy saw the held beat from the low angle and filed it on a place of its own as a change held and unspoken, not a reading of the charcoal, not a payment, with no payer named to it, keeping it from the Day 161 fraction at the damper and the older intervals at Stone 13, 17 and 25 so that no likeness stood up and called itself a rule, for a rule is an account that has found a payer and no payer stood to any of them (T100 carried and advanced, not paid; T061/T082); the shelf kept its two unnamed gaps, the old space at the row's end and the palm's breadth that came in on the night of Seed 6, the crust in the fold kept its empty place unnamed, the pan's gone place kept its round absence on a tray of its own, the sixth green stayed a colour joined to no ear and the foundling a shape with no sale, and no third gap came and no fourth likeness was made a cause (T060/T086, T097); at the going of the light she set him on his bed under the roof and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread frozen and unwiped, let her hand rest a half-beat longer than the setting needed, told the fire it was the thaw, said The house will keep it, counted under her breath toward no opening, pushed coal-black hair back with the burn-wrist, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand and left the widow's mark untouched, because a dusk is not an opening and the two rooms do not touch (T058/T079); in the small hours he turned out the file as a man lays instruments on a towel --- six hearings beside and never among with no seventh and none invented, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, the older intervals, nine stoppings as objects and not a rule with no payer and no tenth before the seventh year was earned, the damper's double bite joined to no kiln, the Tallow 12 fraction and this evening's held beat on separate places, the two shut-room tellings with no third and the told bell beside the heard bell --- and one hundred and sixty-six stood exact, complete and unspent while the charcoal waited under the shelf for a hand that was not his and a day that has not come.
Day 167 | Tallow 18, Y.H. 813 (Eighthday) | the clay road downriver to the Weir, the plank walk on posts, the flat blessing stone, the lower boards, the fish-trade shade below the sill, the out-of-line landing below the north bridge, the clay road home and the bed under the Vail kiln-house roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The finished reeds had gone out of the day and lived only in the carrying arm, which kept the weight and not the cost, and a woman who asked what the last of the cutting would want was answered with the river and the weather so that no entry stood anywhere (T071/T072, T077); Sella set the basket at the edge of the work where no slate lay, gave the instruction to the wool and the running water and never to the basket and kept her half-pace; on the flat blessing stone the reed-cutter straightened his wool-grey cord gone dark with a working thumb, turned it once so the knots stood, tied one low with his teeth and a pull of the thumb, bit the end off and put it back inside his coat with the flat of the hand, telling the weir woman who asked what it held You can ask the cord and No more can the Court, without anger or a smile and with no knot counted or figure given, his hand to the cord before the trap and before the woman (T099 carried); Orra Weir, hands like old rope, pale eel scars uncovered, salt white at her wrists and the nasal Fen half-song flat in her voice where she was tired, pulled one knot down with a small flat knock --- the knock of a thing laid where it belonged, which the boy kept apart from the small flat knock of a vessel cracked all the way through --- and gave the woman who asked what the levy would take now the hands were out of the reeds the date The ninth of Ilmen. The collector's boat came round the bend on the flood and had six oars. One man stood at the stern and never touched them, and to That isn't the levy, It's a date. The river takes what the Court doesn't. You can shout at either one. I've tried, her forty-first double turn legible, tail tucked, left over right with the weight of a thumb, the word forty unspoken and her thumb's return left undecided as a lie or the habit of counting (T024/T059/T025); Mira Silt, slime dried grey in long strokes on her sleeves and silt pale in the hollow of her neck, her head held a little right where the top of her left ear had been and her small smile like a door on the latch, answered a thin run with Thin? Who's to say thin? It comes when it comes. Ask the river, flicked eel-bones while deciding whether to lie and told a buyer who named a figure Two marks the pair and two short. You've said it twice and I've counted it twice. There's nothing in the sentence that wants an answer, her thumb under her apron to the grey cord longer than the figure required and the second twist twice beneath a shifted creel, neither knot counted and her board beside Orra's and not touching, and she did not look into the basket; below the sill in the fish-trade shade a woman wound a child's foot in three turns of wool and went on winding after the foot had gone dark, the cold to one woman and weather to the yard, and the boy read the extremity going before the core, a cold vessel that will not clot and the afterdrop waiting past a warming nobody would perform, exact and worth nothing at two paces with no silt-leach, cause or cure named (T093/T094/T096); at the out-of-line landing the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set for a measure nobody called, his cuff grey from washing and grey again by midday, his slate turned inward and his hand to it before his eyes to the stalls, the market selling over him as water over a stone that lies where a stone lies and a girl with a creel taking the landing sideways without a fall or help (T090/T080); at the going of the light Sella carried him home up the clay road, woodsmoke before her and river wind behind, going wide around the pan's gone place, the bare dark trough and the space beneath the lowest rack without explaining any of the turns, and under the roof set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread frozen, let her hand rest a half-beat longer than the setting needed, told the fire it was the thaw, said The house will keep it, counted under her breath toward no opening, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand and left the widow's mark untouched; in the small hours the file was exact and worthless with the Day 161 fraction and the held beat beneath the shelf on separate places, and one hundred and sixty-seven stood exact, complete and unspent while the fire missed its fifth with its payer unnamed for a hundred and sixty-seven days.
Day 168 | Tallow 19, Y.H. 813 (Ninthday), the eve of the reading | the Clay Yard, the yard well, the drying racks and the edge of the worked strips, the edge of the Cross with the reading block under the moot-post, the flagstones, the hearth, the loaf shelf and the bed under the Vail kiln-house roof, the clay road home, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The eve of the reading drew the district's arithmetic together without making it agree: a number came up out of a fist at the well and went back into a sleeve, a row of loaves gave up its count to a woman's lips and nobody's mouth, a grey cord a league downriver kept its knots where no Court thumb would spell them, the carried fire under this roof answered four low pulls and kept the silence where the fifth should have been and the river kept a fifth low thing never counted among the hearings, all of it exact and none of it entered (T077, T071/T072, T088), and the boy lay in the basket and let the figures stand where they stood because a likeness in a row will call itself an account if no hand holds it apart and an account once it stands wants a name and a payer; Sella gave the instruction to the wool, the fire, the pot and the broom before anybody and never to the basket, said the house and kept her half-pace from every open slate even though there was no slate open in the yard, and the women went around the clean dark line, the finer grey called the thaw, the broken birth-slates beneath the lowest rack and the field's empty place where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out, with a look and a wide step and no number (T009/T013/T092, T098 carried); at the well a woman moved her lips over a row of loaves and gave nobody a figure, a buyer brought a number up out of his fist, looked at it and put it back in his sleeve so that it arrived nowhere, a neighbour with a wet pot was sent to the pot with Ask the pot what a wet thing costs set down there. The house has said it, and a kiln-hand raking a kiln mouth spoke sideways of a man in Mud End taken quiet in his sleep and called it merciful, and nobody made a lesson of it, put a figure to the quietness or entered a cause across the saying; at the edge of the Cross the square stood quiet with nobody leaning over the reading block, the pot board keeping the small round of dust where a cup had once stood, and a woman crossing with a pot said only that there would be a slate at the watch if a house wanted its figure again and went on with her pot; under the roof Tarn's seventh year stood nearer its end than it had ever stood and had still not given, six counted where the house could check and the seventh at the lip of the sill where it could not, the knife at the very edge with Garl's unforged collector sentence beside it, the Seed 16 breath unspent at the top of each reach, his stroke going over the flagstones past the shallow bright patch without even the half-check and over the collector road without turning, no collector coming, nine stoppings objects and not a rule with no payer named to any and no tenth before the seventh year was earned, the ear-sentence coming to his teeth and no further and the loan uncalled (T036/T076, T061/T082); Jorra came through with dust ground small as meal on both forearms, spoke to the wheel before anyone and told a pot You'll do, and when Sella's instruction came low put his bent finger into the cold iron of the damper and left it standing while her sentence arrived at the pot, not asking about the ear and not looking toward the basket; the boy set the Tallow 12 fraction at the damper and the held beat beneath the loaf shelf on two places of their own and would not let them make a pair, held two waiting hands apart as a surgeon holds two instruments apart on a towel, and kept the crust's empty place, the shelf's two gaps, the pan's gone place, the field's empty place, the sixth green as a colour and the foundling as a shape on separate trays with no third gap and no fourth likeness made a cause (T062, T100, T060/T086, T097); at the going of the light she carried him home up the clay road, going wide around the pan's gone place, the bare dark trough and the rack space without explaining the turns, set him on his bed under the roof, put the fold right, left the thread frozen, let her hand rest a half-beat longer than the setting needed, told the fire it was the thaw and The house will keep it, counted under her breath toward no opening, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand and left the widow's mark untouched, and in the small hours one hundred and sixty-eight stood exact, complete and unspent, spoken by nobody under any roof, the day that would bring the slate not named among the counts and the charcoal waiting under the shelf for a hand that was not his and a day that has not come.
Day 169 | Tallow 20, Y.H. 813 (Tenthday, the second Account-day and market-day of Tallow) | the Cross, the reading block under the moot-post, the moot-post's finger's width of shade, the pot board and market stalls, the clay road, the out-of-line landing below the north bridge and the bed under the Vail kiln-house roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The second Account-day and market-day came up over the Cross grey and close with river wind under the eaves and hot clay coming down on the same breath from High Kiln, eight pigs going over the board before the light was properly in the boards and potters setting bowls on the pot board, and the boy, one hundred and sixty-nine days old and five months and nineteen days and told by nobody, lay in the moot-post's finger's width of shade with cold iron at his cheek while the crowd leaned over the reading block above him (T071/T072); Berr Clay came up short off the clay road with the red wool cord of his office folded square under his coat and the green of tasted lead glaze at one corner of his mouth, his hands large and clean, wiped his thumb twice on his apron before touching nothing, once for the slate and once for the figure that would be on it, held a narrow slate unopened to his forearm with the writing to his sleeve, passed a finger over the figure and let his eyes rest on it a breath longer than the writing required, and read One in twelve, as the notice says low into the crowd, saying The levy is called. It is not called by me and to the man with the number in his fist A number goes into a slate, not into a bundle and if your house wants it again there'll be a slate at the watch --- your house, never I --- taking himself out of his own sentence as a surgeon takes a hand out of a field before the dressing goes on and going back to the pigs by the bell (T015/T041/T047, T077); the Sill assessor wrote the pigs' figures down with hand to slate before eyes to the rail and then to his mouth, a buyer brought a number up out of his coat toward the weighing board, looked at it and put it back in his sleeve so that it arrived nowhere, and the printed figure stood unchallenged while the true subtraction went back up the clay road in the locked box, the figure read still not the figure paid, the two records kept in two places and not made to agree (T077); the bell went four, four and a gap with nobody crossing himself, the heard bell kept beside the told and its missing fifth not laid across the kiln's missing pull or the boards' knot (T078); Hessa Rale came through going up the clay road with Berr's narrow slate held edge-on to her forearm and the writing to her sleeve, her hand to it before her eyes went to the crowd, the small flat correction of the strap and the turn of the wrist bringing the edge round, her coat shortened at the skirt for the road's mud with the dried pale scurf unbrushed, two straps settled and the thumb along the grain with nothing pressing where the writing was, and to the woman who asked what she carried said I don't read it. I carry it. There's a difference, and the road doesn't care which and to the next question Cross to Greybank. Reading goes up before noon. I've got a road to walk, answering the question that had been put and not the one behind it, without rudeness or apology; no figure came off the slate, and the boy filed the order of her reaching as the eighth such hand in the district on a place of its own, joined to none of Marra's pause, the neat dark hand, the Court clerk, the soft-cuffed clerk, the soft hands at Slate Row, Berr's twice-wiped thumb, the finger beneath the loaf shelf, the reed-cutter's cord or Orra's knot-string, for two records going inward are two records going inward and a shape is not a cause (T080 advanced and not paid); below the north bridge the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set for a measure nobody called, cuff grey from washing and grey again by midday, slate turned inward, hand to it before eyes to stalls, nobody taking him off and the market selling over him as water over a stone that lies where a stone lies, a girl with a creel taking the out-of-line landing sideways, catching the rail and going on selling without once looking at her feet and nobody making a fall of it (T090); at the going of the light Sella carried him home up the clay road, woodsmoke before her and river wind behind, set the basket in the moot-post's finger's width of shade where no slate lay, kept her half-pace from every open slate, gave the wool and the river wind their instructions and never the basket, said nothing of what had been read over him, went wide around the pan's gone place, the trough at the well and the space beneath the lowest rack, and under the roof set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread frozen and unwiped, let her hand rest a half-beat longer than the setting needed, told the fire it was the thaw, said The house will keep it, counted under her breath toward no opening, pushed coal-black hair back with the burn-wrist, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand and left the widow's mark untouched, because a dusk is not an opening and the two rooms do not touch (T058/T079); in the small hours the file was exact and worthless --- six hearings beside and never among with no seventh and none invented, three near-full answers advanced and not paid with the wheel-keeper's knowing among them, the older intervals, nine stoppings as objects and not a rule with no payer and no tenth before the seventh year was earned, the damper's double bite joined to no kiln, the Tallow 12 fraction and the Day 166 held beat beneath the shelf on separate places, the two shut-room tellings with no third and the told bell beside the heard bell --- the crust's empty place, the shelf's two gaps, the pan's gone place, the field's empty place, the sixth green as a colour and the foundling as a shape staying on their own trays with no third gap and no fourth likeness made a cause, three counts in three places not becoming one, and one hundred and sixty-nine standing exact, complete and unspent in the dark of the wool as his hand went up an inch toward the note of water over water through the finger's-width door and came down against the fold, the fire missing its fifth under the floor with its payer unnamed for a hundred and sixty-nine days and the charcoal waiting under the shelf for a hand that was not his and a day that has not come.
Day 170 | Tallow 21, Y.H. 813 (Firstday) | under the Vail kiln-house roof at the hearth and the bed, the kiln mouth, the drying racks, the yard well, the flagstones, the kindling and the loaf shelf, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The last working week opened with hot clay under tallow and the kiln breathing in fours with a gap while the carried fire under the floor answered four low pulls and missed its fifth, its payer unnamed for a hundred and seventy days, the boy one hundred and seventy days old and five months and twenty days and nobody under any roof saying so (T071/T072, T088); Sella Vail gave the instruction to the wool, the fire, the pot, the broom and the running water and never to the basket, said the house where another woman would say I, kept her half-pace from every open slate and left the widow's mark where it hung, for a morning and a shelf are not openings, and set the pot rim out with its handle two inches beyond the reaching hand with her hand resting a half-beat longer than the setting needed (T058/T079); the yard went around the clean dark line, the finer grey called the thaw, the broken birth-slates beneath the lowest rack and the field's empty place where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out, with a look and a wide step and no number, the trough bare and dark with the water called weather and the clay jar cold under its glaze, glanced at once and not touched (T009/T013/T092, T098 carried); Tarn took the broom only after it had been instructed and told the kindling Six counted. The next I don't count, with the small knife at the very edge of its sill and Garl's unforged collector sentence beside it, six counted where the house could check and the seventh at the lip where it could not, his chest tight from the dark, Ash worked into his knuckles in the colour of the clean dark line and the pale clip-ring holding what light there was, his stroke going over the flagstones past the shallow bright patch and over the road a house sends a man down when it wants a collector without once turning along it and no collector coming, nine stoppings still nine with no tenth before the seventh year was earned and the Seed 16 breath unspent (T036/T076, T061/T082, T006 held); a pot set down wrong at the racks gave its high thin note before its time and the woman who set it down did not hear it, which he filed as a danger of the month and not as a fourth near-full answer, no room changed and no pot lost; a neighbour with a wet pot who asked what a firing cost set down there was sent to the pot with Ask the pot what a wet thing costs set down there. The house has said it, and carried the question away level, nothing slopped, nothing priced, nothing refused (T077); the charcoal under the loaf shelf waited unread and Sella's finger did not go beneath the board, so the Day 161 fraction and the Day 166 held beat stood on separate places and did not stand up as a rule, three counts in three places and none becoming the others; at the going of the light she carried him home up the clay road, woodsmoke before her and river wind behind, going wide around the pan's gone place, the bare dark trough and the space beneath the lowest rack without explaining the turns, set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face and left the thread of drool frozen and unwiped, and one hundred and seventy stood exact, complete and unspent in the dark of the wool, spoken by nobody under any roof.
Day 171 | Tallow 22, Y.H. 813 (Secondday) | the Vail wheel-shed, the cold damper, the flagstones, the finger's-width-open back room (not entered), the drying racks, the yard well, the loaf shelf and the bed under the Vail kiln-house roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Before first grey three or four men came up the clay road with their collars high and went low into the back room, which kept its finger's width of open, and Sella Vail carried past at the half-pace, turned no head, gave them no name and did not say the room was cold, while the boy did not count men who had been given no name and the two shut-room tellings stood side by side with no third (T035/T031, T065/T095); Jorra Vail came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent little finger and under the nails, not bone-coloured slip, kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray, asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, spoke to the wheel before anyone (It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady), told a pot You'll do, knew its full note three turns before it left his hands and withheld the knowing from a High Kiln kiln-master who came up on the wrong wind and said a pot singing before its time generally sings itself out early, the knowing going to the near-full tray, beside the hearings and never among them, and no pot lost in the yard (T061/T082, T009/T013/T092); then came the chapter's one meaningful change: when Sella's voice went low with The fire's low. It wants---, Jorra's bent finger went to the cold iron of the damper and **held** while she finished It wants feeding, moved the tongue a hair's breadth and left the finger standing in the iron, nobody speaking of the waiting and no payer named to the fraction, and while the finger still stood there Tarn, come up with the broom after it had been instructed, checked his stroke for half a beat behind the standing finger on the flagstones over the shallow bright patch kept since Seed 22, then went on toward the road without a word or a turn of the head, the stones keeping the patch where this stroke too had stopped (T062, T036/T076); the surgeon's register measured the half-beat as the small distance between a hand's intention and a hand's arrest and left it in the margin, and the boy felt the thing before he understood it --- the Seed 22 half-check returned changed after a hundred and fifty days, in the very breath of a sentence allowed to finish and a finger standing in the iron, with both men looking elsewhere --- and filed it on a place of its own **beside** the Day 161 fraction at the damper and the Day 166 held beat beneath the loaf shelf, three held changes and not a pair, still less a doctrine, for a rule is an account that has found a payer and no payer stood to any of the three (T061/T082, T100 kept apart); at the well a neighbour's question about what the firing would want now the kilns were hot was answered with the weather, the pot and the river and no figure, a man's cold and a woman's wet under it ended in weather, and Sella told the fire it was the thaw, turned the coals with the back of a broken pot, said The house will keep it, counted under her breath toward no opening and pushed coal-black hair back with the burn-wrist so the grey streak showed pale, while her finger stayed away from the loaf shelf and the charcoal waited unread (T077, T058/T079); in the small hours the file was laid out as instruments on a towel, six hearings beside and never among with no seventh and none invented, three near-full answers advanced and not paid with Jorra's knowing among them, the older intervals at Stone 13, 17 and 25, and nine stoppings as objects and not a rule with no tenth before the seventh year was earned, the loan uncalled and the Seed 16 breath unspent; one hundred and seventy-one stood exact, complete and unspent in the dark of the wool, the fire missing its fifth with its payer unnamed for a hundred and seventy-one days and the river keeping its fifth low note through the door, three counts in three places and none becoming the others.
Day 172 | Tallow 23, Y.H. 813 (Thirdday) | the Clay Yard, the kiln mouth, the drying racks, the yard well, the kindling and flagstones, the clay road, and the Vail kiln-house hearth, loaf shelf and bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The last working week came into its third day with the kilns already at their heat and the yard smelling of a thing held at a temperature it would not stay at, the danger of the month in the dust ground small as meal, the sweetish edge of lead glaze riding up from High Kiln on a wrong wind and the kiln's breath in fours with its gap, a wet pot giving its high thin note before its time which the boy filed as a danger and not as a fourth near-full answer, no room changed and no pot lost (T088, T061/T082); Tarn took the broom only after it had been given its instruction, set the small knife back at the very edge of its sill and told the kindling Six counted. The next I don't count, six standing in the worn thumb-mark where the house could check and the seventh at the lip where it could not, Garl's unforged collector sentence beside it, his chest tight from the dark, Ash worked into his knuckle creases in the colour of the clean dark line, the pale clip-ring at his left wrist holding what light there was, the warm stone she gave him in the winter Garl died lying cold in its clay pot in the loft, two years gone and not looked at in a year (T036/T076, T006 held); he said Yes, house to an early thaw and a late, agreeing so entirely that no argument could find a foot while going on wanting the late because a late thaw puts off the paying, his broom going over the collector's road without once turning along it and no collector coming, and the sentence about an ear under this roof being worth more than the debt that held it came up into his teeth and went no further, the breath he should have spent drawn and not spent, the loan uncalled, no tenth stopping stood up before the seventh year was earned and something waiting to give at the end of a term that was not yet ended (T036/T076, T061/T082); at the well a woman's question about what the kilns would want now the last week was in was answered with the heat kept, the pot and the river and no figure, and the yard went around the clean dark line, the finer grey called the thaw, the broken birth-slates and the field's empty place with a look and a wide step and no number (T077, T009/T013/T092, T098 carried); Sella told the fire it was the thaw, turned the coals with the back of a broken pot, said The house will keep it, counted under her breath toward no opening, pushed coal-black hair back with the burn-wrist and set the pot rim out with its handle two inches beyond the reaching hand without touching the widow's mark, because a morning and a shelf are not openings (T058/T079); the surgeon's register read the held breath as a clamp left too long and the near-saying stopped at the word as a chart closed over its finding with the neat margin showing, exact and worth nothing at two paces, an inventory that was not a key and a list that was not a hand; at the going of the light she carried him home up the clay road, woodsmoke before her and river wind behind, going wide around the pan's gone place, the bare dark trough and the space beneath the rack, set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread frozen and unwiped, let her hand rest a half-beat longer than the setting needed and went to her own bed, the two rooms not touching; in the small hours he laid out the file with six hearings and no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, nine stoppings with no payer named, the damper as one iron with two bites joined to no kiln, the two shut-room tellings with no third, the told bell beside the heard, and the third movement of the equality on Tallow 22 on a third place beside the Day 161 fraction and the Day 166 held beat --- three held changes and not a doctrine, no finger of his drawing the line between the shelf's two gaps, the crust's empty place, the pan's gone ring and the field's empty place, no third gap and no fourth likeness made a cause (T060/T086, T097, T062/T100); cold came cheek, temple, wrist and stopped, and one hundred and seventy-two stood exact, complete and unspent in the dark of the wool, the payer under the floor without a name for a hundred and seventy-two days, the charcoal waiting under the shelf for a hand that was not his and a day that had not come, as his hand outside the wool went up an inch toward the note of water over water and came down against the fold.
Day 173 | Tallow 24, Y.H. 813 (Fourthday) | under the Vail kiln-house roof at the hearth, the bed and the loaf shelf's low angle, the kiln mouth, the drying racks, the yard well and the flagstones in the Clay Yard, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The last tend opened with hot clay under tallow and wet wool and the sour breath of the unswept corner beneath the sweet dry smell, the boy one hundred and seventy-three days old and five months and twenty-three days and nobody under any roof saying so (T071/T072), the kiln breathing behind the wall in fours with a gap while the carried fire under the floor answered four low pulls and missed its fifth with its payer unnamed for a hundred and seventy-three days (T088), the river's note of water over water coming through the finger's-width door as a fifth low thing never counted among the hearings and the boards keeping four nails, four, and a knot the width of a thumb, three counts in three places and none becoming the others (T061/T082); Sella Vail gave the instruction to the wool, the fire, the pot, the broom and the running water and never to the basket, said the house where another woman would say I, kept her half-pace, turned the coals with the back of a broken pot, told the fire it was the thaw, counted under her breath toward no opening, glanced once at the clay jar cold under its glaze without touching it, pushed coal-black hair back with the burn-wrist so the grey streak showed pale, set the pot rim out with its handle two inches beyond the reaching hand, let her hand rest a half-beat longer than the setting needed and left the widow's mark untouched, for a morning and a shelf are not openings (T058/T079); the yard went around the clean dark line, the finer grey called the thaw, the broken birth-slates beneath the lowest rack, the pan's gone ring and the field's empty place where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out, with a look and a wide step and no number (T009/T013/T092, T097/T098 carried), while a rack woman reached past the bare board with her hand before her eyes found the basket and two neighbours' cold and wet-under-it ended in weather; Tarn took the broom only after it had been instructed, said Yes, house to an early thaw and a late while going on wanting the late because a late thaw puts off the paying, six counted in the worn thumb-mark where the house can check and the seventh at the lip where it cannot, Garl's unforged collector sentence beside the knife, the ear-sentence up into his teeth and no further, the Seed 16 breath unspent, his stroke over the collector's road without once turning along it and no collector coming, the half-check of Tallow 22 left where it was and not repeated (T036/T076, T061/T082); the month's danger took its order in dust ground small as meal, the sweetish lead edge riding a wrong wind off High Kiln and a pot giving its thin note before its time, which the boy filed as a danger and not as a fourth near-full answer, no pot lost, no room changed, the firing read as a theatre he could not enter with heat kept and counted behind a screen and a gate of two bites, his file exact and worthless, an inventory that is not a key and a list that is not a hand, and one hundred and seventy-three standing exact, complete and unspent in the dark of the wool while the charcoal waited under the shelf for a hand that was not his and a day that had not come.
Day 174 | Tallow 25, Y.H. 813 (Fifthday) | the Vail wheel-shed, the cold damper, the flagstones, the drying racks and the yard well in the Clay Yard, the back room (not entered), the kiln-house hearth and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Before first grey three or four men came up with their collars high and went low through the finger's-width door of the back room and Sella Vail carried past at the half-pace, turned no head, gave the house no name for them and did not say the room was cold, while the boy did not count men who had been given no name and the two tellings of a counting room shut in the war stood side by side with no third (T035/T031, T065/T095); Jorra Vail came through with fine grey slate dust ground small as meal into both forearms past the bent little finger and under the nails, white in the creases where a fist had opened and not bone-coloured slip, kept dusty forearms and clean ones on one tray and asked neither which slate had been broken nor whether it was a birth-slate, the broken birth-slates lying under the lowest rack and entered by nobody; he spoke to the wheel before anyone (It doesn't care whose hands. It cares if they're steady), told a pot You'll do, knew the full note it would give three turns before it left his hands and withheld the knowing from a High Kiln kiln-master come up on the wrong wind who said a pot singing before its time generally sings itself out early, the knowing going to the near-full tray, beside the hearings and never among them, advanced and not paid, no pot lost and no lesson made (T061/T082); when Sella's voice went low at the hearth with The fire's low. It wants---, Jorra's bent finger went into the cold iron of the damper and **held** there while she finished feeding and gave the end of the instruction to the pot, then moved the tongue a hair's breadth and left the finger standing, nobody speaking of the waiting and no payer named to the fraction, the holding remembered and not a word and its coming round again not a change twice over (T062); Tarn's stroke went over the shallow bright patch on the flagstones without the half-check and over the collector's road without once turning along it, neither man looking toward the other or naming the other's silence, and the three held changes --- the Day 161 damper fraction, the Day 166 beat beneath the loaf shelf and the Tallow 22 flagstone half-check --- stayed three on three places and did not stand up as a rule, for a rule is an account that has found a payer; at the well a woman's question about what the kilns would want now the last tend was in was answered with the weather, the pot and the river and never a figure, sent to the pot with Ask the pot what a wet thing costs set down there. The house has said it, carried away level so nothing slopped, nothing was priced and nothing refused (T077), and the widow's mark hung where it hung, for a well and a morning are not openings (T058/T079); under the floor the carried fire missed its fifth with its payer unnamed for a hundred and seventy-four days and the river kept its fifth low thing through the door, and one hundred and seventy-four stood exact, complete and unspent, told by nobody, the boy having stopped expecting to be told, which was not the same as not wanting it.
Day 175 | Tallow 26, Y.H. 813 (Sixthday) | the yard well, the drying racks, the worked strips, the loaf shelf and hearth under the Vail kiln-house roof, the clay road at the going of the light and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Midday stood in the Clay Yard with the heat held in the clay and the oiled paper lifting at the kiln-house windows, Sella Vail carrying the boy at the half-pace with instruction to the wool, the running water and the river wind and never to the basket, and a neighbour with a pot just out of the tubs asking what a firing cost set down in the Vail house being answered, after one glance at the cold glazed jar and a hand that shut half over nothing without touching it, with Ask the pot what a wet thing costs set down there. The house has said it, the question going away level in the arm that carried it, nothing slopped, nothing priced, nothing refused, an answer given as a direction that arrived nowhere and left no entry (T077, T058/T079); two neighbours settled the morning between a cold and a wet one under it and the argument ended in weather with nobody owning either day, while the yard went around the clean dark line, the finer grey called the thaw, the space beneath the lowest rack where the broken birth-slates lay and the empty place where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out, with a look and a wide step and no number, dry slip dust fine as meal in the creases of a fist and the sweetish lead edge off High Kiln coming and going on a wrong wind, entered in nothing (T009/T013/T092, T098 carried); under the roof the underside of the loaf shelf became a roof over the basket and Sella stood with her back to it, moving her lips along her row with two unnamed gaps, giving nobody a figure, while a rack woman put two bare boards and a child's appetite under one appetite in one breath and the boy kept the shelf's two gaps, the fold's crust and its empty place, the pan's gone ring on its own tray and the field's empty place in four rooms and on four trays that did not touch, no third gap coming, no fourth likeness made a cause and no finger of his drawing the line (T060/T086, T097); the charcoal waited under the shelf's front edge unread and uncounted, her finger not going beneath the board, so the Day 166 held beat stood where it had been left, and the boy did not read, count or copy the marks, which waited for a hand that was not his and a day that had not come (T100 carried, not paid); at the going of the light she carried him home up the clay road, woodsmoke before her and river wind behind, going wide around the pan's gone place, the bare dark trough, the rack space and the missing hand's place without explaining the turns, and under the roof set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread frozen, let her hand rest a half-beat longer, told the fire it was the thaw, said The house will keep it, counted under her breath toward no opening, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand and left the widow's mark untouched, because a dusk is not an opening and the two rooms do not touch; the cold arriving cheek, temple and wrist stopped at the wrist with the afterdrop waiting, and one hundred and seventy-five stood exact, complete and unspent while the fire missed its fifth with its payer unnamed for a hundred and seventy-five days.
Day 176 | Tallow 27, Y.H. 813 (Seventhday) | the Clay Yard and the kiln mouth, the drying racks, the yard well, the kindling and flagstones under the Vail roof, the loaf shelf and the bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The last tend stood at its heat with the yard smelling of a thing held between two hands that would not stay held, the danger of the month in dust ground small as meal on the sill and white in the whites of knuckles, the sweetish edge of lead glaze riding a wrong wind off High Kiln and the kiln's breath in fours with its gap, an order and no name that could not be carried into a chart, a rack woman's pot giving its thin early sound filed where a danger goes and not among the three near-full answers or the six hearings, no pot lost and no room changed (T061/T082); Tarn took the broom only after the house had given the broom its instruction, six counted in a thumb-mark worn nearly flat where the house could check and the seventh at the lip where it could not, the small knife at the very edge with Garl's unforged collector sentence beside it, the warm stone Sella gave him in the winter Garl died lying two years gone and cold in a clay pot in the loft, kept and not reconciled, which is not forgiving (T006 held, T036/T076), Ash worked into his knuckle creases in the colour of the clean dark line, the pale clip-ring holding what light there was and giving it back, his chest tight since the dark and the Seed 16 breath still unspent with its small interval at the top of each reach; he said Yes, house to an early thaw and a late while going on wanting the late because a late thaw puts off the paying, his stroke going over the collector's road without once turning along it and no collector coming, and the sentence about an ear under this roof being worth more than the debt that held it came up into his teeth and went no further, the breath he should have spent drawn and not spent, the loan uncalled and no tenth stopping stood up before the seventh year was earned, because an account stood up before its hour is an account with a payer already written in and no payer stood here; the boy kept the fraction at the damper, the beat beneath the loaf shelf and the flagstone half-check on three places and added nothing to them, for a hand held back from the iron is still a hand that knows where the iron stands and a hand that waits a second and third time is holding, not making the change twice over (T062/T100/T036/T076); a woman's wet-pot question went to the pot with The house has said it and away level with nothing priced, and the yard went around its two unswept lines and the missing hand's place with a look and no number (T077, T009/T013/T092, T098 carried); under the roof Sella told the carried fire it was the thaw, turned the coals with a broken pot, said The house will keep it, counted under her breath toward no opening, pushed coal-black hair back with the burn-wrist, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand and did not touch the widow's mark, and the surgeon's register read the held breath as a clamp left too long on a vessel that would not bear the taking off and the near-saying stopped at the word as a chart closed over its finding with the neat margin showing, exact and worth nothing at two paces; one hundred and seventy-six stood exact, complete and unspent while the fire missed its fifth with its payer unnamed for a hundred and seventy-six days and the charcoal waited under the shelf for a hand that was not his and a day that had not come.
Day 177 | Tallow 28, Y.H. 813 (Eighthday) | the clay road at the going of the light, the Vail kiln-house bed, hearth and loaf shelf's low angle, under the roof in the small hours, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | Sella Vail carried the boy home up the clay road at the going of the light with woodsmoke going before her in long low bars and river wind coming up behind with the taste of water over water, the two passing on the stair of the evening without taking each other's colour or spending a word on either, the carrying arm keeping the weight in muscle and giving no account of the cost, and went wide around the pan's gone place, the bare dark trough, the space beneath the lowest rack where the broken birth-slates lay and the empty place in the returning line where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out, giving none of the turns to the basket and saying nothing of them (T009/T013/T092, T098 carried); under the roof she set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, base first and the weight settled the way a woman sets a vessel that must not rock, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread frozen, let her hand rest a half-beat longer than the setting needed --- the half-beat learned in the winter she sat up three nights with a child of her own who cooled while everybody was kind and never told anybody --- told the fire it was the thaw, turned the coals with the back of a broken pot, said The house will keep it, counted under her breath toward no opening, pushed coal-black hair back with the burn-wrist so the grey streak showed pale, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand and left the widow's mark untouched, for a dusk is not an opening and the two rooms do not touch (T058/T079); in the small hours he turned out the file as a man lays instruments on a towel before a case begins, each thing into its own hollow and nothing resting across anything else, six hearings of the full note beside and never among --- five from four sources and the sixth, the small flat knock out of Dessa Silt as the men lifted, where a thing from a person must go --- with no seventh and none invented to fill the empty place, three near-full answers advanced and not paid with the wheel-keeper's withheld knowing among them and not among the hearings, the older intervals at Stone 13, 17 and 25, nine stoppings as objects and not a rule with no payer named to any and no tenth before the seventh year was earned, the damper as one iron with two bites joined to no kiln and no Grathic word for both mercies (T061/T082, T036/T076, T062); the Day 161 fraction, the Day 166 held beat beneath the shelf and the Day 171 flagstone half-check stood on three places and not as a pair or a doctrine, the two shut-room tellings stood with no third, the told bell beside the heard bell, the sixth green a colour joined to no ear and the foundling a shape with no sale, and no third gap came and no fourth likeness made a cause; he wanted the crust, the breast, the pot handle and a number spoken by somebody who would have known what a number counted and got none of them, the surgeon's register reading the whole as an inventory that is not a key and a list that is not a hand, exact, complete and worth nothing at two paces, with one hundred and seventy-seven standing unspent in the dark of the wool, the payer under the floor without a name for a hundred and seventy-seven days and the charcoal waiting under the shelf for a hand that was not his and a day that had not come.
Day 178 | Tallow 29, Y.H. 813 (Ninthday) | the kiln mouth, the drying racks, the loaf shelf, the wheel-shed edge, the cold damper, the flagstones, the kindling and the bed under the Vail kiln-house roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The last working day of Tallow came in with hot dry clay over the Yard and the sour breath of the unswept corner under the sweet dry smell, the kilns brought to their last heat of the month and nobody speaking a date over the basket, the danger keeping an order and no name in meal-fine dust, a pot's early note at the kiln mouth and the sweetish edge of lead glaze riding the wrong wind off High Kiln, a potter telling a cup it would do and the cup standing where it was set and not lost, no lesson made and no room changed (T077, T061/T082); the yard went around the clean dark line, the finer grey called the thaw, the broken birth-slates beneath the lowest rack and the empty place in the returning line with a look and a wide step and no number, a rack woman reaching past the bare board with her hand before her eyes found the basket, and Sella moving her lips along her loaf row with its two unnamed gaps and giving nobody a figure, her eye going once to the cold glazed jar and her hand shutting half over nothing, her finger not going beneath the board and the charcoal waiting under the front edge for a hand that was not hers to read and a day that had not come (T060/T086, T097, T100); before first grey three or four men had gone low into the back room and she carried past at the half-pace, gave them no name and did not say the room was cold, the two shut-room tellings standing with no third, while Jorra came through with slate dust ground small as meal past the bent finger, kept dusty and clean forearms on one tray, spoke to the wheel, told a pot You'll do, withheld its full note from a High Kiln kiln-master and put that bent finger into the cold damper ahead of Sella's unfinished sentence, holding it until feeding reached the pot and leaving it standing, nobody speaking of the waiting and no payer named to the fraction (T035/T031, T062, T065/T095); Tarn took the broom only after it was instructed, went over the bright patch on the flagstones without the half-check and over the collector's road without turning along it, no collector coming, six counted where the house could check and the seventh at the lip where it could not, the ear-sentence at his teeth unsaid, the Seed 16 breath unspent and the loan uncalled (T036/T076); the surgeon's register read the held breath as a clamp left too long and the near-saying stopped at the word as a chart closed over its finding with the neat margin showing, and the three held changes stayed on their three places, not repeated and not made to stand up as a rule; at the going of the light Sella carried him home, set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread frozen, let her hand rest a half-beat longer, told the fire it was the thaw, said The house will keep it, counted under her breath toward no opening, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand and left the widow's mark untouched, because a shelf and a dusk are not openings, and one hundred and seventy-eight stood exact, complete and unspent while the fire missed its fifth with its payer unnamed for a hundred and seventy-eight days and the kiln breathed in fours behind the wall.
Day 179 | Tallow 30, Y.H. 813 (Tenthday, the third Account-day and last market of Tallow) | the Cross, the reading block under the moot-post and the moot-post's finger's width of shade, the pot board and stalls, the north bridge and the out-of-line landing, the clay road, the Vail kiln-house bed, hearth and loaf shelf under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The third Account-day and the last market of Tallow came up over the Cross with river wind under the eaves and hot clay coming down on the same breath from High Kiln, the boy one hundred and seventy-nine days old and five months and twenty-nine days and the only thing in the square that kept the number (T071/T072); **the chapter's one meaningful change:** Sella Vail set the basket in the moot-post's finger's width of shade where no slate lay, base first and the weight settled, and stepped back half a pace and stood through the forenoon and into the afternoon, giving the basket no instruction, no reason and no word about why the strip under the post was other than the place a pace to the left where the crowd leaned over the reading block, and at the going of the light she came back for it, the shade gone a finger up the post, and lifted it base first and level from the side the iron had not cooled (T058/T079); Berr Clay came up short off the clay road with the red wool cord of his office folded square under his coat and the green of tasted lead glaze at one corner of his mouth, wiped his thumb twice on his apron before touching nothing, held a narrow slate unopened to his forearm, passed a finger over the figure and read One in twelve, as the notice says low into the crowd, saying The levy is called. It is not called by me and A number goes into a slate, not a bundle. If your house wants it again, there'll be a slate at the watch --- your house, never I --- and went off toward High Kiln with the box locked and the true subtraction inside, believing he had done a kindness, so that the district's arithmetic changed for the month and settled nothing, the printed figure standing unchallenged, the figure read aloud still not the figure paid, and the figure going into the slate not knowing the hand that went in on Seed 15 and did not come out (T015/T041/T047, T077, T098 carried); the bell went four, four and a gap longer than a church bell with nobody crossing himself, the heard bell kept beside the told, and the river's fifth low note under the bridge kept apart from the fire's missing fifth at home (T078); Hessa Rale came through before noon toward Greybank with Berr's narrow slate edge-on to her forearm and the writing turned to her sleeve, her hand to it before her eyes went to the crowd, telling the woman who asked I don't read it. I carry it. There's a difference, and the road doesn't care which and Cross to Greybank. Reading goes up before noon. I've got a road to walk, no figure coming off the slate and her record-first reaching filed as the eighth such hand in the district, advanced and not paid and joined to none (T080); below the bridge the soft-cuffed clerk stayed set at the out-of-line landing for a measure nobody called, cuff grey from washing and grey again by midday, slate turned inward and hand to it before eyes to stalls, nobody stepping around him or taking him off (T090); across the square a silent woman with clay under her nails, a pale line across the back of one hand and the sweetish green of lead glaze at her mouth turned a cup a quarter and set it back where it stood and went off with the light, the sixth green kept a colour joined to no ear and never Fenna in narration, and at the market's edge the foundling was not seen, a shape with no sale and his sixth glimpse unspent, no loaf passing and no slate opened within reach of the basket (T050/T051, T033/T034 held); at the going of the light she carried him home up the clay road, going wide around the pan's gone place, the bare dark trough, the rack space and the missing hand's place without explaining the turns, set him on his bed and not in the Debt Loft, put the fold right without looking at his face, left the thread frozen, let her hand rest a half-beat longer, told the fire it was the thaw, said The house will keep it, counted under her breath toward no opening, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand and left the widow's mark untouched; in the small hours the file was exact and worthless --- six hearings beside and never among with no seventh, three near-full answers advanced and not paid, nine stoppings as objects with no tenth before the seventh year was earned, three held changes on three places, the two shut-room tellings with no third and the told bell beside the heard --- with no third gap, no fourth likeness made a cause and no finger of his drawing the line, and one hundred and seventy-nine stood exact, complete and unspent in the dark of the wool, the payer under the floor without a name for a hundred and seventy-nine days, the fire missing its fifth, the charcoal waiting under the shelf for a hand that was not his and a day that had not come, and the firing month going out without a boundary drawn, forward into Ember.
Day 180 | Ember 1, Y.H. 813 (Firstday) | the Clay Yard and the kiln mouth, the drying racks and the wheel-shed edge, the flagstones, the Vail kiln-house hearth, loaf shelf and bed under the roof, Low Kiln, Sill, Grath | The kiln month opened without a boundary drawn while the district kept calling Tallow the firing month and Ember the kiln month and neither was corrected, the boy one hundred and eighty days old and six months exactly with nobody under any roof saying so, hot clay coming into the room before the grey and wet wool under it and the sour breath of the unswept corner under both, while Sella Vail gave the instruction to the wool, the fire, the pot, the broom and the running water and never to the basket, said *the house* where another woman would say *I*, kept her half-pace, glanced once at the clay jar cold under its glaze without touching it, set the pot handle two inches beyond the reaching hand, and at the kiln mouth put her hand to the widow's mark **before** the kiln, held it one breath, let go, lifted the clay lid and tipped the carried coal under the back of a broken pot so the fire took and answered four low pulls with the silence where the fifth should have been, its payer unnamed for a hundred and eighty days, nobody praising the opening and nobody seeing what it mended (T058/T079 advanced, not paid; T088, T071/T072); the yard went around the clean dark line where Ash had turned and the finer grey called the thaw beside it, the broken birth-slates under the lowest rack, the pan's gone ring and the empty place in the returning line where a hand went in on Seed 15 and did not come out, with a look and a wide step and no number (T009/T013/T092, T098 carried), while Tarn took the broom only after it was instructed, told the kindling *Six counted. The next I don't count* with six in the worn thumb-mark where the house could check and the seventh at the lip where it could not beside Garl Vail's unforged collector sentence, brought the ear-sentence about an ear under that roof being worth more than the debt all the way to his teeth and no further, drew the breath he should have spent and did not spend it, stood up no tenth stopping, called no loan, said *Yes, house* to an early thaw and a late while wanting the late because it puts off the paying, and swept the collector's road without once turning along it and no collector came (T036/T076), his stroke going over the bright patch without the half-check so the Day 171 movement was not repeated and the three held changes stayed on three places (T062); through the finger's-width door the wheel went and the river kept its note of water over water as a fifth low thing never counted among the hearings, overhead the boards kept four nails, four and a thumb-width knot, and under the floor the carried fire missed its fifth --- three counts in three places that did not become one --- while the boy, who wanted the crust two inches from his ear, the breast, the pot handle beyond his hand and a number spoken by somebody who would have known what a number counted and got none of them, laid out the file in the small hours with six hearings beside and never among, no seventh invented, three near-full answers advanced and unpaid, nine stoppings as objects and not a rule, the two shut-room tellings at two and the charcoal waiting under the shelf for a hand that was not his and a day that had not come.
